   6 Select [F7] (OK).

       Return operation is executed up to the designated sequence.

In "return operation", all programmed commands are executed as in normal operation with
exceptions that function generation and external outputs for S, T, and M commands are not
executed. Calling a subprogram, return from a subprogram, coordinate system shift, etc. are all
executed.
The term "block count" indicates the count data of the sequences having been executed since the
program was started after the resetting of the NC. Control statements such as GOTO and CALL are
not counted. Since the block count data and the sequence name are not cleared by resetting the or
turning ON/OFF the power, they can be used for restoring the operation after resetting the NC, etc.
They are cleared at the first starting up of the operation.

[Supplement]

 An attempt to execute mid-block sequence restart operation while a schedule program or a main
 program is executed causes an error.
                                                                                                                                 6124-E P-58
        SECTION 4 UPGRADED SEQUENCE RESTART FUNCTION (MID-BLOCK RESTART FUNCTION)

4-2. Sequence Restart Operation

           Sequence restart from the designated sequence

            Procedure :

           1 Execute return operation to the designated sequence by refer to "4-1. Sequence Restart

                   Command".

           2 Press the [SEQUENCE RESTART] key on the machine operation panel.

                   The axes move to the end point of the designated block at a jog feedrate.

           3 Press the [CYCLE START] button and the programmed operation restarts.

           Sequence restart from the tool located position

            Procedure :

           1 Move any of X-, Y- and Z-axis to the desired restart position.

           2 Execute return operation to the designated sequence by refer to "4-1. Sequence Restart

                   Command".
                   The designated sequence should be as close to the block of the position to be returned as
                   possible.
                   Set "MC POSIT" for MID-BLOCK RESTART and the name of the axis you have moved to the
                   desired restart position for RETURN AXIS.

           3 Press the [CYCLE START] button.

                   Internal processing is executed from the command position in the one block ahead of the
                   designated sequence. When the current position of the specified axis passes the stop position,
                   the SLIDE HOLD lamp is turned on to end the internal processing.
                   The position defined by the actual tool position data is taken as the restart point.

           4 Press the [SEQUENCE RESTART] key on the machine operation panel.

                   The axes move to the sequence restart point at a jog feedrate.
                   On completion of positioning the NC enters the slide hold state.

           5 Press the [CYCLE START] button and the programmed operation restarts.

                   [Supplement]

                     The restart point is slightly shifted in the tool advancing direction from the desired restart
                     point.
                     If the position where the machine is stopped at the moment is in the reverse of the travel
                     direction of the internal processing, the NC judges that the internal processing passes the
                     machine stop position, ending the internal processing.

           c. Press the [CYCLE START] button. Internal processing is executed for the end point (X90.
                 Y100. Z-30.) in the N03 sequence. Internal processing ends at a point slightly shifted from
                 the end point (X130. Y310. Z-30.) in the tool advancing direction with the SLIDE HOLD
                 indicating lamp lit.
                 The position defined by the actual tool position data is taken as the restart point.

           d. Press the [SEQUENCE RESTART] key and the Z-axis moves to the restart point at a jog
                 feedrate. The RUN lamp starts blinking after the completion of positioning.

                 at a jog feedrate and, on completion of positioning, the SLIDE HOLD indicating lamp
                 lights.

   Sequence restart from a desired position in a program

    Procedure :

    1 Execute return operation to the designated sequence by refer to "4-1. Sequence Restart

           Command".
           Select "SLIDE HOLD" for MID-BLOCK RESTART item.

    2 Press the [CYCLE START] button.

           Internal processing is executed from the end point of the sequence one block ahead the
           designated sequence.

    3 After the internal processing has advanced to the desired restart point, press the [SLIDE

           HOLD] button.
           The position defined by the actual tool position data is taken as the restart point.

    4 To change the restart point, press the [CYCLE START] button. Since internal processing

           restarts when the [CYCLE START] button is pressed, you can change the restart point as
           necessary. Press the [SLID HOLD] button after the internal processing has progressed to the
           desired restart point.

    5 If necessary, you can change the restart point as desired by repeating the cycle start and slide

           hold operation.

    6 Press the [SEQUENCE RESTART] key and the axes return to the restart point at a jog

           feedrate. When the axes reach the restart point, the NC enters the slide hold state.

    7 Press the [CYCLE START] button and the programmed operation restarts.
                                                                                              6124-E P-61
                                                             SECTION 5 WARM-UP FUNCTION

SECTION 5  WARM-UP FUNCTION

The warm-up function is provided to execute warm-up automatically. It automatically turns on the power
according to the calendar timer setting and executes the specially prepared warm-up program.

[Supplement]

 For details of a calendar timer, read the instruction manual of the calendar timer supplied with the product.
 Note that setting and handling of the calendar timer will vary depending on the manufacturer and the type.
 Before reading the instruction manual, make sure that the manual supplied with the machine is the one for
 the calendar timer used in the machine.

5-1. Storing Warm-up Program

To use the warm-up function, the warm-up program must be stored in the user are of memory
(MD1:) by the file name of "W.WAF". For storing the warm-up program use the normal program
storing procedure.

5-1-1. Program Format

The program format of the warm-up program is identical to the main program format; it begins with a
program name preceded by address character O and ends with M02 or M30.
<Example>

                                           O9999

                            S200 M3

                            VC128=50

                            G0 Z500

                       NA1      X-200 Y-200

                                X200  Y200

                            G4 F5

                            VC128=VC128-1

                            IF [VC128 GT 0] NA1

                            M5

                            M2

                                      ME32128R0300700030001
                                                            6124-E P-62
                           SECTION 5 WARM-UP FUNCTION

5-2. Operation

5-2-1. Outline of Operation

            Set the cycle on and off time on the calendar timer according to the operation schedule.
            (After turning on the cycle, allow at least three minutes before turning it off. For cycle off, refer to
            items (2) and (3) in Supplements 5-2-2.)
            The power is automatically turned on to the NC according to the calendar timer setting.
            Approximately 90 seconds after the power on, the end buzzer sounds for the parameter set time.
            When the buzzer stops sounding, the NC enters the automatic operation mode, and selects and
            starts the warm-up program (file name: W.WAF, program name: as desired).
            After the completion of the warm-up program (execution of M02 or M30, or pressing the reset button
            on the operation panel), the NC automatically selects the program having been selected before
            shutting the power off. (Whether or not the previously selected program is selected after the
            completion of the warm-up program is determined by parameter setting.)

5-2-2. Timing Chart

Calendar timer output

Control power

Operation end buzzer
Automatic selection
of warm-up program

Automatic selection
of warm-up program

Reset by M02 / M30,
or by pressing the

before power shutting off

                           Timing Chart for Automatic Power ON by Calendar Timer

                                                                                                                                                       ME32128R0300700050001
                                                     6124-E P-63
                    SECTION 5 WARM-UP FUNCTION

5-2-3. Supplements

(1) After the completion of the warm-up program, the program selected last by the operation at the
      operation panel is automatically selected. Therefore, if the warm-up program (file name:
      W.WAF) was selected at the operation panel before shutting off the power, W.WAF is selected
      again after the completion of the warm-up program.

(2) Even if the calendar timer output is turned off during the execution of the warm-up program, the
      warm-up program is continuously executed.

(3) An alarm occurs if the W.WAF file does not exist in the memory when the power is automatically
      turned on according to the setting on the calendar timer.

(4) After the power is turned on, keys on the operation panel (mode selection keys, function keys
      and alphanumeric keys), cycle start button and external cycle start button are all disabled until
      the warm-up program starts automatically. These keys and buttons are operative, however, if
      the warm-up program file selection is impossible.

(5) If the [RESET] button is pressed during warming up, the program having been selected before
      shutting off of the power is automatically selected. Note that this automatic selection is not
      possible if "NOT SELECT" is selected for THE PROGRAM SELECTED BEFORE THE POWER
      OFF of NC optional parameter (AUTO SET AT NC RESET/POWER ON).

(6) If the calendar timer goes on after the position data is displayed on the screen with the power
      turned on by an operator, the warm-up function does not operate.

(7) Completion of the warm-up program does not cause power shutoff if the automatic power
      shutoff function is selected.

(8) While the warm-up program is running after the power was turned ON, "WARM-UP PROGRAM
      RUNNING" is displayed.

[Supplement]

 If the operator turns off the power once while the calendar timer output is on and then turns it on,
 the warm-up program is executed again.

5-3. Parameter

            In relation to the warm-up function, setting is required for the following parameter.
            MC USER PARAMETER OPERATION END BUZZER TIMER
            Set the buzzer sounding time using [F1] (SET) or [F2] (ADD).
SECTION 6                                                                                                 6124-E P-64
                                             SECTION 6 SIMPLIFIED LOAD MONITOR FUNCTION

           SIMPLIFIED LOAD MONITOR FUNCTION

The simplified load monitor function monitors the spindle load ratio and outputs the alarm signal if the
detected load ratio exceeds the parameter set value.
A total of 80 sets of parameters are used for this function and system variable VSLNO selects the desired
parameter set. M142/M143 turns the simplified load monitor function off/on.

6-1. Setting for the Simplified Load Monitor Function

            (1) Turning ON/OFF the Function
                   M142...Turns the simplified load monitor function OFF
                   M143...Turns the simplified load monitor function ON

            (2) Selecting the Spindle Load Monitor Parameter Number
                   For monitoring the spindle load, "maximum load (LOAD MAX)", "continuous load time (LOAD
                   TIMER)" and "monitor mode (MONIT. MODE)" are set for parameters and 80 sets of parameters
                   are provided. To execute monitoring, one set of parameters must be selected in advance.
                   VSLNO... Monitor parameter number selection system variable
                   Since VSLNO is backed up, previous setting for this variable is used if VSLNO is not set for the
                   monitoring. If no value has ever been set for this variable, "1" is assumed.

            (3) Ignoring Spindle Motor Start-up Current
                   At the start up of the spindle motor, the start-up current flows the motor drive circuit causing the
                   detected spindle load to exceed the set maximum load. To prevent the occurrence of an alarm
                   in this situation, the NC ignores the start-up current of the spindle motor by the two steps of
                   processing indicated below.

                   a. The function ignores the spindle overload state until the actual spindle speed falls within
                         the range 75% to 125% of the specified spindle speed (S command).
                                                                                                                 6124-E P-65
                                                     SECTION 6 SIMPLIFIED LOAD MONITOR FUNCTION

b. After the spindle speed reaches the range 75% to 125% of the specified spindle speed (S
      command), the function stays immune to the spindle load for the parameter-set length of
      time to allow the spindle to rotate in the stable state.

Spindle load meter pointer  Start-up current         T1; The function is immune to
                                                         the overload state since the
                            T1                           spindle rotation is unstable.
                                                 T2
                                                     T2; Spindle overload state is
                                                         not checked within this
                                                         parameter-set period.

                                                     Set value

                                                                           Stable spindle state

                                                                                        Time

                                                     Spindle is judged to be operating
                                                     at a constant speed.
                                                                           S command

Spindle speed                                        75%        100%

                                                                                                            Time

                                                                                                                                                                                    ME32128R0300800020001

                                       Fig.6-1 Ignoring Start-up Current of Spindle Motor

(4) Overload Monitoring Method
      When the spindle reaches the constant speed and rotates in the stable state, with the monitor
      parameter set by VSLNO and the monitor function turned on by M143, spindle overload monitor
      begins.
      If the actual spindle load exceeds the parameter set maximum load for the length of parameter
      set time, the spindle overload alarm occurs.
                                                                                 6124-E P-66
                     SECTION 6 SIMPLIFIED LOAD MONITOR FUNCTION

6-2. Screen Display

From the function menu of the initial display screen in any operation mode, select [F3] (LOAD ON/
OFF) to display the LOAD window, monitor the spindle load in the LOAD window.
The LOAD MONITOR window can also be displayed on the "MAIN PROGRAM" and "SCHEDULE
PROGRAM" screens in each operation mode.

                                          ME32128R0300800030001

                     Fig.6-2 LOAD Window

a. The LOAD MONITOR meter displays the load in three colors: blue (load up to the continuous
      rating of the spindle motor); yellow (over the continuous rating of the spindle motor and up to
      the short-time rating); red (over the short-time rating of the spindle motor). The peak state will
      be maintained. The LOAD MONITOR meter is reset each time the LOAD MONITOR window
      appears.

b. "LOAD MONITOR" appears after the immune period to ignore the start-up current is terminated
      during monitoring (M143), indicating overload is being monitored. If the spindle load exceeds
      the maximum load, the background color of the OVERLOAD MONITOR frame changes from
      green to yellow.

c. The value displayed on the right side of "LOAD MONITOR" is the spindle overload monitor
      parameter number currently selected.

d. The value displayed on the right side of the parameter number described in c) above is the
      maximum load of the spindle overload monitor parameter currently selected.

                                    SECTION 7 EXTERNAL PROGRAM SELECTION FUNCTION

           EXTERNAL PROGRAM SELECTION
           FUNCTION

7-1. External Program Selection A (Pushbutton Type)

            The external program selection function / external program selection A (pushbutton type) selects a
            main or a schedule program using a pushbutton switch. When a pushbutton is pressed, the
            corresponding program is selected.

7-1-1. Program Type (Main or Schedule Program) Selection

            Which type of the program (main program or schedule program) should be selected may be
            determined by the setting for the parameter.

           NC optional parameter (bit) No. 17, bit 5

            Check box (checked):Schedule program
            Check box (not checked):Main program

7-1-2. Disabling the Selection of the Same Program

            It is possible to disable the loading of the selected program if the program already existing in the
            memory is selected.
            This can save the program loading time.

           NC optional parameter (bit) No. 17, bit 6

            Check box (checked):Disables the loading of the same program.
            Check box (not checked):Enables the loading of the same program.

7-1-3. Program Selection

            Press the pushbutton switch corresponding to the desired program to select it.
            Selection of a program is possible in the range from P1.MIN (or SDF) to P16.MIN (or SDF).
            (Note that the number of selectable programs differs depending on the specification.)
            On completion of program selection, the lamp lights corresponding to the selected program. The
            lamp, if lit, indicates that the corresponding program is in the selected state.

7-1-4. Supplements

               Program selection buttons are inoperative when a program is being selected.

               Program selection is impossible if the NC is in an alarm state.

               An alarm occurs if the selected program is not found.

               An alarm occurs if program selection is attempted during machining.
7-2.                                                                                                                      6124-E P-70
                                                    SECTION 7 EXTERNAL PROGRAM SELECTION FUNCTION

      External Program Selection B (Rotary Switch Type)

      The external program selection function / external program selection B (rotary switch type) selects a
      main or a schedule program using a rotary switch. Select the desired program by turning the rotary
      switch.

7-2-1. Program Type (Main or Schedule Program) Selection

      Which type of the program (main program or schedule program) should be selected may be
      determined by the setting for the parameter.

      NC optional parameter (bit) No. 17, bit 5

      Check box (checked):Schedule program
      Check box (not checked):Main program

7-2-2. Disabling the Selection of the Same Program

      It is possible to disable the loading of the selected program if the program already existing in the
      memory is selected.
      This can save the program loading time.

      NC optional parameter (bit) No. 17, bit 6

      Check box (checked):Disables the loading of the same program.
      Check box (not checked):Enables the loading of the same program.

7-2-3. Program Selection

      Turn the rotary switch to select the desired program.
      Selection of a program is possible in the range from P1.MIN (or SDF) to P16.MIN (or SDF).
      (Note that the number of selectable programs differs depending on the specification.)
      On completion of program selection, the program selection lamp lights. The lamp lights if the
      selected program agrees with the one set by the rotary switch.

7-2-4. Supplements

               Program selection rotary switch is inoperative when a program is being selected.
               Program selection is impossible if the NC is in an alarm state.
               An alarm occurs if the selected program is not found.
               An alarm occurs if program selection is attempted during machining.

7-3-7. Parity Check

For external program selection type C (BCD type), the parity check function is available.
The parity check function improves the reliability of the signals.

Parameters

MC USER PARAMETER (EXTERNAL PROGRAM SELECT)

                                                                                                                                                                                           ME32128R0300900190001

               Fig.7-4 MC USER PARAMETER (EXTERNAL PROGRAM SELECT) Screen

CHECK PARITY (EVEN) IS MADE EFFECTIVE

(Check mark):Executes parity check in even parity scheme.
(No check mark):Does not execute parity check in even parity scheme.

CHECK PARITY (ODD) IS MADE EFFECTIVE

(Check mark):Executes parity check in odd parity scheme.
(No check mark):Does not execute parity check in odd parity scheme.
If no check mark is set for both parameters, parity check is not executed.
If a check mark is set for both parameters, parity check is executed in even parity scheme.
SECTION 8                                                                                                 6124-E P-77
                                          SECTION 8 EXTERNAL M SIGNAL OUTPUT FUNCTION

           EXTERNAL M SIGNAL OUTPUT FUNCTION

This function outputs contact point signals to an external device corresponding to the M code entered in a
program. The external device executes processing in response to the output signal and, after the completion
of the processing, it returns the answer signal to the NC. It regards that the command processing has
completed when it receives the answer signal and proceeds the program to the next step.
There are two different numbers of output points - four or eight.
In either case, only one point is provided for an answer signal.

8-1. External M Codes

M181 to M188 are used as external M codes.
4-point specification ... M181 to M184
8-point specification ... M181 to M188

[Supplement]

 In a program, one block can contain only one external M code.
 If one block contains two or more commands, the rightmost external M code will be executed.

 <Example> M181 M182 ------ M182 will be executed.

8-2. Timing Chart

           Previous block                        To the next block

           M181 - M188
           output signal

Input of answer signal

                                                 ME32128R0301000030001

                           Fig.8-1 Timing Chart

[Supplement]

 1) The M181 - M188 output signals are turned on when an external M code command is
      executed and turned off when the answer signal is returned.

 2) The answer signal may be turned off only after checking that the M181 - M188 output signal
      has been turned off.

 3) The NC is in the S.T.M. function execution status while an external M signal is output and
      program execution is suspended during this period.
SECTION 9                                                                                                    6124-E P-78
                                                     SECTION 9 CYCLE TIME REDUCTION FUNCTION

              CYCLE TIME REDUCTION FUNCTION

The cycle time reduction function reduces non-cutting time by enabling simultaneous operations such that
axis feed is executed at the same time the spindle starts to shorten total machining cycle time.

9-1. Ignoring Spindle Rotation Answer M300 (1 Block)

 With M03/M04, the program advances to the next block without waiting for the answer signal of

   M03/M04.

 With M05, the spindle stop processing starts simultaneously with the execution of axis feed

   commands and the program advances to the next block without waiting for the answer signal of
   M05.

 With M19, the spindle orientation processing starts simultaneously with the execution of axis

   feed commands and the program advances to the next block without waiting for the answer
   signal of M19.

Example Program

N100 G00 X100 M3 M300

N101          Z10 (M3)

[Supplement]

1) Specify M300 with a spindle rotation command in the same block.

2) To confirm the answer signal in the next block, specify the spindle start command in that block
    again.

3) An alarm occurs if M300 is specified in the fixed cycle mode.

9-2. Ignoring / Confirming Answer Signal for Other than
        Spindle Rotation M301, M302 (1 Block)

               When M301 is specified, answer signal for M codes other than spindle operation is ignored to

                   advance the program to the next block.

               M302 is used to confirm the M code answer signals having been ignored by the execution of

                   M301.
                   These M codes are valid for the following M codes.

                     M32 (door close)
                     M33 (door open)
                     M181 - M188 (external M signal)

           Example Program

                     N200 M33 M301 (Ignores M33 answer signal)
                     N201 M181 M301 (Ignores M181 answer signal)
                     N202 M302 (Confirms M33 and M181 answer signals)

              SECTION 9 CYCLE TIME REDUCTION FUNCTION

[Supplement]

Specify M301 with the M code for which the answer signal should be ignored in the same block.
                                                                                                                                 6124-E P-80
                                                                      SECTION 10 F1-DIGIT FEED COMMAND FUNCTION

SECTION 10 F1-DIGIT FEED COMMAND FUNCTION

The F1-digit feed command function allows the designation of a feedrate using a one-digit number specified
following address character "F". Actual feedrate is controlled according to the setting of a switch that
corresponds to the specified one-digit number.
This function allows the feedrate to be set using either the switches on the operation panel or the parameters.

10-1. Setting Method

The F1-digit feed command function has the following three feedrate designation methods.

 F1 to F9 (parameter method)

 F1 to F4 (feed switch method, 4-set specification)

 F1 to F8 (feed switch method, 8-set specification)

Designation of an F command in other format is regarded as a regular F command that directly
specifies a feedrate.
Using a real number or a variable such as "F1." is regarded as a regular F command.
Therefore, variables may not be used in the F1-digit feed command format.
<Example>

F1     F1-digit command Feedrate is determined by the setting of switch F1
F200   200 mm/min
F5.    5 mm/min
LA1=8
F=LA   8 mm/min

10-2. Parameter Method

10-2-1.Screen Display

            When the parameter method is used for setting the F1-digit feed command, feedrates are set using
            the F1 PARAMETER SET pop-up window.
            This pop-up window is called by selecting [F1] (PARAM. F1 DIGIT) from the function menu of the
            automatic or MDI operation mode screen.
            If the function item [F1] (PARAM. F1 DIGIT) is not displayed, press the " " extension key.
                                                                                                                                 6124-E P-81
                                                                      SECTION 10 F1-DIGIT FEED COMMAND FUNCTION

                                                                                                                                                                                                                     ME32128R0301200030001

                                               Fig.10-1 F1 PARAMETER SET Pop-up Window

10-2-2.Setting Unit System

            The setting unit conforms to the setting for the NC OPTIONAL PARAMETER (INPUT UNIT
            SYSTEM) as in other data setting modes (zero setting, tool data setting, and parameter setting
            modes).

                                                                                                                                                                                                                     ME32128R0301200040001

                               Fig.10-2 NC OPTIONAL PARAMETER (INPUT UNIT SYSTEM) Screen
                                                                                     6124-E P-82
                          SECTION 10 F1-DIGIT FEED COMMAND FUNCTION

10-2-3.Function Commands

For the function keys used for setting the data, [SET], [ADD], [ACCEL] and [DECEL] are available.

10-2-4.Updating the Data

In addition to the normal data setting method that uses SET and ADD functions, it is possible to
increase or decrease the data by continuously adding or subtracting the preset data (value set for
optional parameter (long word) No. 22) using the ACCEL or DECEL function. When updating the
data using these ACCEL or DECEL function, a timer is provided to avoid excessively sudden
changes of the data. The value set for optional parameter (long word) No. 22 is always treated as
the value in the least unit system (1 m in the mm system and 1/1000 inches in the inch system).

10-2-5.Maximum and Minimum Setting Data

           FEED MAX

            Minimum value:0 (both for mm and inch systems)
            Maximum value:Maximum programmable F value
            (65535.000 mm/min or 2580.1181 in/min)

           FEED

            Minimum value:0 (both for mm and inch systems)
            Maximum value:The value set at the above FEED MAX.

           ACCEL

            If the value exceeds the maximum value as the result of [F4] (ACCEL) function, it is processes as
            indicated below.

               FEED

                   The calculated value will be replaced by the value set as the value set at FEED MAX.

               FEED MAX

                   If the calculated value exceeds the allowable setting value, the following error occurs.
                   5209 Input data overflow
                                                                                                6124-E P-83
                                     SECTION 10 F1-DIGIT FEED COMMAND FUNCTION

10-2-6.Others

(1) System Variables
      All setting data are held in system variables and can be referenced and set using a program.

  System Variable                Description         Subscript            Remarks
          Name                                       Command
                   F1-digit feed parameter            Available    Unit conversion data

                   F1-digit feed parameter

(2) Continuous Addition/Subtraction of Data
      The data set for FEED and FEED MAX may be changed using the value set for optional
      parameter (long word) No. 22. The setting range of the value set for this parameter is indicated
      below.
      Minimum value:0
      Maximum value:65535000

(3) "F" Value Display in ACTUAL POSITION Screen and Other Screens
      At the ACTUAL POSITION and BLOCK DATA screen, an F value is displayed in the manner as
      indicated below, the same display format as with F4-digit feed commands.
      With the mm system, the value at the second place right to the decimal point is rounded to
      display the actual position data. In the case of the inch system, the value at the third place is
      rounded.
      mm system: Display is given up to one digit right to a decimal point.
      inch system: Display is given up to two digits right to a decimal point.
                                                                                     6124-E P-84
                          SECTION 10 F1-DIGIT FEED COMMAND FUNCTION

10-3. Feed Switch Method

Rotary switches are provided on the F1-digit feed operation panel and used to set the desired
feedrate. If an F command (F1 to F4 or F1 to F8) is specified in a program, the corresponding rotary
switch is selected and the axis will move at the feedrate set at the selected rotary switch.
The indicating lamp of the rotary switches shows which of the rotary switches is selected.

10-4. Supplements

            (1) The F1-digit feed status (F1 to F9) is not cleared even when the NC is reset. It is cleared when
                   a direct F command is specified or the power is turned on.

            (2) The F1-digit feedrate setting may be changed while an axis is moving.
            (3) The feed override function is disregarded while an F1-digit feed command is executed.
            (4) Alarm occurs if an F1-digit feed command is specified in the G95 (feed per revolution) mode.
                                                                                                                                 6124-E P-85
                                                                      SECTION 11 ANY-ANGLE CHAMFERING FUNCTION

SECTION 11 ANY-ANGLE CHAMFERING FUNCTION

When chamfering (C-chamfer, R-chamfer) a corner, it is not easy to program chamfering by using a G code
(G01, G02, G03) if the corner has an arbitrary angle.
The any-angle chamfering function allows chamfering at any corner by simply designating the apex of the
corner and the chamfer size. The function calculates the start and end points and executes chamfering
automatically.
This function is usable for chamfering of all the following corners: a corner composed of two straight lines, a
corner composed of a straight line and an arc, and a corner composed of two arcs.

             5

                5                                                     Radius 5

5C                                                                5R

C-chamfer (chamfer size: 5 mm)       R-chamfer (chamfer size: 5 mm)

                                                                  ME32128R0301300010001

5                                    5
         5C                                   5R

5                                                              5

C-chamfer (Chamfering amount: 5 mm)  R-chamfer (Chamfering amount: 5 mm)

                                                                                  ME32128R0301300010002

Fig.11-2 Chamfering between Straight Line and Arc

55
                                                            5

C-chamfer (Chamfering amount: 5 mm)  R-chamfer (Chamfering amount: 5 mm)

             Fig.11-3 Chamfering between Arcs

             CHFC (CHFR)
             Yp__Zp__L__ (Q__J__K__ )  In the G19 plane

             CHFC (Chamfer C)          C-chamfering command (1 shot)

             IJ                        chamfering) (1 shot), Positive value Q=0 to
                                       99999999
                                       Imaginary direction of movement (vector) (1 shot)
                                       Indicates the direction; command value should be
                                       specified according to the selected unit system.

11-3. Supplements

   Chamfering is executed in the selected plane.

   The block that follows the one containing the chamfering command must contain an axis

      movement command.

   The chamfering command is valid during the execution of the AG command or in the cutter

      radius compensation mode.

   If you specify the chamfering command in the incremental programming mode, specify the

      distance of axis travel in the next block starting from the end point of chamfering.

   The Q command cannot be specified in the chamfering command block if G02/G03 mode is

      selected in the next block.

[Supplement]

    If chamfering is specified in the cutter radius compensation mode, it is not possible to specify

      the imaginary entry and cancel direction using I and J.
      Regardless of the selected mode, Q, L, I and J specified in the block containing the
      chamfering command are processed as Q, L, I and J of the chamfering command.

    If the I, J, K commands are specified in the chamfering command block when G02/G03 mode

      is selected, they are regarded as I, J, K commands for circular interpolation.

11-4. Alarm

2461 Chamfering commands illegal

[Code]

XYYZZ:
X = 1->Data word
When chamfering command at arbitrary angles is designated, the relevant sequence or the next
sequence read in advance has a wrong command format.
YY = 00
ZZ =
01->Chamfering command is designated in a mode other than G01 mode.
02->In the sequence designating the chamfering command (1st sequence), the start and end points
are the same.
03->In the sequence read in advance (2nd sequence), the start and end points are the same.
04->No L command is designated.
05->The L command value is out of the setting range (0 to 99999999).
06->The Q command value is out of the setting range (0 to 99999999).
07->The pre-fetched sequence is neither G00 nor G01 mode.
08->A chamfering command was specified during execution of a macro command. Or, a coordinate
calculation command was specified in the pre-fetched sequence.
09->The planes selected by sequence No. 1 and sequence No. 2 are not the same.
0A->Chamfering was attempted on a plane including the rotary axis in a mode other than cylindrical
side surface machining (G175).
0B->I, J, and K commands are specified at the same time.
0E->The center points between arc modes of No.1 and No.2 sequences are same.
X=2 Data word: calculation
A calculation error occurred during shape definition by an arbitrary angle command.
YY=02 Calculation for obtaining the coefficient of the straight line passing through the specified
point and the point specified by the previous sequence (1st straight line)
YY=03 Calculation for obtaining the coefficient of the straight line passing through the specified
point and the point specified by the pre-fetched sequence(2nd straight line)
ZZ:
bit0->Overflow in conversion of a value into an integer
bit1->Exponent underflow
bit2->Exponent overflow
bit3->Attempt to calculate the square root of a negative value
bit4->Attempt to divide a value by 0
bit5->Angle overflow in calculation of Sin, Cos, Tan, Cot values

YY:

02->Calculation of chamfering end point

05->Calculation for determining chamfering direction

YY=00

large.

The three dimensional (3-D) circular interpolation function executes circular interpolation in any desired plane
in addition to the X-Y, Y-Z and Z-X planes.

desired plane.

12-1. Programming Format

           G02/G03 Xp__ Yp__ Zp__ I__ J__ K__ (P__ Q__ R__)

            G02/G03
            Direction of arc rotation (Refer to 12-2. "3-D Arc Rotating Direction".)
            Xp,Yp,Zp
            Coordinate values of end point
            (Xp: X or U axis, Yp: Y or V axis, Zp: Z or W axis)
            I, J, K
            Coordinate values of arc center
            (P, Q, R)
            Coordinate values of an arbitrary point in the circular interpolation plane, viewed from the start point
            of the arc, or the vector normal to the circular interpolation plane
            (These commands are necessary if the plane cannot be defined by Xp, Yp, Zp, I, J, and K.)

               The arc being defined is regarded as the 3-D arc if all of I, J and K are specified. In other cases,

                   the arc is regarded as the 2-D arc in the X-Y, Y-Z, or Z-X plane.

               The arc is defined in the plane where the three points, start point (actual position), center of arc

                   and end point (target position), exist.

 The coordinate values of the end point may be specified in either absolute values or

   incremental values according to the programming mode selected by G90 and G91.

 The coordinate values of the center of the arc must always be specified in incremental values

   from the start point regardless of the mode selected by G90 and G91.

 If the start point, the center of the arc, and the end point lie on a straight line (a half or a full

   circle), the interpolation plane cannot be determined. In this case, it is necessary to specify a
   point in the interpolation plane or the vector normal to the interpolation plane using P, Q, and R.
   Which of the point or the vector should be specified is determined according to the setting for
   NC optional parameter (bit) No. 37, bit 5.

12-2. 3-D Arc Rotating Direction

            The rotating direction of the arc is specified by the rotating direction of the 3-D arc projected on the
            2-D plane.

           Programming Format G256 I__ (J__ , K__) (designating the plane of projection)

             I+: The arc is projected on the G19 plane and the projected arc is viewed from the X-axis
                      positive direction.

             I-: The arc is projected on the G19 plane and the projected arc is viewed from the X-axis
                      negative direction.

             J+: The arc is projected on the G18 plane and the projected arc is viewed from the Y-axis
                      positive direction.

             J-: The arc is projected on the G18 plane and the projected arc is viewed from the Y-axis
                      negative direction.

             K+: The arc is projected on the G17 plane and the projected arc is viewed from the Z-axis
                      positive direction.

             K-: The arc is projected on the G17 plane and the projected arc is viewed from the Z-axis
                      negative direction.

12-3. Shorter Arc and Longer Arc

The circular interpolation is executed along either a shorter or longer path from the start point to the
end point around the center of the arc.
Programming format

G172 (or G173) X__ Y__ Z__ I__ J__ K__ (P__ Q__ R__)

G172   : Shorter arc
G173
X,Y,Z  : Longer arc
I,J,K
P,Q,R  : Coordinate values of the end point

       : Coordinate values of the center point

       : Vector normal to the plane where arc is included
         (Optional parameter (bit) No. 37, bit 5: Not checked)

SECTION 13 AXIS NAME DESIGNATION FUNCTION

Axis names used in programming are usually determined as names specific to the individual machines, like X-
axis for table travel. The axis name designation function, however, allows the programmer to designate the
name of the individual axis as desired.
This function will facilitate programming the machining using angular attachment or in five-face machining
models such as MCM because the function allows the tool cutting-in axis (cycle axis) to be treated as the Z-
axis on all machining faces.

                                                                                          Y

                z                    Rotating axis of a tool
                               y

                                  x

Coordinate system specific                                                            X

to the machine                       The coordinate system designated by
                                     a programmer to machine face "A"
(Machine coordinate system)       Z  (Program coordinate system)

                                  Fig.13-1 Program Coordinate System

The illustration above shows that the Z-axis in the program coordinate system is defined in the Y- direction in
the machine coordinate system and the Y-axis in the program coordinate system is defined in the Z+ direction
in the machine coordinate system using the axis name designation function.

                                                                                 6124-E P-106
                        SECTION 13 AXIS NAME DESIGNATION FUNCTION

13-2. Applicable Range

(1) Mode
      In the automatic or MDI mode, the NC program is executed in the program coordinate system.
      In other operation modes, the commands in the program are processed as the data in the
      machine coordinate system.

(2) Command Axis
      The commands of the standard axes (X, Y, Z) and the axis related commands (I, J, K, P, R) are
      all processed as the data in the program coordinate system.

(3) External Axis
      The axis name of the mirror image switches (X, Y, Z) is processed as the axis name in the
      program coordinate system.

(4) G Codes
      The axis related G codes are all processed in the program coordinate system.
      G17, G18, G19 (plane selection)
      G54, G55, G56 (tool length offset)
      G02, G03 (circular interpolation)
      G41, G42 (cutter radius compensation)

(5) M Codes
      M codes are processed in the program coordinate system with exceptions of the following.
      M22, M23, M24, M25 (clamp/unclamp)
                                                                               6124-E P-107
                      SECTION 13 AXIS NAME DESIGNATION FUNCTION

                                    SECTION 13 AXIS NAME DESIGNATION FUNCTION

13-5. Supplements

(1) Block Display
      In the display of block data, the G codes and the actual position data may differ from those in
      the NC program. The reason for this is the block data display shows the operation of the
      machine axes although the program coordinate system may differ from the machine coordinate
      system due to the switching of the axes.

 G codes that could differ from those in the NC program

   G17, G18, G19 (plane selection)
   G54, G55, G56 (tool length offset)
   G02, G03 (circular interpolation)
   G41, G42 (cutter radius compensation)

 Axes for which the position data could differ from the programmed commands

   Standard axes (X, Y, Z) and the axis related commands (I, J, K)

(2) Zero Offset (G92)
      The commands specified with G92 are processed as the commands specified in the new
      coordinate system.
      Therefore, the zero offset data must be specified carefully - for which axis and in which plane
      the offset data is desired.
      <Example program>

                         Z0            Y0

G0 X0 Y0 Z0
G14 X1 Y3 Z-2
G92 Y500

                         Y1 Face A

                                    500 mm          X0
                                    (19.69 in.)

   Z1                                                         Program zero for
                                                              machining on face A

                                                          X1

Before executing G92                   After executing G92

   Zero Offset                         Zero Offset

X                  2000             X               2000

Y                  2000             Y               2000

Z                  2000             Z               1500

                                                                                                                                                                                         ME32128R0301500070001

                                            Fig.13-6 Zero Offset by G92

The values input in the zero offset mode are stored in the memory as the machine axis data. In
the case indicated above, the zero point data is changed for the Z-axis (machine axis).
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-111
                                                         SECTION 13 AXIS NAME DESIGNATION FUNCTION

(3) Upper Limit Return in Fixed Cycles
      M52 used in fixed cycles and coordinate calculation, etc. is valid for the Z-axis (machine axis) in
      the machine coordinate system. If the Z-axis in a program corresponds to the X- or Y-axis of the
      machine axis, designation of M52 causes alarm B.

(4) Feedrate
      Usually, programmed feedrates for Z-axis differ from those of other axes (X-axis, Y-axis).
      When programming feedrates, the following must be taken into consideration.

        Always keep in mind the machining face and actually moving axis when specifying a

            feedrate command. Especially, when X- or Y-axis is used as the Z-axis (machine axis),
            sufficient care must be paid so that the feedrate will not exceed the maximum feedrate of
            the Z-axis.

(5) G Codes Valid for Machine Coordinate System
      Concerning G codes such as G22 (programmable travel limit) and G30 (home position return)
      which are valid in the machine coordinate system, the commands are executed in the machine
      coordinate system and not in the program coordinate system.
      <Example>
      G22 X1000 Y1000 Z1000 I-1000 J-1000 K-1000
      
      Travel limits of the X-, Y- and Z-axis are set at "1000" and "-1000" in the positive and the
      negative direction, respectively.

 The rapid traverse rate of the Z-axis differs from the rapid traverse rate of other axes (X- and Y-
 axis). If the same program is executed by changing the coordinate system, the positioning path
 could differ depending on the selected coordinate system when multiple axes are moved at the
 rapid traverse rate simultaneously. This could cause the interference of the tool axis (Z-axis) with
 the workpiece. Therefore, programming must be made taking this into consideration.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-112

SECTION 14 MULTIPLE-POINT SPINDLE ORIENTATION
                   FUNCTION

The multiple-point spindle orientation function executes spindle orientation at a desired angular position.
This function is effectively used for executing tool breakage detection and automatic tool length offset for such
as a special tool whose tool bit cannot be brought into contact with the touch sensor when the spindle stops at
the normal spindle orientation position.

Y                                      Y

                                                Drive key

   Drive key

   X                                                                        X

[Normal spindle orientation position]  [Spindle orientation position for
                                       detecting breakage of a boring bar]

                                                ME32128R0301600010001

   Fig.14-1 Multiple-point Spindle Orientation
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-113
                                                SECTION 14 MULTIPLE-POINT SPINDLE ORIENTATION FUNCTION

14-1. Setting the Spindle Orientation Direction

            The spindle rotating direction for orientation is determined by an M command.

               M19:

                   Spindle orientation is executed in the CW direction.

               M118:

                   Spindle orientation is executed in the CCW direction.

               M119:

                   Spindle orientation direction is not definite; to shorten the spindle orientation time, spindle
                   orientation is executed in the direction along the longer path from the present spindle position to
                   the orientation position.

            The multiple-point spindle orientation function is valid for M118 and M119 in addition to M19. The
            following deals with multiple-point spindle orientation using M19 to simplify explanation.

               Programming format is:

                   M19 RS = 

               Angle is expressed by  which is specified in the degree unit. Decimal fractions are rounded off.

               Programmable range is from 0° to 360°. The desired angular orientation position is defined in

                   the spindle CW rotating direction in reference to the spindle orientation position for tool change
                   operation.

            [Supplement]

              1) If M19 (or M118 or M119) is specified without RS, spindle orientation is executed at the
                   orientation position for tool change.
                   That is, this operation is equivalent to "M19 RS = 0".

              2) An RS command must always be specified with M19 (or M118 or M119) in the same block.
                   a) If an RS command is specified independently, it does not cause an alarm and the answer
                       returns immediately. This command is cleared by the next M19.
                   b) An RS command is modal.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-114
                                                SECTION 14 MULTIPLE-POINT SPINDLE ORIENTATION FUNCTION

14-2. Tool Breakage Detection and Automatic Tool Length
        Offset

            The multiple-point spindle orientation function allows a special tool such as a boring bar to stop at
            the position where the tool bit faces the touch sensor.

            (1) Programming Format
                   To execute the tool breakage detection function or the automatic tool length offset function
                   using the multiple-point orientation function, specify the commands in the following format.

                 CALL OO30 PRS = 

                   Specify the desired angular orientation position in the spindle CW rotating direction in reference
                   to the spindle orientation position for tool change operation.

            [Supplement]

              1) CALL OO30
                   The spindle is positioned at the same position as in tool change operation. For the machine
                   equipped with the spindle orientation pin, the pin is inserted to the spindle.

              2) CALL OO30 PRS = 
                   The spindle is positioned at the angular position specified by . In this operation, the pin is not
                   inserted to the spindle even if the machine is equipped with the spindle orientation pin.
                   (The spindle orientation pin is inserted if =0.)

                                                                                   Spindle orientation position
                                                                                   (for tool change)

                                                             
                                                                                  RS = 

                                                                  Spindle orientation position
                                                                  (with multiple-point orientation function)

                                                                                                                                6124-E P-115
                                                                    SECTION 15 MANUAL OPERATION OF INDEX TABLE

SECTION 15 MANUAL OPERATION OF INDEX TABLE

This is the function for operating the index table in manual operation mode.
Manual operation of the index table is limited to the manual rapid traverse. Pulse handle feed or manual
cutting feed is not usable for index table operation. Moreover, manual interruption is impossible in automatic
operation mode.

15-1. Operation Method

            Procedure :

           1 Select the manual operation mode.

           2 Select the 4th axis on the operation panel.

           3 Operate the index table using the manual rapid traverse keys (+, -).

           4 When the manual rapid traverse key is released, the table stops after rotation to 1- or 5-degree

                   pitch position.

15-2. Supplement

            (1) The table is indexed to 1-degree or 5-degree pitch position depending on the table index
                   interval. So, there is a little time lag after the manual rapid traverse key is turned off until the
                   machine starts slowdown.

                                                                                                                Time lag

B-axis button           ME32128R0301700030001
     OFF

     Fig.15-1 Time lag

(2) For the rotary index axis with limits, the limit values may include the fraction which is smaller
      than the pitch unit. The rotary axis can move within the limits determined by the maximum and
      minimum values of the multiple that does not exceed the preset value in the pitch unit.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-116
                                        SECTION 16 AUTOMATIC SCHEDULE PROGRAM UPDATING FUNCTION

SECTION 16 AUTOMATIC SCHEDULE PROGRAM
                   UPDATING FUNCTION

The OSP can use schedule programs so that different kinds of workpieces can be machined continuously
using a pallet changer, etc. without requiring operator's intervention.
The automatic schedule program updating function allows an operator to add or modify the schedule during
scheduled operation by designating the automatic updating of the schedule and entering the new schedule
program.

16-1. Schedule Program

16-1-1.Restrictions on Schedule Program

            Since the function allows the schedule program to be updated while the machine is operating
            according to the schedule, the following restrictions are set to ensure safe operation.
            When the automatic schedule program updating function is valid, only the following blocks may be
            used in the schedule program.

               PSELECT block

                   Selects and executes a main program.

               VSET block

                   Sets variables.

               END block

                   Ends the schedule program.

            Only these three kinds of blocks may be used while the function is valid and other blocks such as a
            GOTO block and an IF block cannot be used.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-117
                                        SECTION 16 AUTOMATIC SCHEDULE PROGRAM UPDATING FUNCTION

16-1-2.Designation of Automatic Updating of a Schedule Program

            For the designation of automatic updating of a schedule program, the SCHEDULE PROGRAM
            SELECT (MEMORY MODE) window is used.
            Designation may be made by following the procedure indicated below.
            Procedure :

           1 Select the automatic mode.
           2 Select [F1] (SCHEDULE SDF_OPER) from the function menu.

                   The pop-up function menu for schedule program is displayed.

           3 Select [F1] (PROGRAM SELECT) from the pop-up function menu.

                   The SCHEDULE PROGRAM SELECT window is displayed.

           4 Select the schedule program to be updated automatically from the list of schedule programs.

                   Use the up/down cursor keys to select the desired schedule program.
                   After selecting the schedule program, press the right/left cursor keys and the cursor moves to
                   the UPDATE selection area.

           5 Select [F1] (MENU) then select EFFECT from the displayed pop-up function menu.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                     ME32128R0301800030001

                                  Fig.16-1 SCHEDULE PROGRAM SELECT (MEMORY MODE) Window

           6 Select [F7] (OK).

                   This designates automatic update for the selected schedule program and closes the window.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-118
                                        SECTION 16 AUTOMATIC SCHEDULE PROGRAM UPDATING FUNCTION

16-1-3.Editing a Schedule Program

            Although a schedule program can be edited in normal program editing procedure, the schedule
            program presently executed or the one entered for automatic updating cannot be edited directly.
            To edit such schedule programs, copy the desired schedule program to another file.

                                           Schedule program
                                     (being executed or entered)

        Copy

S1.SDF        S2.SDF

                                                           Edit the copy of the schedule program

                                                                                                                                                                   ME32128R0301800040001

                                         Fig.16-2 Editing a Schedule Program

[Supplement]

 The automatic schedule program updating function compares the schedule program being
 executed with the new schedule program when the new schedule program is entered. If there is a
 mismatch in blocks before the block presently executed, the new schedule program is not
 entered. Therefore, addition or modification to the schedule program may be made only in blocks
 to be executed later.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-119
                                        SECTION 16 AUTOMATIC SCHEDULE PROGRAM UPDATING FUNCTION

16-1-4.Entering the Updating Schedule Program

            Enter the schedule program that replaces the selected schedule program by following the procedure
            below.
            Procedure :

           1 Select the automatic mode.
           2 Select [F1] (SCHEDULE SDF_OPER) from the function menu.

                   The pop-up function menu for schedule program is displayed.

           3 Select [F3] (PROGRAM ENTRY) from the pop-up function menu.

                   The SCHEDULE PROGRAM ENTRY window is displayed.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                     ME32128R0301800050001

                                           Fig.16-3 SCHEDULE PROGRAM ENTRY Pop-up Window

           4 Select the program to be entered from the schedule program list.

                   Use the up/down cursor keys to select the desired schedule program.
                   Select [F6] (ENTRY). (The entered schedule program is displayed in the display area at the
                   upper section of the window.)
            This enters the selected schedule program and closes the window.
            After the entry of the schedule program for updating, this new schedule program is automatically
            selected when the next block is going to be executed after the completion of the presently executed
            block. The operation then continues based on the new schedule program.
            [Supplement]
              The entered schedule program file is cleared when a program or a schedule program is selected
              next.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-120
                                                                           SECTION 17 ADDITIONAL AXIS (ROTARY AXIS)

SECTION 17 ADDITIONAL AXIS (ROTARY AXIS)

17-1. Normal Operation Specification

            Rotary tables have infinite movement range, and, differing from standard axes (X, Y, Z), they return
            to their original position in a rotation of 360°. Due to the nature of rotary axes, they are programmed
            in the manner different from programming linear axes. The following describes the program rules for
            the rotary axes.

17-1-1.Axis Names

               A-axis: The table rotating around the X-axis

               B-axis: The table rotating around the Y-axis

               C-axis: The table rotating around the Z-axis

17-1-2.Removing the Additional Axis

            When the additional axis is removed, "----" is displayed as the current position of the removed axis.
            If the removed axis is commanded, alarm B 2359 Data word: rotating axis command (Code No. 4)
            results.

17-1-3.Programming Format

          Minimum Setting Unit

            0.001° or 0.0001°
            (Varies depending on the machine specification.)

          Programmable Range

               Absolute value:

                   0 to 360.000(0.001° specification)
                   0 to 360.0000 (0.0001° specification)

               Incremental value:

                   ±360.000 (0.001° specification)
                   ±360.0000 (0.0001° specification)
                                                                                           6124-E P-121
                                       SECTION 17 ADDITIONAL AXIS (ROTARY AXIS)

Rotating Direction Command

Conforms to ISO-841, ISO-1056 and JISB6310.

            Programming Mode           Command          Direction of Rotation
      Absolute command (4th axis)          M15                      CW
      Absolute command (5th axis)          M16                     CCW
                                          M115                      CW
           Incremental command            M116                     CCW
 M15, M16, M115 and M116 are modal.          +                      CW
                                             -                     CCW

1 mm/min = 1 deg/min

Calculating the Feedrate Command F

Carrying out cutting by moving only A-axis

                                                                                                                                                                   ME32128R0301900110001

                             Fig.17-8 Feedrate F in Operation Including Rotary Axis

Assume the workpiece diameter is D = 200 mm (7.87 in.). To obtain a feedrate of 150 mm/min (5.91
ipm) on the workpiece periphery, the feedrate to be programmed should be calculated in the
following manner.

(1) At the actual tool position, movement distance L in one turn (360°) is:
      L =  × D=3.14 × 200 = 628 mm (24.72 in.)

(2) Required time T to feed the tool this distance at the feedrate f=150 mm/min (5.91 ipm) is:
      T = L / f = 628 / 150 = 4.19 min

(3) The movement distance LNC, used for internal processing of the NC is:
      LNC = 360° = 360 mm (14.17 in.)

(4) The feedrate F to be specified in a program is:
      F = LNC / T=360 / 4.19 = 86

That is, "F86" should be specified in a program to perform cutting at the feedrate of 150 mm/min
(5.91 ipm).

[General Formula] F = {360 / ( × D)} × f.......... 1

where,
f: Required feedrate at the tool position
D: Workpiece outer diameter
: Ratio of circumference of circle to its diameter
Using this formula, the feedrate to be specified in a program may be calculated as indicated below
for the example case shown above.
F = {360 / (3.14 × 200 )} × 150 = 86
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-130
                                                              SECTION 17 ADDITIONAL AXIS (ROTARY AXIS)

This also applies to the calculation of the feedrate F when the B- or C-axis is used. If the C-axis is
used, however, double the distance "r" between the center of rotation and the tool position to obtain
the value of "D".

                                        r

                                                                                                                                      ME32128R0301900110002

             Fig.17-9 Distance "r" between the Center of Rotation and Tool Position (C-axis)

Carrying out cutting by moving A- and X-axis simultaneously

Assume the workpiece diameter is D = 200 mm (7.87 in.). To obtain a feedrate of 150 mm/min (5.91
ipm) when cutting the workpiece by simultaneous two-axis interpolation mode while feeding the A-
axis 120° and X-axis 150 mm (5.91 in.), the feedrate to be programmed should be calculated in the
following manner.

(1) Actual tool movement distance L on the workpiece is:

       L = ( x D x a/360)2+ x2 = (3.14 x 200 x 120/360)2 + 1502 = 258 mm (10.16 in.)

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    ME32128R0301900120001

(2) Required time T to feed the tool this distance at the feedrate f=150 mm/min (5.91 ipm) is:
      T = L / f = 258 / 150 = 1.72min

(3) The movement distance LNC, used for internal processing of the NC is:

LNC = a 2+ x 2 = 1202 + 1502 = 192 mm (7.56 in.)

                                                                               ME32128R0301900120002

(4) The feedrate F to be specified in a program is:
      F=LNC / T = 192 / 1.72 = 112

That is, "F112" should be specified in a program to perform cutting at the feedrate of 150 mm/min
(5.91 ipm).

[General Formula] F = {  a2 + x2                    } x f.......... Formula 2

                         ( x D x a/360) 2+ x 2

                                                                               ME32128R0301900120003

where,
f: Required feedrate at the tool position
a: Incremental value (deg.) of A-axis command
D: Workpiece outer diameter
x: Incremental value (mm) of X-axis command
: Ratio of circumference of circle to its diameter
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-131
                                                              SECTION 17 ADDITIONAL AXIS (ROTARY AXIS)

Entering "a = 360°" and "x = 0 mm" to formula (2) derives formula (1).
In the operation performed by simultaneous two-axis control between the B- and Y-axis, replace "a"
and "x" in the formula (2) with "b" and "y" to calculate the feedrate to be programmed. ("b" and "y" are
incremental values of B-axis and Y-axis command, respectively.)
If the C-axis is included in the simultaneous two-axis control, constant feedrate cannot be obtained
at the cutting position because the distance between the rotation center and the tool changes as
cutting progresses. In this case, divide the total cutting area into multiple segments in which
changes in the rotation center to the tool distance are relatively small and calculate the feedrate F to
be specified in a program for the individual segments.

[General Formula] F' = {  c2 + x 2             } x f.......... Formula 3

                          (2 x  x R' x C/360)

                                                                                                                           ME32128R0301900120004

where,
C: Incremental value (deg.) of C-axis command
R': Average value of distances between the C-axis rotation center and the tool within a segmented
area
Note that formula (3) is applicable only to the cases where simultaneous control is executed
between the C- and X-axis, the X-axis passes the center of the C-axis rotation, and R' is relatively
large compared to "x".

                          Tool path generated
                          with a workpiece fixed

                            C

17-1-4.Selecting the Rotary Axis with Limits

It is possible to switch A-axis between the rotary axis with limits and the normal type rotary axis
without limits by setting data at the parameter bit and turning on the power.
The relevant parameter is the NC optional parameter (bit) No. 70 bit 6.

NC optional parameter bit

No.  bit                                       Description

70   6                    The rotary axis with limits is selected as A-axis when the
                          power is supplied.

                          The normal type rotary axis is selected as A-axis when the
                          power is supplied.

Rotary axis with limits is selected when the power is supplied.

17-2. Multi-turn Specification

With the multi-turn specification, operations of an NC rotary table (A-, B- and C-axis) may be
programmed in the same manner as a linear axis. In this specification, M commands (M15 and M16)
used for specifying the rotary table rotating direction are invalid and the direction of rotary table
rotation is determined based on the relationship between the actual coordinate and the target
coordinate.
The mirror image function is also enabled for the NC rotary table.
In the explanation below, the term "position" and "coordinate" means the following.
Position:
Indicates the physical position of the NC rotary table.
(Position data is expressed by a value in the range from 0° to 360°.)
0°, 360° and 720° indicate the same position.
Coordinate:
Indicates the theoretical coordinate position of the NC rotary table.
0°, 360° and 720° indicate different coordinate positions.

-360               0  360                         720                                                      1080 Coordinate Value

                                                      All of these indicate the physically same position.  ME32128R0301900140001

17-2-1.Programming Format

          Minimum Setting Unit

0.001° or 0.0001°
(Varies depending on the machine specification.)

Programmable Range

-9999.999 to 9999.999° (0.001° specification)
-9999.9999 to 9999.9999° (0.0001° specification)

Rotating Direction Command

The rotating direction is determined based on the positional relationship between the actual
coordinate and the target coordinate. If the target coordinate is larger than the actual coordinate, the
NC rotary table rotates in the CW direction and if the target coordinate is smaller than the actual
coordinate, the NC rotary table rotates in the CCW direction. In this operation, the programming
mode (absolute or incremental) has no influence.

Target coordinate     Actual coordinate           Target coordinate
(command value)       of NC rotary table          (command value)

      CCW rotation    CW rotation                      Presently selected coordinate

                                                                                                  ME32128R0301900170001
                                                                                    6124-E P-133
                                SECTION 17 ADDITIONAL AXIS (ROTARY AXIS)

Feedrate Command

            Refer to "Feedrate in Simultaneous Two-axis Control" and "Calculating the Feedrate Command F".

17-2-2.Outline of Operations

          Automatic and MDI Modes

Commands, operations and position display greater than 360° (one turn) are possible regardless of
the absolute or incremental programming mode.
<Example>

N001 G90 G0 C0.0
N002 C540.0
N003 G91 C540.0
N001 ... Positioning of the C-axis at 0° coordinate.
N002 ... Positioning of the C-axis at 540° coordinate.

                Positioning is executed at 180° position by making 1.5 turns.
N003 ... Positioning of the C-axis at 1080° coordinate.

                Positioning is executed at 0° position by making 1.5 turns from the actual position.

If the mirror image function is specified, it is executed as indicated below.

Absolute mode (G90)

The work zero point is taken as the center of the mirror image function.
<Example>
Mirror image C-axis: ON
N001 G90 C-20
N002 C540

-540°                Work zero  540°

mirror image function ON

The NC rotary table may be manually operated based on the coordinate values as with a linear axis.

These operations are possible as with a linear axis.

As with the multi-turn specification, a rotary axis with limit can also be processed like a linear axis.
The relationship between these two types of rotary axis is indicated below.

 If both the "rotary axis with limit" specification and the "multi-turn" specification are selected,

   priority is given to the "rotary axis with limit" specification.

17-2-5.Turning the Power ON/OFF and Resetting the NC

            The coordinate value of the NC rotary table actual position in the work coordinate system is changed
            to a value within 360° by the following operations.

            Procedure :

           1 Turning the power ON/OFF
           2 Turning the machine lock from ON to OFF
           3 Resetting the NC to clear alarm A

            It is possible to select whether or not the coordinate value (in the work coordinate system) of the NC
            rotary table actual position is changed to a value within 360 by the setting for ROUNDING
            PROCESS IS CARRIED OUT WHEN CONTROL IS RESET of the rotary axis parameters.

                  YES Changed to a value within 360°
                  NO The present value remains.
            "NC reset" includes the following operations.

               Pressing the NC RESET button

               Mode reset operation (selecting the manual mode by the mode selection switch on the

                   operation panel)

               Turning the machine lock ON/OFF
