17-2-6.Sequence Restart and Home Position Return

Sequence Restart

The multi-turn specification NC rotary table returns to the operation restart point within 360° table
rotation if sequence restart operation is executed.
(The table rotates to the operation restart position within 360° rotation, then the return position is
taken as the return coordinate.)
The table rotation direction is determined based on the relationship between the actual coordinate
and the return coordinate.
<Example>

                  360         720      1080       1440

           0

                                                                                                                                                                                                         ME32128R0301900250001

Assume the actual coordinate is 1080°.
N198 X50
N199 G90 C540
Assume the operation should be restarted from N199 block.
Since the restart coordinate is 540°, the table rotates CCW 180° from 1080° coordinate so that it is
positioned at 900° coordinate. Then, the actual coordinate is changed from 900° to 540°.

Home Position

(1) Positioning at the Home Position
      The multi-turn specification NC rotary table executes positioning at the home position through
      the shorter path (within 360°).
      <Example>

                  360         720 (C)  1080       1440

    0
                                                              (B) (A)

                                                                                                                                                                                             ME32128R0301900260001

Assume 0° position is the position of the home position.

 (A) If the actual coordinate is 960°
          Positioning is executed at the home position coordinate 1080° (0° position) in the CW
          rotation.

 (B) If the actual coordinate is 840°
          Positioning is executed at the home position coordinate 720° (0° position) in the CCW
          rotation.

 (C) If the actual coordinate is 900°
          (The actual coordinate is equidistant from the two home position coordinates.)
          Positioning is executed at the home position coordinate 1080° (0° position) in the CW
          rotation.
          If the actual coordinate is equidistant from the two home position coordinates,
          positioning at the home position is executed in the CW rotation of the rotary table.
                                                                                                      6124-E P-138
                                                  SECTION 17 ADDITIONAL AXIS (ROTARY AXIS)

(2) Incremental Commands Immediately after Positioning at Home Position
      When an incremental command is specified in the block immediately after the home position
      return command (M60, G30), the incremental command is executed in reference to the
      coordinate where the rotary table was positioned just before the execution of the home position
      return command.
      <Example>

                         360         720           1080            1440

    0                                                              ME32128R0301900260002

Assume the 0° position is taken as the home position.
 N101 G90 C960
 N102 M60
 N103 G91 C100

N101 Positioning at 960°
N102 Returning to the home position at 1080°
N103 Positioning at 1060° (960 + 100)

The work zero offset setting range is indicated below.

the specified coordinate value.

coordinate system (H = 0).
-9720.000 ~ 9720.000° (Actual coordinate display, for ±27 turns)
In a work coordinate system, therefore, these limits are shifted by the work zero offset amount.

17-2-9.Installation of Multi-turn NC Rotary Table

            To use the multi-turn specification NC rotary table, the system parameters described in "Setting
            System Parameters" must be set. If the NC rotary table must be operated before setting the
            machine zero offset, rotate it only in the CW direction.
            If the NC rotary table is rotated in the CCW direction although the machine zero offset data is not
            set, "ALARM A 1243 CON 0 pass" occurs. When the alarm lamp is lit, turn off the power once then
            turn it on again.
            After installing the multi-turn NC rotary table, set the system parameters.

          Setting System Parameters

            (1) ZERO OFFSET (MACHINE)
                   For the multi-turn NC rotary table, CAL, SET, and ADD functions are used in the following
                   manner when setting the machine zero offset data.

                    CAL

                         The function calculates the machine zero offset data (within 360°) from the actual position
                         (OCON, within 360°) of the multi-turn NC rotary table. The machine zero offset data always
                         falls within 360°.

                    SET

                         Set a value so that the value to be input will be within 360°.

                    ADD

                         The input value is added to the existing machine zero offset data.
                         Input a value so that the machine zero offset value is within 360°.

          Home Position Data

            The home position data for the rotary table must be within 360°.
            Setting the home position data is not necessary if the rotary table home position does not exist.

17-2-10.Multi-turn Rotary Table Actual Position Reset Function

          Overview

            When the multi-turn function of the axis of rotation is valid and increased or decreased in one
            direction, the actual position is a large value. Returning this to "0" requires many rotations and takes
            time. This function serves to keep the actual value of the multi-turn rotary table rounded within 0 to
            360° by a part program command.
                                                                      6124-E P-140
                  SECTION 17 ADDITIONAL AXIS (ROTARY AXIS)

Function Details

Use G438 command to reset the actual position of the multi-turn rotary table. The reset here means
keeping the actual position rounded within 0 to 360° on the workpiece coordinate.

Use the following format for program command.
<<Command format>>
Multi-turn rotary table actual position reset command

G438 P1

Program example) G438 C1
                             Resets the actual position of the multi-turn rotary table C-axis.

  The actual position of the multi-turn rotary table axis ordered at "1" is kept rounded within 0 to

     360° on the workpiece coordinate. The actual position of the multi-turn axis is changed;
     however, the rotary table remains unchanged.

  Nothing is performed for the axis without command.

  When "1" is ordered for the axis with multi-turn function invalid or one other than rotation axis

     (such as X-axis) or removing axis, alarm B 2665 is displayed.

  When the value which is other than "1" is ordered, alarm B 2665 is displayed.

  The summation of shift quantity remains unchanged with this command. When the summation

     of shift quantity is  0, the result of resetting the actual position with this command is not
     necessarily within 0 to 360°.

  Only a sequence name command or a block skip command can be issued before this

     command.

  The actual position is changed after issuing this command. If this command is issued while the

     function to memorize the command value into the inside, it does not function well. Thus, this
     command is issued to the symmetry axis of flat surface command and alarm B 2665 is
     displayed during executing following functions.

        The function of coordinate calculation on the flat surface axis is underway.

        The function of chamfering at any angle is underway.

        The function of cutter radius compensation is underway.

        The function of fixed cycle is underway.

  This function is valid during return search.

  The function can be used even if the spindle trace control axis is a multi-turn type. However, if

     ordered without spindle trace control, the function is invalid.

  The function can be used for synchronous rotation axis.

  The function can be used during machine locked condition.
                                                           6124-E P-141
       SECTION 17 ADDITIONAL AXIS (ROTARY AXIS)

Alarm

Alarm B 2665 Program bad direct:Multi-turn rotary axis position reset

        Reset command (G438) for the multiple revolution rotary table is inappropriate.
        [Index]
        None
        [Character-string]
        None
        [Code]
        1: The command value is other than "1".
        2: The commanded axis does not support multiple revolution rotary table.
        3: The commanded axis is removed.
        4 to 7:
        For target axes of the plane command, issue a reset command for the multiple revolution
        rotary table (G438) and,

             4: In the coordinate calculation function for axes of the plane command.
             5: In the any angle chamfering command function.
             6: In the tool radius compensation function.
             7: In the fixed cycle function.
        [Probable Faulty Locations]
        Program error.
        [Measures to Take]
        Correct the program.

SECTION 18 CYLINDER SIDE-FACE MACHINING
                   FUNCTION

The cylinder side-face machining function allows the machining on the side face of a cylindrical workpiece to
be programmed in the same manner as if machining is executed in a conventional plane. To enable this, the
function replaces the rotary axis with a linear axis and generates a programming plane by developing the side
face of the cylinder.

X  into a plane.

   the selected plane assuming the                                                     a cylinder is accomplished.
   operation in a conventional plane.

                                                          SECTION 18 CYLINDER SIDE-FACE MACHINING FUNCTION

18-1. Programming Format

The procedure for executing cylinder side-face machining is indicated below.

Procedure :

  1 Select the plane that includes the rotary axis. (G17, G18, G19)

  2 Select the cylinder side-face machining mode. (G175 R__)

  3 Program the machining.

      For details of the commands, refer to "18-1-3. Machining Commands".
      Programming unit of a rotary axis is "degree".
      For a feedrate (F), designate the feedrate on the developed plane.

  4 Cancel the cylinder side-face machining mode.(G174)

18-1-1.Cylinder Side-face Machining Mode

Selecting the Cylinder Side-face Machining Mode

G17 Xp__Yp__ G175 R__

G18 Zp__Xp__ G175 R__

G19 Yp__Zp__ G175 R__

G17:   Xp-Yp plane selection
G18:   Zp-Xp plane selection
G19:   Yp-Zp plane selection
Xp:    X-axis, U-axis, or a rotary axis for which X-axis is set as the corresponding basic axis
Yp:    Y-axis, V-axis, or a rotary axis for which Y-axis is set as the corresponding basic axis
Zp:    Z-axis, W-axis, or a rotary axis for which Z-axis is set as the corresponding basic axis
       (For the corresponding axis, refer to "18-1-2. Corresponding Basic Axis".)
G175:  Selects the cylinder side-face machining mode.
R:     Cylinder radius (1 to 5000 mm (0.04 to 196.85 in.))

Which of the rotary axes is included in the selected plane is determined by the axis addresses
designated in the plane selection block.
<Example>

G17 A__X__  (A=-Y)  ...... A-X plane
G17 B__Y__  (B=X)   ...... B-Y plane
G18 C__Z__  (C=-X)  ...... C-Z plane
G18 A__X__  (A=Z)   ...... A-X plane
G19 B__Y__  (B=-Z)  ...... B-Y plane
G19 C__Z__  (C=Y)   ...... C-Z plane
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-144
                                             SECTION 18 CYLINDER SIDE-FACE MACHINING FUNCTION

Canceling the Cylinder Side-face Machining Mode

G174

[Supplement]

 1) In the cylinder side-face machining mode, select the plane that includes a rotary axis.
 2) Plane selection is not possible by the combination of a basic axis and its corresponding rotary

      axis or its parallel axis.
 3) Name of rotary axis, rotating direction, and corresponding basic axis must be set for the rotary

      axis parameters. For details of rotary axis parameter, refer to "SECTION 17 ADDITIONAL
      AXIS (ROTARY AXIS)/Rotary Axis Parameters in 17-1-3".
 4) Set the multi-turn specification for the additional axis. This specification is set using the rotary
      axis parameter. For details of rotary axis parameter, refer to "SECTION 17 ADDITIONAL AXIS
      (ROTARY AXIS)/Rotary Axis Parameters in 17-1-3".
 5) Angular commands are used for the address characters A, B and C that represent a rotary
      axis.
 6) Selection of plane (G17, G18, G19) must be made in a block different from the block that
      contains G175 (selecting the cylinder side-face machining mode).
 7) The following functions can be designated only after the cylinder side-face machining
      designation.

        Any-angle chamfering function
        Coordinate calculation function
        Cutter radius compensation function
        Graphic enlargement/reduction function
        Area machining function

 8) The cylinder side-face machining mode cannot be selected or canceled in the following
      modes.

        Fixed cycle function
        Cutter radius compensation function
        Area machining function
        Coordinate calculation function
        Any-angle chamfering function
        3-D coordinate conversion function

 9) The following commands cannot be specified in the cylinder side-face machining mode.

        3-D coordinate conversion function
        3-D circular interpolation

18-1-2.Corresponding Basic Axis

      When programming cylinder side-face machining, a rotary axis is replaced with a basic axis. The
      basic axis that replaces a rotary axis is called the corresponding basic axis of that rotary axis. The
      corresponding basic axis can be either of the two basic axes that are not used as the center axis of
      the rotary axis. The plane defined by the corresponding basic axis and the center axis is the cylinder
      side-face development plane.

[Supplement]

 When you read the separate volume Programming Manual, regard the X-axis (X coordinate) as

Any-angle Chamfering Function

When chamfering (C-chamfer, R-chamfer) a corner that has an arbitrary angle, simply specify the
coordinate values of the corner apex and the chamfer size. The function automatically calculates the
start and end points and executes chamfering accordingly.

G17 (Xp-Yp) plane  CHFC         Xp__Yp__ L__ [Q__] [I__] [J__]
G18 (Zp-Xp) plane  CHFR         Zp__ Xp__ L__ [Q__] [K__] [I__]
G19 (Yp-Zp) plane  CHFC         Yp__ Zp__ L__ [Q__] [J__] [K__]
                   CHFR
                   CHFC
                   CHFR

CHFC     :    C-chamfering command
              R-chamfering command
CHFR     :    Corner apex coordinate value
              Chamfer size (0 ~ 99999999mm)
Xp, Yp, Zp :  Travel distance after chamfering (0 ~ 99999999mm)
              Imaginary direction of movement (directional vector of Q)
L        :

Q        :

I, J, K  :

I        ... Xp-axis direction

J        ... Yp-axis direction

K        ... Zp-axis direction

[Supplement]
 Always use length command for chamfer size L and travel distance after chamfering Q.

Fixed Cycle

A fixed cycle operation is possible in the cylinder side-face coordinate system. For a cycle axis, only
a linear axis may be used.
In fine boring (G76) and back boring (G87) cycles, use angular commands for shift amount I, J, and
K if a rotary axis corresponds to the shift movement axis. For I (cut-in depth per cut) and J (tool
extraction depth) in a deep hole drilling cycle (G83), always use a length command regardless of the
selected plane.
For details, refer to the separate volume Programming Manual.

Area Machining

An area machining operation is possible in the cylinder side-face coordinate system. For a cycle
axis, only a linear axis may be used.
For reference point coordinate values, I (length along the horizontal axis direction) and J (length
along the vertical axis direction), commands should be specified in angles for the axis that
corresponds to a rotary axis.
For details, refer to the separate volume Programming Manual.

The pallet identification function provided for the machine equipped with 2-pallet APC is used to judge the
pallet presently mounted on the machine table using a program. The function is available for the parallel 2-
pallet APC, the rotary 2-pallet APC and other types of 2-pallet APC.
For the execution of this function, either a system variable or a pallet identification command is used.

N001  IF [VPLTK EQ 1] N100

      MSG [PALLET NG]             Note: Only for the NC with the
      GOTO NEND                   message function

N100 (Part program for A-pallet)

           GOTO NEND
N200 (Part program for B-pallet)

NEND M02

After the execution of a pallet change cycle, the pallet on the machine table is judged. If A-pallet is
on the machine table, the program is executed continuously and if B-pallet is on the machine table,
alarm B occurs and the program stops at N200.

SECTION 20 TOOL MANAGEMENT FUNCTION

The tool management function manages various information about tools.
Information will be given a number (tool number) from 1 to 99999999.
To specify a tool change in the machining program, set this tool number.
Specify a tool number in the format "TOO" (T command).
T commands, which are not restricted by the number of pots in the magazine, enable even a machine with a
less magazine capacity to manage a lot of tools.

                                               Assign a tool number
                                               to each tool.

              4                                expectancy data of up to 999 tools.

                                                                              SECTION 20 TOOL MANAGEMENT FUNCTION

20-1. Tool Management Screens and Information to be
        Managed

            The tool management function manages the tools in the TOOL LIFE sheet in the TOOL DATA
            screen.
            The TOOL LIFE sheet displays the tool number currently specified yellow.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                     ME32128R0302200020001

                                                        Fig.20-1 Tool Management Screen
            The information items to be managed and their descriptions are shown below:
            (1) Distinction between commanded tool and spare tool

                   Whether the tool of a specific tool number is the "commanded tool" or a "tool to be used as a
                   spare tool" is determined. Each tool number is judged to be "ORG (commanded tool)" or "SPR
                   (spare tool)."

                    ORG: Tool as commanded in the machining program
                    SPR: Spare tool

            [Supplement]
              Commanded tool/Spare tool can be set only on a machine incorporating a spare tool changeover
              function.

            (2) G No.
                   GRP No. manages group numbers when changing a tool to a spare tool.
                   Each tool is included in an appropriate group for management.
                   If any tool selected by a T command is not accepted, the tool will be changed to another
                   available tool within the same group.
                   GRP No. (group numbers) can be set between 0 and 999.
                   For a tool you do not want to change to a spare tool or for a tool without any spare tool, set the
                   group number to 0. The tool in group No. 0 will not be changed.
                                                                                    6124-E P-160
                                  SECTION 20 TOOL MANAGEMENT FUNCTION

(3) T.NO (Tool Number)
      T.NO is to manage information about tool numbers.
      This information is just for display. To change settings, modify the information about the
      appropriate tool number on the TOOL DATA sheet. (Refer to the separate volume
      "OPERATION MANUAL / DATA OPERATION, SECTION 3 TOOL DATA SETTING.")

(4) POT
      POT displays the pot number in which each tool is stored.
      This information is just for display. To change settings, modify the information about each tool
      number in the MAGAZINE INFO. sheet. (Refer to the separate volume "OPERATION MANUAL
      / DATA OPERATION, SECTION 3 TOOL DATA SETTING.")

(5) KIND
      KIND displays the tool kind information as in the table below.

  symbol                            symbol
  UNDEF.     Not set                                                  Double-sided cutter
  ACS. TL    Other tools          CHAMFER                             Tap

BACK-BOR                          FACEMILL
                                  SIDE-CTR

(6) MNG. MODE (tool life determination modes)
      This item manages the tool life determination mode. There are seven determination modes:

a. NOT MODE

b. TIME (SPARE)
      Judges the tool concerned to be no longer serviceable if it operates over the set operating
      time, and changes the tool to a spare tool when the same T command is executed.

c. TIME
      Judges the tool concerned to be no longer serviceable if it operates over the set operating
      time, but keeps the tool operating without changing it to a spare tool.

d. COUNT (SPARE)
      Judges the tool concerned to be no longer serviceable if the number of holes the tool has
      drilled exceeds the set number of drilled holes, and changes the tool to a spare tool when
      the same T command is executed.

e. COUNT
      Judges the tool concerned to be no longer serviceable if the number of holes the tool has
      drilled exceeds the set number of drilled holes, but keeps the tool operating without
      changing it to a spare tool.

f. DIST. (SPARE)
      Judges the tool concerned to be no longer serviceable if it operates over the set operating
      distance, and changes the tool to a spare tool when the same T command is executed.

g. DIST.
      Judges the tool concerned to be no longer serviceable if it operates over the set operating
      distance, but keeps the tool operating without changing it to a spare tool.
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-161
                                                                 SECTION 20 TOOL MANAGEMENT FUNCTION

[Supplement]

 1) The above management modes can be set only on machines with a spare tool changeover
      function.

 2) Tool overload (MOP) causes an alarm and immediately stops the ongoing operation,
      irrespective of which management mode is selected.

 3) In case of tool wear (MOP) and tool breakage (detected by a touch sensor), the tool will be
      changed to a spare tool when the same T command is executed, irrespective of which
      management mode is selected.

* To allow travel control, it is necessary to set NC optional parameter, bit No.87 bit 0.

No.  bit      bit status                                   Description

87   0                    Travel control is not carried out in tool life management
                          function.

                          Travel control is carried out in tool life management
                          function.

(7) OK/NG (tool state and causes of defects)
      Manages the current state of the tool.

 The table below shows the information displayed in the OK/NG field:

OK        NG1 (LIFE)                          NG2 (LIFE)      NG3 (LIFE)

          NG1 (WEAR)                          NG2 (WEAR)      NG3 (WEAR)

          NG1 (OVLOAD)                        NG2 (OVLOAD)    NG3 (OVLOAD)
          NG1 (TORQUE)                        NG2 (TORQUE)    NG3 (TORQUE)

          NG1 (BREAK)                         NG2 (BREAK)     NG3 (BREAK)

          NG1 (USER)                          NG2 (USER)      NG3 (USER)

 Below are the main causes of defects related to "NG" tools:

 Tool life expiration detected by the tool life management function (Refer to "SECTION

   21 TOOL LIFE MANAGEMENT FUNCTION.")

 Tool breakage detected by the automatic tool breakage detection function

 Tool wear and tool overload detected by the overload monitoring function (MOP)

 Tool defect detected by the operator

 The levels of tool defects are classified as follows:

OK No defect

NG1 The tool remains used until the operator takes action.

         The tool will be changed to a spare tool when the next T command is executed.
NG2 In this case, an alarm will occur and the ongoing operation will immediately stop if

         there is no spare tool.

NG3  An alarm will occur and the ongoing operation will immediately stop even during
     cutting.

The level of defect (NG1, NG2, NG3) depends on the cause of the defect.

20-2. Tool Management Data Setting

20-2-1.TOOL MANAGEMENT Screen Display and Data Setting

The procedure for displaying the TOOL MANAGEMENT screen and setting management data on
each tool is described below:

TOOL MANAGEMENT screen display

Procedure :

  1 Press the TOOL DATA SET key among the mode selection keys.

The data in the data table on the TOOL MANAGEMENT screen can be sorted by tool number or

                         ascending order of the tool numbers.

                         The tool data and the data in the data table on the TOOL LIFE sheet are sorted by
                         descending order of the tool numbers.

                     [F3] (GRP.NO (UP))

                         The tool data and the data in the data table on the TOOL LIFE sheet are sorted by
                         ascending order of the group numbers.

                     [F4] (GRP.NO (DOWN))

                         The tool data and the data in the data table on the TOOL LIFE sheet are sorted by
                         descending order of the group numbers.

20-2-5.Resetting Defective Tool Data

            In case of a tool defect due to a specific cause, the defective tool registration will not be reset until
            the operator enters "OK" in the OK/NG evaluation column of the tool life table.
            After the cause of the tool defect is removed or the defective tool is replaced, reset the defective tool
            registration as follows:

            Procedure :

           1 Place the cursor in the "OK/NG" column for the tool number concerned.

           2 Select [F1] (SET) from the function menu.

                   OK/NG 2 SETTING is displayed in the function menu.

           3 Select [F2] (OK) from the function menu.

                   Rewrite other management data if necessary.
                   The causes for defects and precautions for resetting are described below:
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-168
                                                                 SECTION 20 TOOL MANAGEMENT FUNCTION

Tool Life (LIFE)

"NG2" is triggered if the left time is below "0" when the TOOL MANAGEMENT mode is time
(changeover) or number (changeover) or distance (changeover). "NG2 (LIFE)" will be displayed in
the OK/NG SETTING window.
Change the tool with a new one and rewrite the tool life data, and then rewrite the OK/NG data to
"OK".
To use the current tool for a while, modify the tool management mode data or rewrite the LEFT TIME
data to be positive, and then rewrite the OK/NG data to "OK".

Tool Breakage (found by the automatic tool breakage detection function) (BREAK)

When tool breakage is found by the automatic tool breakage detection function, the OK/NG data will
be set as "NG2".
NG2 (BREAK) will be displayed in the OK/NG SETTING window.
Change the defective tool with a new one, and then rewrite the OK/NG data to "OK".

Overload (found by MOP) (OVLOAD)

When the MOP sends an overload alarm signal, the OK/NG data will be "NG3" and the NC will stop.
NG3 (OVLOAD) will be displayed in the OK/NG SETTING window.
When the NC RESET button is pressed, "NG3" will change to "NG2".
Investigate the cause of overload and take required measures, such as replacement of the tool, and
then rewrite the OK/NG data to "OK".

Tool Wear (found by MOP) (WEAR)

When the MOP sends a tool wear alarm signal, the OK/NG data will be "NG2". NG2 (WEAR) will be
displayed in the OK/NG SETTING window.
Investigate the cause of the defect and take required measures, such as replacement of the tool,
and then rewrite the OK/NG data to "OK".

User (judgment by operator) (USER)

When "NG2" is entered as OK/NG data in the OK/NG column from the operation panel, the OK/NG
data will be "NG2".
After taking required measures, rewrite the OK/NG data to "OK".
                                                                                  6124-E P-169
                                SECTION 20 TOOL MANAGEMENT FUNCTION

20-3. Changeover of Spare Tool

20-3-1.Selection of Spare Tool

When the tool commanded in a part program is defective (NG2 or NG3), the changeover to the
spare tool in the same group as the commanded tool will be made.
If all the tools in the same group set in the magazine are found to be defective, the operation will be
stopped with an alarm at the time the commanded tool is set to the spindle.

20-3-2.Changeover of Tool Offset Number

Designation of the tool offset number can be done either by specifying H number and D number
directly or by specifying the 1st offset number, 2nd offset number and the 3rd offset number.
Using the former method, only one offset number is available for the same tool when the
changeover to the spare tool is made. Using the latter method, up to three offset numbers are
available even when the changeover to the spare tool is made.

Command in    When the Tool Specified by T Command     When the Spare Tool is Used
Part Program        (the Commanded Tool) is Used

  D1 - D999   Offset number specified in part program  1st offset number of spare tool
  H1 - H999                                            An alarm is triggered if the tool
                                                       number of the spare tool
     DA HA                                             exceeds 1000.
     DB HB
    DC HC     1st offset number of commanded tool      1st offset number of spare tool

              2nd offset number of commanded tool      2nd offset number of spare tool

              3rd offset number of commanded tool      3rd offset number of spare tool

[Supplement]

 (1) Tools in the same group will be selected in the following order;

    When started from the initial condition (when ORG/SPR is not designated)

      a) The tool commanded by the part program
      b) If tool noted in a) is not usable, the first usable tool found in a tool number search beginning

          with tool 1 and conducted in ascending order

    When a tool was selected before (when ORG/SPR is designated)

      a) The tool which has been selected
      b) If the tool of a) is not usable, the tool commanded by the part program
      c) If tool noted in b) is not usable, the first usable tool found in a tool number search beginning

          with tool 1 and conducted in ascending order
 (2) The tool offset amount is changed when the tool is changed to the spare tool.

20-4. Management of Tools for Which ATC Is Not Used

            For MDB which is not equipped with ATC, and for large diameter tools and universal attachments
            which cannot be stored in the ATC magazine, tool life management and automatic tool length offset
            are possible. Tool numbers can be designated for manual tool setting.
            For a group number used by the tool management function, always set "0".

20-4-1.Command Format

            TN =  (: 0 to 99999999)
            Designate the tool number for the next tool.

               The timing at which the set tool number data is transferred from the next tool to the active tool:

                   a. Without ATC (MDB)
                         After the completion of M06 (impossible in the manual mode)

                   b. Manual Tool Change for ATC Specification
                         After the completion of M70

                   c. Attachment for which ATC cannot be Used
                         After the completion of M177

20-4-2.Tool Data Setting

            The tool number of a manually set tool can be set and applied on the TOOL DATA screen. The
            procedure for setting and applying the data is described as follows.

            (1) Create the tool data on the TOOL DATA sheet.

            (2) Select [F1] (INSERT TOOL) on the MAGAZINE INFO. sheet to display the SETTING popup
                   window.

            (3) Apply the tool data created on the TOOL DATA sheet to the pot.

            It is possible to set the next tool and the current tool on the machine without the ATC.
            Enter the tool number to the current tool no. and the next tool no. on the MAGAZINE INFO. sheet.

               If a manually-set tool is set when the ATC tool is set as the next tool or the current tool, an error

                   will occur.

               If the tool number set in the MAGAZINE INFO. sheet is set to a manually-set tool, an error will

                   occur.

SECTION 21 TOOL LIFE MANAGEMENT FUNCTION

The NC machine counts and totals the time used for cutting or the number of workpieces, or the travel used
for cutting for each tool with cutting commands (G01, G02, and G03). The total cutting time or counted
number of workpiece, or cutting travel is compared with those preset to determine the serviceability of specific
cutting tools. For a cutting tool whose service life has expired, the management function checks whether a
spare tool is registered for it and if so, the spare tool is automatically mounted when the life-expired tool is
next specified. In case no spare tool is available, the cycle stops with alarm display.
If the tool becomes no longer available in the middle of machining, the changeover takes place when the
same tool number is commanded. The changeover to spare tools is made in ascending order of tool numbers
within the same groups.

21-1. Setting Tool Life Management Data

For further information about the TOOL LIFE sheet, Refer to "SECTION 20 TOOL MANAGEMENT
FUNCTION."
Set TOOL LIFE sheet as follows:

Procedure :

  1 Set a tool no. to the pot in the TOOL DATA sheet.
  2 Group the tools (group setting).
  3 Set SET TIME (time, quantity, travel) and LEFT TIME (time, quantity, travel).

The command "TLFON" programmed after the above setting starts tool life management.

 Setting NC optional parameter bit No. 87 bit 0 is necessary in order to carry out travel control.

No.  bit  bit status  Description

                      Travel control is not carried out in tool life management
                      function.

87   0

                      Travel control is carried out in tool life management function.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-173
                                                                      SECTION 21 TOOL LIFE MANAGEMENT FUNCTION

21-2. Command for Activating Tool Life Management

            For activating the tool life management function, execute the command TLFON.
            Once executed, the TLFON command remains effective until the power supply is turned off or the
            TLFOFF command is executed.
             TLFON: Tool life management on
             TLFOFF: Tool life management off

            The procedure for setting the "TLFON" command is described below:.

            Procedure :

           1 Select the MDI OPERATION mode by pressing the MDI key.

           2 Key in "TLFON" through the keyboard.

           3 Press the WRITE key.

           4 Press the CYCLE START button.

                   [Supplement]

                     When tool life management is carried out in terms of a count number, the command TLCO
                     should be used in addition to the command TLFON. The manner for entering the TLCO
                     command is explained below.
                     1) Select the MDI operation mode.
                     2) Key in "TLCO Qxxx" through the keyboard.

                          Here, "xxx" indicates the number to be deducted from the set count data. Its range is
                          from 0 to 999.
                     3) Press the WRITE key.
                     4) Press the CYCLE START button.
                     When this command is executed while the management mode of the tool in the spindle is
                     "count", the remaining value is counted down by the number entered following the address
                     character Q.
                     In case no number is specified following "Q", then the data is counted down by one (1).
                     The TLCO command performs countdown only when it is commanded.
                     In the automatic mode operation, the TLFON, TLFOFF and TLCO commands must be
                     issued in the same manner.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-174
                                                                      SECTION 21 TOOL LIFE MANAGEMENT FUNCTION

21-3. Resetting Tool Life Data

           When a tool displayed as NG is judged to be usable

            When the life of the tool exceeds the set time but the operator judges it to still be usable, follow the
            procedure below to extend its life.

            Procedure :

           1 Press the TOOL DATA SET key among the mode selection keys.

                   The lamp at the upper of the "TOOL DATA SET" key lights, and any tool data setting screen
                   appears.

           2 Display the TOOL DATA screen and select the TOOL LIFE sheet.

                   The TOOL LIFE MANAGEMENT screen appears.

           3 Place the cursor in the "OK/NG" column for the tool number you want to extend its life.

           4 Select [F1] (SET) from the function menu.

                   OK/NG SETTING is displayed in the function menu.

           5 Select [F2] (OK) from the function menu.

           6 Move the cursor to the "LEFT TIME" input column, and enter an appropriate value.

                   <Example>: Enter "30" if the tool is judged to be usable for another 30 minutes.
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-175

Replacing a tool with a new one

When the life of the tool exceeds the set time and it is replaced with a new one, reset the information
about the tool number concerned according to the following procedure below:

Procedure :

  1 Register information about the new tool on the TOOL DATA sheet.

      For further information, Refer to "OPERATION MANUAL/DATA OPERATION, SECTION 3
      TOOL DATA SETTING."

  2 Switch the screen to the TOOL LIFE sheet.

  3 Place the cursor in the "OK/NG" column for the tool number you want to extend its life.

  4 Select [F1] (SET) from the function menu.

      OK/NG SETTING is displayed in the function menu.

  5 Select [F2] (OK) from the function menu.

  6 Move the cursor to the "SET TIME" input column, and enter an appropriate value.

  7 Move the cursor to the "LEFT TIME" input column, and enter an appropriate value.

Setting LEFT TIME Data to the Same Value as SET TIME for All Tools

Follow the procedure below to change the "LEFT TIME" data on all tool numbers to the same value
of the "SET TIME" data.

Procedure :

  1 Select the MDI OPERATION mode.

  2 Enter the command TLFR through the keyboard.

  3 Press the WRITE key.

  4 Press the CYCLE START button.

21-4. Program Examples

21-4-1.Tool Life Management by Tool Used Time and Travel Data

N1 TLFON

N2 T2       T2 : End mill

N3 M6

N4 T5       T5: Drill

N5 G0 X20 Y30 S200

N6 G56 Z15 H2

N7 G1 G41 X40 D2 F100 M3

   (The tools programmed within blocks are managed in accordance with the setting done in
   the MANAGEMENT mode. The replacement of the tool having been used to its life occurs
   when that tool is commanded.)

:

:

N24 TLFOFF

N25 M2

 With the program example given above, the tool life management function is called up and

   cancelled by the programmed commands. The commands - TLFON and TLFOFF - can also be
   entered through the keyboard. Once the tool life management function is activated by the
   command TLFON either programmed or entered through the keyboard, it remains active until
   the "TLFOFF" command is issued, the power is turned off or the reset button is pressed.

 When the tool life of the T2 (end mill) tool has expired, its spare tool T32 is automatically

   selected when the same command is executed next. At the same time, the tool offset data is
   also changed. In this program offset data at address #2 is called since "G41 D2" and "G56 H2"
   are programmed while the programmed tool is used for cutting. However, after the replacement
   with the spare tool, the tool offset data stored at the address identical to the tool number of the
   spare tool will be called. An alarm is triggered if the tool number of the spare tool exceeds 1000.

   Command                     Spare Tool
                                     

                           T2               T32

   G56 H2   Offset data at address H2 and D2 are Offset data at address H32 and D32
   G41 D2
            called.            are called.
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-177
                                                         SECTION 21 TOOL LIFE MANAGEMENT FUNCTION

How to integrate travel

The following conditions are used to integrate travel for a tool whose travel control has been set.

   It is operating by the machining program and not in the machine lock state.

   It is operating linear interpolation, circular interpolation, or NURBS curve interpolation.

      It is operating linear interpolation and cut-in by the cycle axis in the fixed cycle.
      In the case of deep hole synchronized tapping, travel when returning is also integrated.

   Travel of linear axis and rotary axis for cylinder side machining function are integrated.

21-4-2.Tool Life Management by Count Data of Machining Cycles

N1 TLFON

N2 T2     (T2: End mill)

N3 M6

N4 T7     (T7: Boring bar)

N5 G0 X20 Y80 S300

N6 G56 Z-40 HA M3

N7 G41 G1 X40 F200 DA

N80 Y-40

:

N13 TLCO Q4

   (The numerical value of the Q command (N13: 4, N30: 7) is the unit amount for countdown
   from the initial setting value. It can be set as required. When only the "TLCO" command is
   programmed without the Q command, countdown from the initial setting value is made in
   units of "1". Note that when the "TLCO" command is not programmed, countdown is not
   made even when tool life management is in the LIFE COUNT mode.)

N14 G56 Z10 HA M3

N15 G1 X80 F150

N16 Y-10

N17 X0

:

N30 TLCO Q7

N31 M06

:

 Suppose the initial setting value be "100P", the initial setting value is automatically deducted to

   96P (100P - 4P) by the execution of sequence N13 and then to 89P (96P - 7P) by the execution
   of the sequence N30. The initial setting value is deducted in the same manner and when the
   remaining value becomes "0", then the tool life of that tool is determined to have expired. At the
   next T command calling that tool, its spare tool is automatically selected.

 It is recommended to use the Q data identical to the number of holes or workpieces. This will

   give an idea of approximate tool life.

21-5. Changing Alarm Levels

When a tool life is expired, the NC tries to change the tool with a spare one. However, if the
replaceable tool is not found, the NC generates an alarm. This parameter bit is used to select the
alarm level from B and C.

NC optional parameter bit

No.  bit                   Description

                           Alarm B 2436 is displayed.

60   4

                           Alarm C 3263 is displayed.

SECTION 22 READ/WRITE AND GET/PUT FUNCTIONS
                   (WITH FILE INPUT/OUTPUT FUNCTION)

Through data communications (input: numeric data only, output: numeric or character data) with an external
device using the RS232C interface, the function allows the setting of variables (common, local, and system
variables) by the host computer, output of data to a printer, etc.
The file input/output function reads an existing data file from a sector device (MD1) with a command, writes
arbitrary data (result of calculation, alphabet, etc.) to a file, and creates a new file.
Up to two files can be read and writing is possible to one file.

22-1. System Configuration

            (1) Configuration I

Common variables  GET                     READ  CN0  External device
System variables  PUT  Read area                CN1  Punch
Local variables        Write area WRITE              Printer
I/O variables                                        PC
                                                     etc.

                       NC unit                  CN3

                                                                                                                                6124-E P-181
        SECTION 22 READ/WRITE AND GET/PUT FUNCTIONS (WITH FILE INPUT/OUTPUT FUNCTION)

22-2. Function I

22-2-1.READ Function

            The READ function receives data from an external device through the RS232C interface and stores
            the data to the read area (max. 160 characters).
            Data reading ends by reception of the delimiter code set at the NC optional parameter (READ/
            WRITE GET/PUT).

               Format: READ n

               n: RS232C interface channel number

                   1 CN1:

            [Supplement]

              1) Only a sequence number (NOOO) may be specified before the READ command.
              2) During the execution of the READ command, do not execute the LIST OUTPUT command,

                   the PUNCH command, or other similar commands using the same RS232C interface channel.
              3) If the NC is reset during the execution of the READ command, it is necessary to execute the

                   READ command again to end the data receive processing normally or reset the NC again
                   after the elapse of the time set for the no-response timer ("Preparation Complete Waiting
                   Timer" of NC optional parameter READ/WRITE PUT/GET).
              4) An alarm occurs if the received data exceeds 160 characters.
              5) The READ command and print-out of the gauging data cannot be executed at the same time.
              6) Select the device that corresponds to the above n (RS232C interface channel number) at 1.
                   DEVICE on the optional parameter (READ/WRITE GET/PUT) screen displayed in the
                   PARAMETER mode.

            [Supplement]

              Even though the device has been set at the above optional parameter, the individual READ/
              WRITE command needs designation of RS232C interface channel number to be used.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-182
        SECTION 22 READ/WRITE AND GET/PUT FUNCTIONS (WITH FILE INPUT/OUTPUT FUNCTION)

22-2-2.WRITE Function

            The WRITE function sends the data from the write area (max. 160 characters) to an external device
            through the RS232C interface.

               Format: WRITE n

               n: RS232C interface channel number

                   1 CN1:

            [Supplement]

              1) Only a sequence number (NOOO) may be specified before the WRITE command.
              2) During the execution of the WRITE command, do not execute the LIST OUTPUT command,

                   the PUNCH command, or other similar commands using the same RS232C interface channel.
              3) The data to be sent can include up to 160 characters.
              4) The WRITE command and print-out of the gauging data cannot be executed at the same time.
              5) Select the device that corresponds to the above n (RS232C interface channel number) at 1.

                   DEVICE on the optional parameter (READ/WRITE GET/PUT) screen displayed in the
                   PARAMETER mode.

            [Supplement]

              Besides designation of the channel number that corresponds to the device, it is necessary to
              designate the channel number of RS232C interface that corresponds to the READ or WRITE
              command.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-183
        SECTION 22 READ/WRITE AND GET/PUT FUNCTIONS (WITH FILE INPUT/OUTPUT FUNCTION)

22-2-3.GET Function

            The GET function fetches the numeric data, stored in the read area by the execution of the READ
            command, and sets it to the specified variable.

               Format: GET s, l

               s: Input element

                   For an input element, common variables, system variables, and local variables can be used.

               l: Number of input digits

                   <If input element is designated>
                   Specify the number of digits the numeric data (includes decimal point and sign) or "EMPTY"
                   data to be fetched from the read area in an unsigned integer. (max. 10 digits, 10 digits if
                   omitted)
                   <If input element is not designated>
                   Specify the number of characters to be skipped in an unsigned integer. (max. 160 characters)

            [Supplement]

              1) The get function reads out the number of characters specified by <l> from the codes having
                   been read into the read area by the READ command, starting at the position indicated by the
                   read-out pointer and, after converting the read code into the numeric data, sets the numeric
                   data (ASCII code) to the variable specified by <s>.
                   The read-out pointer is set at the start of the read area when the READ command is executed
                   or the NC is reset, and is moved in the forward direction by the number of specified digits at
                   the execution of the GET command.

              2) If entry of <s> is omitted, the input digit value <l> indicates the number of characters to be
                   skipped.
                   Example: GET,10
                   The function skips 10 characters in the read area.

              3) If all of the read out data are spaces, the numeric data is regarded to be "0".
              4) An alarm occurs if the number of characters designated with the GET command exceeds the

                   number of characters read into the read area by the READ command.
              5) An alarm occurs if there is a format error (more than one decimal point, other than 0 to 9, more

                   than 10 digits) in the numeric data to be read.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-184
        SECTION 22 READ/WRITE AND GET/PUT FUNCTIONS (WITH FILE INPUT/OUTPUT FUNCTION)

22-2-4.PUT Function

            The PUT function stores numeric data or character-string to the write area where the data is output
            to an external device by the WRITE command.

               Format: PUT s, l

               s: Output element

                   For an output element, common variables, system variables, local variables, and character-
                   string enclosed by quotation marks (max. 16 characters; `ABC') can be used.

               l: Number of output digits

                   <If output element is designated>
                   Specify the number of digits the numeric data (includes decimal point and sign) or "EMPTY"
                   data to be output to the write area in an unsigned integer. (max. 10 digits, 10 digits if omitted)
                   <If output element is not designated>
                   Specify the number of space codes to be output in an unsigned integer. (max. 160 characters)

            [Supplement]

              1) The data of the output element specified for <s> is first converted into the real or integer,
                   according to the specified output element type, then to the ASCII code. After that the character
                   code is written to the write area by the number of digits specified for <l>.
                   Conversion of data according to the type of specified output element:

                   If common or local variable is an output variable

                       Converted into real

                   If common or local variable is an input variable

                       Converted into integer

                   If system variable is specified

                       Converted into integer according to the attribute of the variable
              2) If the output element is a character-string enclosed by quotation marks, the character-string is

                   converted into the ASCII code (JIS8 code subset) to be output (refer to 22-7. APPENDIX in
                   this section).
                   Example: PUT `ABCDEFGHI'  ABCDEFGHI

                   Quotation marks enclosing the character-string must be in the same block. Specification of

                       them in multiple blocks is not permitted.

                   A character-string may contain up to 16 characters.
                   When a quotation mark is used in a character-string, enter two quotation marks.

                       Example: PUT '''95'  '95
                       Example: PUT 'I''M'  I'M

                   It is possible to use lower case alphabetic character codes by entering a circumflex accent

                       (^) at the beginning of the character-string enclosed by quotation marks.
                       Example: PUT `^ABCD1234'  abcd1234
              3) Character-string type hexadecimal can be designated using a hexadecimal character-string (0
                   to 9, A to F) and a dollar symbol ($) preceding the character-string.
                   Example: PUT $616263 abc
                    Refer to [22-7. Appendix (Alphabet & Symbol table)]
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-185
        SECTION 22 READ/WRITE AND GET/PUT FUNCTIONS (WITH FILE INPUT/OUTPUT FUNCTION)

22-3. Function II (File Input/Output Function)

22-3-1.FOPENA (FOPENB) Function

            The FOPENA (FOPENB) function opens read-in file A (file B) in a sector device.
            Input the device name and the file name of the read-in file following a command.
            It is possible to open both read-in file A and read-in file B at the same time.

               Format: FOPENA (FOPENB) _(device-name:)file-name.extension

            [Supplement]

              1) Only a sequence number (NOOO) may be specified before the FOPENA (FOPENB)
                   command.

              2) Effective device is MD1 (omissible).
              3) If a device name is omitted, MD1 is assumed.
              4) File name and extension cannot be omitted.
              5) An alarm occurs if a non-existent file is specified.

22-3-2.FWRITC Function

            The FWRITC function opens write-in file C.
            Input the device name and the file name of the write-in file following the command.

               Format: FWRITC _(device-name:)file-name.extension

            [Supplement]

              1) Only a sequence number (NOOO) may be specified before the FWRITC command.
              2) Effective device is MD1 (omissible).
              3) If a device name is omitted, MD1 is assumed.
              4) File name and extension cannot be omitted.
              5) An alarm occurs if an existent file is specified.

           Option function: ";A" Specifies the append mode added to an existing file.

               Format: FWRITC _(device-name:)file-name.extension;A

            [Supplement]

              1) Only a sequence number (NOOO) may be specified before the FWRITC command.
              2) Effective device is MD1 (omissible).
              3) If a device name is omitted, MD1 is assumed.
              4) File name and extension cannot be omitted.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-186
        SECTION 22 READ/WRITE AND GET/PUT FUNCTIONS (WITH FILE INPUT/OUTPUT FUNCTION)

22-3-3.READ Function

            The READ function sends the data from read-in file A or B which has been opened by the execution
            of the FOPENA (B) command to the read area (max. 160 characters) in unit of lines.
            At each execution of the READ command, data is sent by one line.
            In the read area, the data is processed by the GET command explained below.

               Format: READ n

               n: Designation of the file where the data is sent to the read area.

                   A: One line of data is read from read-in file A
                   B: One line of data is read from read-in file B

            [Supplement]

              1) Only a sequence number (NOOO) may be specified before the READ command.
              2) If one line of data which is read from a file exceeds 160 characters, execution of the READ

                   command ends after sending 160 characters of the data. In the next execution of the READ
                   command, the data is sent from the remaining data.
              3) Only one read area exists. The existing data in the read area is destroyed by the execution of
                   the READ command.

22-3-4.WRITE Function

            The WRITE function sends (writes) the data in the write area (max. 160 characters) to the write-in
            file C which is opened by the execution of the FWRITC command.
            A carriage return code ($0A) is appended to the end of the data.
            At each execution of the WRITE command, one line of data is written.
            In the write area, the data is written by the PUT command explained below.

               Format: WRITE C

            [Supplement]

              1) Only a sequence number (NOOO) may be specified before the WRITE command.
              2) Although the write/read area has the capacity to store the data of up to 160 characters, it is

                   recommended to limit the number of characters at 64 characters to give better visibility.
              3) One line means a unit of data delimited by a carriage return code ($0A).
              4) The read (write) pointer is zero cleared at the execution of the READ (WRITE) command.

22-3-5.GET Function

            The GET function fetches the numeric data stored in the read area by the execution of the READ
            command and sets it to the specified variable.
            The function is the same as that explained in Section "22-2-3. GET Function".
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-187
        SECTION 22 READ/WRITE AND GET/PUT FUNCTIONS (WITH FILE INPUT/OUTPUT FUNCTION)

22-3-6.PUT Function

            The PUT function stores the numeric data and character-string to the write area where data is output
            to an external device by the WRITE command.
            The function is the same as that explained in Section "22-2-4. PUT Function". In this case, however,
            the following NOTICE must be taken into consideration.

            [Supplement]

              If a code not shown in "22-7. Appendix (Alphabet & Symbol Table)" or ASCII code table is used, it
              will cause the file to be destroyed, displayed data to be destroyed, and other troubles. Therefore,
              do not use such codes.

22-3-7.CLOSE Function

            The CLOSE function closes files which have been opened by the execution of the FOPENA,
            FOPENB, or FWRITC command.

               Format: CLOSE n

               n: Designation of the file to be closed.

                   A Read-in file A is closed.
                   B Read-in file B is closed.
                   C Read-in file C is closed.

            [Supplement]

              1) Unless the file is closed, it is not possible to open other file using the same command which
                   was used to open the file.

              2) For a write-in file, a file cannot be created unless the CLOSE command is executed.

22-3-8.Supplements

            (1) The gauging data print-out (PRINT command) is not executed during the execution of the
                   functions indicated above.

            (2) The functions indicated above can be executed during return search.

            (3) If the reset key is pressed during the execution any of the functions indicated above, execution
                   of the reset function is suspended until the file access is completed. Accordingly, there will be a
                   delay before the NC is reset.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-188
        SECTION 22 READ/WRITE AND GET/PUT FUNCTIONS (WITH FILE INPUT/OUTPUT FUNCTION)

22-4. GET/PUT of Variables

(1) When using the GET/PUT function for reading or storing system variables that requires units
      such as VZOFX, VTOFH, and VTOFD, ensure that their unit agrees with the unit system used
      in the part program.
      The numeric values are stored in the state of being right-justified.

 In the case where VZOFX [1] = 10.000 mm                         10000
                                                                 1000.0
     GET/PUT in units of 0.001 mm (real number unit: N)          10.000
     GET/PUT in units of 0.01 mm (real number unit: N)           10.000
     GET/PUT when the real number unit is Y
     GET/PUT in units of 1.000 mm (real number unit: arbitrary)

 In the case where VZOFX [1] = 10.0000 inch

GET/PUT in units of 0.0001 inch (real number unit: N)            100000

GET/PUT where real number unit is Y                              10.0000

GET/PUT in units of 1.0000 inch (real number unit: arbitrary) 10.0000

(2) When the common variable VC having an empty value is put, the NC outputs the character-
      string `EMPTY'. When the character-string `EMPTY' is entered into the common variable VC
      by the GET function, the relevant VC becomes empty. The character-string is put in the state
      of being left-adjusted.

(3) If the common variable or local variable data exceeds 8 digits, the data is indicated with
      . These symbols are put in the state of being left-adjusted.

                       SECTION 23 Hi-CUT Pro

SECTION 23 Hi-CUT Pro

23-1. Outline

            The Hi-CUT Pro is a new generation NC function. The Hi-CUT Pro engine is installed in the NC unit
            for a machining center, which realizes efficient part and box-type machining while assuring
            accuracy. The functions of Hi-CUT Pro are as follows.

            (1) The Hi-CUT Pro determines appropriate acceleration/deceleration performance according to
                   the feedrate upper limit set on the PARAMETER screen or the feedrate upper limit designated
                   in the G code block of the Hi-CUT Pro, and thereby makes automatic acceleration/deceleration
                   control suitable for the workpiece shape.

            (2) The machining accuracy is ensured, within the machining tolerance set on the PARAMETER
                   screen or the machining tolerance designated in the G code block of the Hi-CUT Pro during
                   machining.

            In addition, two modes such as "STANDARD" and "HIGH SPEED" are available as the machining
            mode to choose how to determine the acceleration/deceleration performance and they can be
            selected according to the machining purpose.

23-2. Features

            (1) With a machining program, the previous machining program can be used just as it is.

            (2) Quite similar operations sequence return, manual interrupt and so on as those with the
                   standard NC are available.

            (3) The Hi-CUT Pro makes high-speed and high-precision machining easy.

           High-speed machining

            [Designation of the feedrate upper limit only]
            Because the Hi-CUT Pro automatically and optimally controls the cutting feedrate within its upper
            limit range, the best cutting feedrate complying with the machining conditions can be easily
            obtained, substantially reducing the machining time.

           High-precision machining

            [Within the machining tolerance (allowable machining error)]
            It is unnecessary to slightly change the cutting feedrate for a corner or edge while caring about the
            deterioration of accuracy as before. The Hi-CUT Pro keeps the machining error within the preset
            machining tolerance (allowable machining error), ensuring a stable machining accuracy during high-
            speed machining.
                                                                      6124-E P-194
                                                    SECTION 23 Hi-CUT Pro

23-3. Hi-CUT Pro

23-3-1.Operating the Hi-CUT Pro

            (1) To use the Hi-CUT Pro for machining, you must designate the EXECUTION MODE of the Hi-
                   CUT Pro.

            (2) Next, designate the FEEDRATE UPPER LIMIT, MACHINING TOLERANCE and MACHINING
                   MODE according to the machining contents.

            (3) Each of the control parameters such as the EXECUTION MODE of the Hi-CUT Pro can be
                   specified in any of the following two methods:

                   a. Parameter setting

                   b. Programming

            (4) The Hi-CUT Pro takes effect only when the cutting feed per minute is designated. That is, even
                   if the EXECUTION MODE of the Hi-CUT Pro is designated, the Hi-CUT Pro does not function in
                   respect with the rapid feed command and the cutting feed per revolution command.

            (5) The Hi-CUT Pro controls three basic rectilinear axes (XYZ) which are orthogonal. Therefore it
                   does not function in respect with other additional rectilinear axes (UVW) or rotary axes. (The
                   feedrate control by Hi-CUT Pro is automatically turned off.)

23-3-2.Explanation of Hi-CUT Pro Control Parameter

When you want to change the "Hi-CUT Pro CONTROL PARAMETER" during operation, refer to
Supplement in "How to Designate Using Hi-CUT Pro Control Parameter Setting" in 23-3-4.

(1) Designation of the EXECUTION MODE [Designation of execution of the Hi-CUT Pro]
      Designate execution or no execution of the Hi-CUT Pro.
      Each mode is as shown in the table below.

Parameter setting  Program G command
 CONTROL OFF                  G130
  CONTROL ON                  G131

(2) FEEDRATE UPPER LIMIT [Designation of cutting feedrate upper limit value]

a. Designate the largest value of cutting feedrate for machining. The cutting feedrate is
      restricted to below this upper limit value.
      FEEDRATE UPPER LIMIT = Max. value of cutting feedrate > 0

b. With the optimum control using this feedrate upper limit, the highest and the most stable
      machining accuracy is ensured without any relationship to the machining contents.

c. When changing the max. value of the cutting feedrate command following the modified
      machining contents, re-set this feedrate upper limit as is necessary.
                                                                            6124-E P-195
                                                          SECTION 23 Hi-CUT Pro

(3) MACHINING TOLERANCE [Designation of allowable machining error]

a. Designate up to how many degrees the machining error is allowed to be. (This is called
      "Allowable machining error".)
      Machining tolerance = Allowable machining error  0
      In accordance with this formula, control is made so that the actual machining error is within
      the allowable machining error.

b. Because the Hi-CUT Pro automatically controls the cutting feedrate based on this
      tolerance, make the machining tolerance as large as possible within the allowable range for
      the purpose of high-speed machining.

c. If you narrow the machining tolerance, the machining error will be reduced. However, the
      cutting feedrate lowers, resulting in prolonged machining time.

(4) MACHINING MODE [Designation of the balance between accuracy and speed]
      Designate whether or not importance is placed on the machining efficiency during machining.
      Generally, for example, the rough machining process is designed for "HIGH-SPEED" and the
      finish process for "STANDARD". (Do not always use as shown by this example, and designate
      freely according to the purpose of the workpieces or the machining status.)

                                                                       Order

Setting            Description                            Accuracy     Machining
                                                                       efficiency

STANDARD Mode with which accuracy and efficiency are                1         2

[1]      balanced

  HIGH- Mode with which importance is placed on the                 2         1
SPEED [2] machining efficiency

With the Hi-CUT Pro control parameters, 2 sets of independent internal data such as
STANDARD and HIGH-SPEED are prepared. When executing Hi-CUT Pro control, one set of
control parameters among these is used by designating the MACHINING MODE.

MACHINING          One of two kinds of internal parameter sets
    MODE           is selected according to the machining
                   mode.

     STANDARD      Internal parameter set with which accuracy
                   and efficiency are balanced

HIGH-SPEED         Internal parameter set with which
                   importance is placed on the machining
                   efficiency

                                ME32128R0302500040001

Fig.23-1 Relationship between machining mode values and parameter values used for the Hi-CUT
                                                           Pro control

      settable:

        Designation of EXECUTION MODE

        FEEDRATE UPPER LIMIT

        MACHINING TOLERANCE

        Designation of MACHINING MODE

              When program operation is running, refer to the after-mentioned Supplement.

                                                                                                SECTION 23 Hi-CUT Pro
(5) The setting unit system conforms to the one actually set on the NC.

                                                                                                                                                                                           ME32128R0302500060002

[Supplement]
 When the "Hi-CUT Pro CONTROL PARAMETER" has been changed during program operation,
 the control does not reflect it in such a way. You must press [F4] (PARA. UPDATE).
 While the parameter is updating, the following parameter updating display lamp is lit.
     PARAMETER UPDATING

                                                              6124-E P-198
                                            SECTION 23 Hi-CUT Pro

How to Designate Using Program Command

(1) When you want to use the Hi-CUT Pro control in an arbitrary portion on the machining program,
      or you want to change the Hi-CUT Pro control parameter in each portion of the machining
      program, it is convenient to designate the Hi-CUT Pro control using the program.

(2) To program the Hi-CUT Pro control designation, insert the following command into the
      machining program.

CONTROL G131[F__] [E__] [J__]

ON                Individual block command

                  For details about each address character (and its data word), refer to "23-

                    3-2. Explanation of Hi-CUT Pro Control Parameter" and "23-3-3. Control
                    Parameter List".

CONTROL G130

OFF               Individual block command

(3) G131 and G130 commands are common to those of Super-NURBS. However, the address
      characters like D, I, L, R, which can be designated in the Super-NURBS, cannot be designated.
      With the Hi-CUT Pro, "4295 Alarm D G131/130 command: unusable address character" is
      issued to ignore the command values.

(4) In the programmed "Hi-CUT Pro control off" (G130) status, "EXECUTION MODE" of the
      parameter-designated Hi-CUT Pro control is followed. For this reason, if you want to program
      the control ON/OFF, select "CONTROL OFF" for "EXECUTION MODE". Note that the initial
      value after the NC has reset is G130 status.

(5) The designation order of each address characters (and its data word) does not matter.

(6) When the "Hi-CUT Pro CONTROL ON" (G131) is programmed, the control parameter
      equivalent to the programmed address character (and its data word) validates the programmed
      value, making the control parameter value equivalent to the parameter setting invalid.

(7) Each address character (and its data word) can be omitted. When omitted, the parameter
      setting is adopted.

(8) Because the F designation of the G131 command block is not destined for the cutting feedrate,
      designate the actual cutting feedrate in other than the G131 command block.

Program example

                      Feedrate upper limit: 10000, machining
                      tolerance: 0.02 are designated.
                      (Parameter-set value is adopted for the
                      omitted value.)
                 In this area, the Hi-CUT Pro is executed.

                       CONTROL OFF is designated.

                SECTION 23 Hi-CUT Pro

Status Display

message also appears at the lower right of the actual position screen 4/5 (Batch display screen).

                                                                                                                                                                                           ME32128R0302500080001

Message showing the machining mode

When the Hi-CUT Pro is being executed, the machining mode is displayed at the right of the
message showing that the control is being executed. The displayed messages are as follows:
[Display kinds]

   Hi-CUT STANDARD

   Hi-CUT HIGH SPEED
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-200
                                                                                                SECTION 23 Hi-CUT Pro

Message during feedrate change

[Display]
Hi-CUT speed change (code No.)
When the feedrate command value of the machining program is beyond the feedrate upper limit
command value of the control parameter, and the actual feedrate is restricted to the feedrate upper
limit value, the message showing the above machining mode is replaced with "speed change (code
No.)." (The speed limit finishes, and after a while the display returns to the original machining mode.)
The details of code Nos. are as shown in the table below.

Code No.                               Details

1         Due to a large amount of data, the feedrate is largely limited so that the balance

          between operation capacity and feedrate is properly maintained.

2         Because the feedrate command value of the machining program is beyond the

          feedrate upper limit of the control parameter, the actual feedrate is limited to the

          feedrate upper limit value.

3         The actual feedrate is limited to the clamp speed of the safety speed monitor

          function.

4         Because a feed axis may exceed the max. feedrate, the actual feedrate is

          controlled so that it is limited to within the max. value.

5         Because the data transmission speed is slow when DNC communication or the

          like is used for operation, the actual feedrate is limited to the speed suited for

          the data transmission speed. Because the amount of data is large, the feedrate

          is limited so that the balance between operation capacity and feedrate is

          properly maintained.
                                                                    6124-E P-201
                                                  SECTION 23 Hi-CUT Pro

23-3-5.Supplement

          Change of Control Parameter

The control parameter is referred to in the portion (for program interpretation) where the machining
program is replaced with the type suited to NC control. Because the program interpretation is carried
out at an early stage of pre-read, some delay is caused until the changed values become effective
when the control parameter has been changed.

(1) When the time until the pre-read data is put into practice is fast, that is

        Block length is short

        Feedrate is large

            In such cases, the control parameter value is changed in a relatively short time.

(2) When the time until the pre-read data is put into practice is long, that is

        Block length is long

        Feedrate is small

            In such cases, it may take some time until the control parameter is changed.

                                       parameter

              Running
