         SECTION 23 Hi-CUT Pro

Program

The Hi-CUT Pro has no special limitations with respect to programming, and the previous program
can be run as it is.
In the blocks where the following commands are given, among other blocks, the Hi-CUT Pro control
is automatically turned off and turned on again after the command has been finished.

   Additional axis command (U, V, W, A, B, and C, excluding X, Y, Z)

   Auxiliary function commands such as S, T, M

   Machining command in G95 (feed per revolution) mode

   G00/G60 (positioning/unidirectional positioning) command

   G04 (dwell) command

   G30 (home position positioning) command

   G31 (skip function) command

   Fixed cycle commands such as G80/G73/G74 and so on.

   Area machining function command

      However, the control is turned off only for positioning to the reference point in the plane, and
      turned on for other machining operations.

   G11/G10 (coordinates parallel and rotational shift) command

      However, the control is turned on for machining commands surrounded by G11 and G10.

   G40/G41/G42 (tool diameter compensation on/off) command

      However, when the axis shift command is given in the same block as the block where the
      diameter compensation on/off command is given, the control is turned on. Further, the control is
      turned on for the machining commands surrounded by G41/G42 and G40.

   G50/G51 (figure enlargement and reduction) command

      However, the control is turned on for the machining commands surrounded by G51 and G50.

   G62 (programmable mirror image) command

      However, the control is turned on for machining commands from G62 and later.

   G92 (work coordinate system change) command

      However, the control is turned on for machining commands from G92 and later.

   COPY function command

      However, the control is turned on for machining commands surrounded by COPY and COPYE.

   G68/G69 (slanted surface machining) command

      However, the control is turned on for machining commands surrounded by G69 and G68.

   System variable, input/output variable command
                                              6124-E P-204
                        SECTION 24 Super-NURBS

SECTION 24 Super-NURBS

24-1. Overview

            Super-NURBS, driven by its own engine, is a highly advanced control system that permits
            machining of high-order curves (free-form curves) by providing the following functions:

               Progress Shape Reconstruction Function: Reconstructs and adjusts high-order curves

               Progress Shape Adaptive Control: Controls the feedrate according to the high-order curves

            A part program created for die machining contains many G01 minute feed commands, which are
            approximated into sculptured surfaces. For such a part program, the Super-NURBS does the
            following:

            (1) Under the Progress Shape Reconstruction Function, the Super-NURBS judges the shape from
                   the G01 minute feed commands and reconstructs the original shape using the free-form curves
                   (high-order curves) to obtain the machining shape which is suited for the axis feed.

            (2) Under the Progress Shape Adaptive Control, the Super-NURBS calculates the interpolated
                   points with high accuracy and density on the reconstructed machining shape. At this time, the
                   Super-NURBS optimizes following items:

                    Interpolated points (position) on the high order curves

                    Feedrates at which the tool passes through the interpolated points (positions)

                    Variance of feedrate at which the tool passes through the interpolated points (positions)

                         (acceleration)

                    Variance of acceleration when the tool passes through the interpolated points (positions)

                         (jerk)

            Since Super-NURBS performs adaptive control of feedrate and minimizes not only acceleration but
            also jerk (acceleration change rate), the axes move smoothly without vibration even when they are
            accelerated or decelerated.
            With these controls, Super-NURBS improves the machined accuracy and quality as well as
            reducing the machining time.
                                                                                     6124-E P-205
                                                               SECTION 24 Super-NURBS

24-2. Features

   The conventional part programs with G01 linear approximation can be used without change.

   The NC operations such as sequence restart and manual interruption are the same with those

      of the standard NC.

   The Super-NURBS allows high-speed, high-precision and high-quality machining with ease.

High speed machining: Simply specify the feedrate upper limit.
The Super-NURBS automatically controls a cutting feedrate to be optimal without exceeding the
feedrate upper limit and the specified feedrate. So, the machining is done at the cutting feedrate
best suited for the machining conditions and the machining time is reduced drastically.

High precision machining: Errors are always kept within machining tolerance.
Frequent adjustment of cutting feedrate is no longer necessary. Such adjustment was needed on
the standard NC to prevent deterioration of precision and quality at corners and edges. The Super-
NURBS maintains machining errors at sculptured surface and edges within the preset machining
tolerance (allowable error). Thus, stable machining accuracy is guaranteed at low-speed and high-
speed machining.

High quality machining: Smooth paths and curved surface are reconstructed based on program
tolerance.
The Super-NURBS automatically reconstructs machining shapes approximated with straight lines in
the previous part programs and creates the original curved shape for smooth feed based on the
program tolerance. This enhances the quality of machined surfaces.

Progress Shape Reconstruction Function    Progress Shape Adaptive Control
     Enhances the surface quality by           Optimizes the feedrate and

reconstructing and adjusting the shapes.    guarantees machining accuracy.

Program tolerance                         Machining tolerance

                                                               ME32128R0302600020001

Fig.24-1 Progress Shape Reconstruction Function and Progress Shape Adaptive Control
                                         6124-E P-206
                   SECTION 24 Super-NURBS

24-3. Super-NURBS

24-3-1.Operation Method

            (1) Select the EXECUTION MODE to activate the Super-NURBS functions.

            (2) Specify the FEEDRATE UPPER LIMIT and the MACHINING MODE according to your
                   machining requirements.

            (3) To make detailed settings according to the machining details and NC data to be used, set data
                   at the following parameters.

                    MACHINING TOLERANCE

                    PROGRAM TOLERANCE

                    UTILIZE RECONST. SHAPE

                    (Shape reconstruction) MAX BLOCK LENGTH

                    (Shape reconstruction) MIN BLOCK LENGTH

                    PROGRAM FILTER MODE

                    FILTER VALUE: LENGTH

                    FILTER VALUE: ANGLE

            (4) The individual control parameters such as the EXECUTION MODE can be specified in either of
                   the following two methods.
                   For parameter setting, there are three groups of data (HIGH QUALITY, STANDARD, and HIGH
                   SPEED). In execution of Super-NURBS, the control parameter values for a group specified in
                   the EXECUTION MODE become effective.

                    Specify by parameter setting

                    Specify by program commands

            (5) The Super-NURBS is valid only when the feed-per-minute or inverse-time-feed is selected as
                   the cutting feed command. This means, the Super-NURBS automatically identifies the rapid
                   traverse commands and feed-per-revolution commands, and does not control them even when
                   the EXECUTION MODE is selected.

            (6) The Super-NURBS controls only the basic three axes (X, Y, Z) perpendicular to each other, and
                   does not control the other additional linear axes (U, V, W) or rotary axes (A, B, C). (Super-
                   NURBS control is automatically turned OFF in the block including the invalid axis control
                   command.) If Super-NURBS for rotary axes is selected, the rotary axes besides the indexed
                   one are also controlled.
                                                                                 6124-E P-207
                                                           SECTION 24 Super-NURBS

24-3-2.Control Parameters

If you want to change the control parameters during program operation, read the Supplement
described in "Designation by Parameter Setting" of 24-3-4.

EXECUTION MODE

This parameter specifies whether to execute the Super-NURBS (CONTROL ON) or not (CONTROL
OFF).
Relation between parameters and G commands are as follows:

                Parameter setting       Programmed G command
                 CONTROL OFF                          G130
                  CONTROL ON                          G131

FEEDRATE UPPER LIMIT

   Specify the maximum cutting feedrate used in machining. The cutting feedrate is kept at or

      under this upper limit.
      FEEDRATE UPPER LIMIT = Maximum cutting feedrate > 0

   Since the feedrate is optimally controlled with this FEEDRATE UPPER LIMIT, the highest

      feedrate and stable machining precision are guaranteed regardless of the machining contents.

   It is recommended to re-set this FEEDRATE UPPER LIMIT each time you change the

      maximum cutting feedrate commands according to change in machining contents.

MACHINING MODE

Specify whether the machining needs more surface quality or more efficiency. In general, specify
"HIGH QUALITY" for finishing and "STANDARD" for semi-finishing. (You do not have to follow this
example strictly and specify freely considering how the workpiece is used or machining conditions.)

Machining mode                                                      Rank
        value
                           Description                     Surface  Machining
                                                           quality  efficiency

HIGH QUALITY Machining mode aiming at higher surface       1              3

[0]             quality

STANDARD [1] Machining mode aiming at medium surface       2              2
                        quality and efficiency

HIGH SPEED [2] Machining mode aiming at higher efficiency  3              1

 Super-NURBS selects one set of parameters from the three independent parameter sets; HIGH

   QUALITY, STANDARD, and HIGH SPEED.
   Each parameter set includes the control parameters such as MACHINING TOLERANCE and
   the UTILIZE RECONST. SHAPE, which are explained later. When executing each of these
   functions, Super-NURBS selects and uses the control parameters in the parameter set that
   corresponds to the specified machining mode.

 All the control parameters in each set are given initial values. However, change them to more

   suitable values before machining, considering how to use the machining mode.
                                                                    6124-E P-208
                                              SECTION 24 Super-NURBS

With all these preparations, all the control parameters can be set to proper values by simply
designating the machining mode.

Machining mode       HIGH QUALITY  STANDARD   HIGH SPEED
                             (0)         (1)         (2)

MACHINING TOLERANCE

PROGRAM TOLERANCE
UTILIZE RECONST. SHAPE

   MAX BLOCK LENGTH
   MIN BLOCK LENGTH

                                                                                                                                                                                                                      ME32128R0302600070001

                 Fig.24-2 Machining Mode Numbers and Corresponding Control Parameters

MACHINING TOLERANCE

   Specify how much machining error you permit for machining (hereafter referred to as allowable

      machining error).
      MACHINING TOLERANCE = Allowable machining error  0
      With this setting, the actual machining errors are held within the allowable machining error.

   The Progress Shape Adaptive Control adjusts the cutting feedrate automatically based on this

      MACHINING TOLERANCE. Therefore, if you want to perform high speed machining, specify
      the largest possible tolerance within the allowable range.

   If you narrow the machining tolerance, the machining error will be reduced. However, the

      cutting feedrate lowers, resulting in prolonged machining time.

PROGRAM TOLERANCE

To create the part programs for cutting sculptured surface, set the error amount allowed for
approximation (program tolerance). Based on this program tolerance, the Progress Shape
Reconstruction Function reconstructs a sculptured surface into the shape formed before linear
approximation, and uses the tolerance for axis control.
Program tolerance = Allowable error for approximation used for creating part program  0

   It is advisable to set the allowable error to about a half of machining tolerance.

   It is advisable to set a smaller value as the program tolerance than the value set as the

      machining tolerance.
                                                    6124-E P-209
                              SECTION 24 Super-NURBS

UTILIZE RECONST. SHAPE

 The linear approximation method varies with the CAM functions used for creating part

   programs. Even when the same CAM is used, the linear approximation method varies with
   workpiece size or how to make the tool paths. The approximation method also influenced by
   CAM operator's know-how.
   In spite of such variation in part programs, you can make effective use of the Super-NURBS by
   selecting a utilization rate of the reconstructed shape according to the part program to be used
   or machining content.

 The utilization rate is classified into the four modes as shown in the table below. Select any of

   them according to the state of command position in the part program (such as specified position
   accuracy and command pitch). When a proper rate is selected, the Super-NURBS recognizes
   the shape correctly and performs optimum control.

Utilization specified by  Utilization specified by
   parameter setting        program command

              Full                       I3

             High                        I2

Medium                    I1

Low                       I0

 The Progress Shape Adaptive Control conducts position control, feedrate control, and

   acceleration control and jerk control (variance of acceleration) based on the result of the
   Progress Shape Reconstruction Function.
   UTILIZE RECONST. SHAPE is used to specify to what degree the reconstruction result is used.
                                                                              6124-E P-210
                                                        SECTION 24 Super-NURBS

 The Progress Shape Reconstruction Function achieves 100% of its capability when proper

   values are set at the following parameters.
   Even if the program tolerance used for program creation or the block length (for shape
   reconstruction) are not clearly known, they can be adjusted to enable optimum control by using
   the utilization rate of reconstructed shape.

a. PROGRAM TOLERANCE

b. [MAX / MIN] BLOCK LENGTH (for shape reconstruction)

c. FILTER VALUE [LENGTH / ANGLE]

Utilization                                Application guideline
   FULL
             Suitable for machining where high precision is required at points
   HIGH      specified by G01, and there are many gently curbed surfaces. (For
             example, relatively large workpiece mostly consisting of gentle
             sculptured surfaces.)

             Suitable for machining where high precision is required at points
             specified by G01, and there are many complicated curved surfaces.
             (For example, a workpiece consisting chiefly of complicated curved
             surfaces or small workpiece.)

MEDIUM        Select this mode when precision at a point specified by the G01

                or the program tolerance are not clear.

              Select this mode when reciprocating movements create gaps in

                height with UTILIZE RECONST. SHAPE set at HIGH.

LOW           Select this mode when program tolerance is large or extremely

                small. In the latter case, travels between command points in the
                part program are very short, the tool fluctuates despite of short
                travel, and the axis feed is unstable.

              Suitable for machining where travels between command points

                are generally long.

(Shape reconstruction) MAX BLOCK LENGTH

A block length or a distance between commands for a part whose original shape is a sculptured
surface becomes relatively shorter and the block length for a part whose original shape is a line (a
plane) becomes longer in a part program with linear approximation. Thus, assuming a block whose
block length is longer than a certain value as a line will approximate its original shape better. The
Progress Shape Reconstruction Function does not reconstruct blocks whose block lengths are
longer than this parameter value and process them as lines.

[Supplement]

 Equation related with the block length: L2  4E (2R - E)
 Here, R is the curvature radius of a shape, L is a block length, and E is the program tolerance.
                                                               6124-E P-211
                                         SECTION 24 Super-NURBS

(Shape reconstruction) MIN BLOCK LENGTH

If the command intervals in the part program (block length) are very small, they are considered to
represent their original shapes sufficiently and they do not need shape reconstruction. The shape
reconstruction control does not reconstruct shapes for blocks whose block lengths are shorter than
this value.
This function is effective for the Shape Reconstruction Control when minimal blocks continue
successively.

(Example)
If the minimum curvature radius of a shape (R) is 5.000 [mm] and program tolerance (E) is 0.010
[mm], the block length (L) will be 0.632 [mm] when calculated with the above formula.
About a half of this block length 0.300 [mm] is a standard set value.

PROGRAM FILTER MODE

The program filter function compensates the command positions in a part program within the
specified tolerance when machining is performed by approximating the curved surface with minute
blocks.

MODE 1  Activates the function for removing singular minute steps in
MODE 2  the part program.
        (This function is effective in removing minute steps which may be
        generated when connecting curved surfaces by CAD).

        Activates the function for reducing the fluctuation of command
        positions (convexes and concaves).
        (This function is useful when the fluctuation between the command
        positions is large compared with the moving distance between
        command positions.)

When both modes are selected it functions in the order of MODE1 and then MODE 2.
The following table shows correspondence between parameters and commands for selecting in filter
mode.

        Filter mode selected by  Filter mode selected by
                 parameter          program command
                     OFF                        K0
                MODE 1 ON                       K1
                MODE 2 ON                       K2
                                                K3
             MODE 1 + 2 ON

                                                                                               SECTION 24 Super-NURBS

  (Program Filter) FILTER VALUE: LENGTH, (Program Filter) FILTER VALUE:
  ANGLE

  Set the required control parameters for the selected program filter mode by referring to the
  explanation described below.
  Unnecessary parameters are ignored even when they are set.

  MODE 1

  Based on the following parameters, the system eliminates the singular minute steps in the part
  program approximated with straight lines. So the tool does not trace the eliminated points.

      FILTER VALUE: LENGTH

        Specify the maximum block length to be removed from the straight lines forming a minute step
        between the command points.

      FILTER VALUE: ANGLE

        Specify the maximum crossing angle formed by the blocks before and after the eliminated
        singular minute step.

        <<Example>>
        FILTER VALUE: LENGTH 0.020 [mm]
        FILTER VALUE: ANGLE 5.0000 [degree]

MODE 2

Based on the following parameters, the system moves the machining start and end points and
command positions forming non-square lines in the part program within the specified distance to
reduce the fluctuation (convexes and concaves).

 PROGRAM TOLERANCE (p)

   Specify the maximum travel distance of command points to be reconstructed.

 (Shape construction) MAX BLOCK LENGTH (L)

   If the distance from a certain point to the next or previous command point is larger than this
   value, the system considers that this is a straight line and does not carry out reconstruction.

 FILTER VALUE: ANGLE ()

   If an angle formed two lines between two command points is larger than this parameter, the
   system regard it as an angle and does not reconstruct the shape.

Designation by Program Commands

(1) Super-NURBS control specification by program commands is convenient when you want to
      execute the Super-NURBS control at arbitrary parts in a part program or to change the Super-
      NURBS control parameters at individual parts in a part program.

(2) Insert the following commands into a part program to specify the Super-NURBS control with
      program commands.

            G131 [F_][J_][E_][D_][I_][L_][R_][K_][P_][Q_]

CONTROL ON   Single block command

             Refer to "24-3-2. Control Parameters" and "24-3-3. Control Parameter

               List" for more information on individual address characters (and their
               data words)

                        G130
CONTROL OFF

                         Single block command

(3) When a program command specifies the "CONTROL OFF" (G130) state, the Super-NURBS
      control follows the parameter settings of the "EXECUTION MODE". So if you want to turn on
      and off the control with the program commands, set the "EXECUTION MODE" to "CONTROL
      OFF". After NC reset, G130 is automatically selected by default.

(4) You can specify individual address characters (and their data words) in an arbitrary order.

(5) When the "CONTROL ON" (G131) is specified as a program command, values in a program
      become effective for control parameters corresponding to address characters (and their data
      word) specified in the program and corresponding control parameter values by the parameter
      setting become ineffective.

Message Showing that Feedrate is Being Replaced

[Message]

   F-LIMIT (Code No.)

When the actual feedrate is equivalent to the feedrate upper limit because the feedrate specified by
the part program exceeds the upper limit set by the control parameter, the screen shows the "F-
LIMIT (code No.)" instead of the message that indicates the above machining mode.

Code No.                                      Description
     1
     2    Super-NURBS greatly limits the feedrate when data amount is
     3    large so that the calculation speed can catch up with the feedrate.
     4
          The actual feedrate is limited to the feedrate upper limit because
     5    the feedrate specified by the part program exceeds the upper limit
          set by the control parameter.

          The actual feedrate is limited to the speed fixed by the safety
          speed monitor function.

          Since there is an axis which may exceed the maximum feedrate,
          the actual feedrate is limited to keep the axis speed within the
          maximum feedrate.

          In the operation using DNC communication, data transfer speed is
          so slow that actual feedrate is limited to the speed that meets the
          data transfer speed. Since data amount is large, Super-NURBS
          greatly limits the feedrate so that the calculation speed can catch
          up with the feedrate.

24-3-5.Supplements

          Changing Control Parameters

Some control parameters are referred in a section (program interpreter) where a part program is
converted into a form proper for the NC control. Since the program is interpreted on an early stage
of reading a program ahead, the changed value becomes valid after a certain amount of delay.
(1) If the pre-read data is quickly executed, the control parameter value will change shortly.

      <<Example>>

        A block length is short.

        A feedrate is high.

(2) If the execution of pre-read data takes time, there may be a delay in the change of control
      parameter value.

      <<Example>>

        A block length is long.

        A feedrate is low.

                                     6124-E P-223
               SECTION 24 Super-NURBS

About Program

The Super-NURBS does not impose any constraints on program commands, and therefore,
conventional part programs can be executed without any alterations.
The Super-NURBS control is turned off automatically in a block where the following commands are
specified and is turned on automatically after the commands have been completed.

   Additional axis commands other than X, Y, Z (If Super-NURBS for rotary axes is selected, the

      rotary axes besides the indexed one are also controlled.)

   Auxiliary function commands such as S, T, and M

   Machining commands in G95 (feed per revolution) mode

   G00/G60 (positioning/uni-directional positioning) commands

   G04 (dwell) command

   G30 (home positioning) command

   G31 (skip function) command

   Fixed cycle commands such as G80/G73/G74

   Area machining commands

      Note that the control is turned off for a positioning to a reference point in a plane and the control
      is turned on for the other machining.

   G11/G10 (coordinate translation/rotation) commands

      Note that the control is turned on for machining commands enclosed between a G11 and a
      G10.

   G40/G41/G42 (cutter radius compensation ON/OFF) commands

      Note that the control is turned on if there are axis movement commands in a control block
      containing radius compensation ON/OFF commands. Also, the control is turned on for
      machining commands enclosed between a G41/G42 and a G40.

   G50/G51 (enlarging/reducing geometry) commands

      Note that the control is turned on for machining commands enclosed between a G51 and a
      G50.

   G62 (programmable mirror image) command

      Note that the control is turned on for machining commands after a G62.

   G92 (change work coordinate system) command

      Note that the control is turned on for machining commands after a G92.

   COPY function command

      Note that the control is turned on for machining commands enclosed between a COPY and a
      COPYE.

   G68/G69 (slope machining) commands

      Note that the control is turned on for machining commands enclosed between a G68 and a
      G69.

   System variables, input/output variable commands
                                                     6124-E P-224
                               SECTION 24 Super-NURBS

Rotary Axis Control

To do machining with a rotary axis simultaneously controlled in the case of a model equipped with a
rotary axis, execute an unclamp command in advance. The M codes for clamp and unclamp
commands are shown below.

    Axis     Clamp    Unclamp

Axis A    M10        M11
Axis B    M20        M21
Axis C    M26        M27

The M code for a clamp or unclamp command may be made invalid. In this case, make the code
valid, and execute the command.

M/C user parameter No. 18 "Additional axis" screen for parameter setting

                     Item                                                 OFF            ON

Make the M codes for clamping and unclamping the A-axis (rotary axis) valid. Invalid  Valid
Make the M codes for clamping and unclamping the B-axis (rotary axis) valid. Invalid  Valid
Make the M codes for clamping and unclamping the C-axis (rotary axis) valid. Invalid  Valid

[Supplement]

 1) The M codes for clamp and unclamp commands are not for Super-NURBS control. For this
      reason, set a command before the start of Super-NURBS control. If it is set in the middle of
      control, the control will be discontinued, resulting in the possibility of an impact on machined
      faces.

 2) If a rotary axis command is executed on the Super-NURBS without unclamping, machining
      may be discontinued because mechanical unclamping is not completed before the execution
      of the command.

 3) When a rotary axis is used for indexing and machining is not performed with the linear axis
      and the rotary axis simultaneously controlled, perform machining with the rotary axis clamped.
      During unclamping, the rotary axis is not mechanically clamped and its position is retained by
      the control of the servomotor. This state may put the servomotor under an excessive load and
      cause its temperature to increase, exerting an impact on machining accuracy.
                                                                               6124-E P-225
                                                   SECTION 25 NURBS Command

SECTION 25 NURBS Command

25-1. Overview

This "NURBS command" function directly specifies high-order free curves in the OSP where
conventional linear commands and circular commands have been available.
The NURBS command in machining programs has the following advantages over the G01
microlinear approximation for machining including large number of free curves as well as machining
for press dies and plastic dies.

 Machining shapes can be specified without approximation errors.

 The size of machining programs can be reduced.

 The transfer of high feedrate programs between the host to the OSP is eliminated.

 Entire smooth shapes can be specified to eliminate unnecessary deceleration to achieve

   machining at higher feedrate as a result.

In addition, most of conventional functions such as the sequence restart and the manual interrupt
are available in operation with NURBS programs.

CAM             Machining program  OSP Super-NURBS

Approximated      G01 linear            Shape
     output     approximation      Reconstruction

                                       Function

                                                                  Machine
                                                                    tools

   NURBS        NURBS curves                      Shape
format output                                    Adaptive
                                                  Control

                                                                  ME32128R0302700010001

                Fig.25-1 NURBS command structure

The OSP "Super-NURBS" is provided with the "Shape Reconstruction Function" reconstructing a
curve internally close to its original shape from linear command. Thus, a conventional program
approximating a curve with G01 linear commands achieves almost the same result as with a
program with NURBS commands. However, a complete original shape may not be reconstructed
because positions for G01 have already been approximated. Also, the program size stays as large
as before and the managing a program remains difficult. These problems can be solved by using
NURBS commands.

As indicated in the figure above, since the "Shape Adaptive Control" is applied to NURBS
commands automatically, set the "Super-NURBS" EXECUTION MODE to "CONTROL ON" before
executing NURBS commands.
                                                                       6124-E P-226
                                           SECTION 25 NURBS Command

25-2. Terminology

The NURBS command specifies an operation for multiple blocks, and a block is shared for multiple
operations, which is different from conventional NC commands. The details on the NURBS
command format will be described in "3. NURBS Command Format". This section describes the
terms concerning the NURBS command.

Correspondence between actual commands and terms

G132: NURBS mode specification, X, Y, Z: Control point, P: Rank, Q: Knot, R: Weight

G00 X 0.000 Y 0.000 Z10.000 S6000 M03

G01                Z 0.000 F6000

G132 Q0.0 X 0.000 Y 0.000 Z 0.000 R1.0 P4

     Q0.0 X 0.000 Y10.000 Z 0.000 R1.0

     Q0.0 X10.000 Y10.000 Z 0.000 R1.0

     Q0.0 X10.000 Y 0.000 Z 0.000 R1.0     NURBS block
                                           NURBS segment
     Q1.0 X20.000 Y 0.000 Z 0.000 R1.0     NURBS section
                                           NURBS mode
     Q2.0 X20.000 Y10.000 Z 0.000 R1.0
                                           General command
     Q3.0 X30.000 Y10.000 Z 0.000 R1.0     invalidating block

     Q4.0 X30.000 Y 0.000 Z 0.000 R1.0

     Q5.0

     Q5.0

     Q5.0

     Q5.0

     Q0.0 X40.000 Y 0.000 Z40.000 R1.0

     Q0.0 X40.000 Y 0.000 Z 0.000 R1.0

     Q0.0 X00.000 Y 0.000 Z 0.000 R1.0

     Q3.0

M02

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        ME32128R0302700020001

NURBS

NURBS stands for Non Uniform Rational B-Spline curve. With the NURBS command, you can
specify high-order free curves.

NURBS curve

A free curve defined by a NURBS command.

Rank

Though rank can be referred as "order", "rank" will be used in the rest of this document. (Rank-1) is
the degree of a NURBS curve.

Control point

Indicates the basic frame position of a NURBS curve. Note that control points do not normally exist
on a NURBS curve.
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-227
                                                                                      SECTION 25 NURBS Command

Weight

Indicates the level of a weight applied to a control point. A control point in a block with a larger
weight attracts a NURBS curve closer.

Knot

An increment of a knot indicates the degree of a change such as the curvature of a NURBS curve.
The smaller the incremental variation is, the greater the degree of change of the NURBS curve
within a corresponding section becomes.

NURBS block

A current NC block, which is specially noted as a NURBS block for the NURBS command.

NURBS segment

A minimum unit constituting a NURBS curve. A segment is usually composed with NURBS blocks
with the number of twice as many as the rank.

General command invalidating block

A NURBS block where a general command such as an F command or a sequence name is invalid.
General command invalidating blocks are NURBS blocks following the first NURBS block in the last
NURBS segment of a NURBS section.

NURBS section

Constituted with a single or multiple consecutive NURBS segments. A minimum unit of NURBS
command ending with NURBS blocks containing only a knot command.

NURBS mode

A unit of the NURBS command constituted with a single or multiple consecutive NURBS sections.

<Example of NURBS Segment>
In NURBS commands, the minimum unit of operation is a NURBS segment. In the example at the
beginning of this section, the rank P of the first NURBS section is 4, resulting in a constitution with 5
NURBS segments.
The table below indicates what commands in the first NURBS section constitute these five NURBS
segments by underlining.
One NURBS segment is constituted with "rank × 2" of NURBS blocks and it is referred by shifting
one block at a time.
Segment No.                                                               6124-E P-228
                                             SECTION 25 NURBS Command

             Commands constitution NURBS segment

1

2

3

                                       4

                                       5

                                                                                                                                                                               ME32128R0302700020002

The second NURBS section in the aforementioned example has a P of 4, thus it is constituted with 3
NURBS segments.

                                                       SECTION 25 NURBS Command

25-3. NURBS Command Format

This section describes the details of the NURBS command format for the OSP.

NURBS Command Format for OSP

NURBS section is a minimum unit of the NURBS command and is specified in the following format:

                                                       ME32128R0302700030001

Individual address words have the following meanings:

                                                                                                                                                                                              ME32128R0302700030002

[ ] in the above example indicates that the entry may be omitted if it has the same command value
as the preceding blocks. ( ) indicates that the entry may be omitted if it has the same command
value as the default.
{ } indicates that at least one or more address words must be described even if all the address words
therein have the same command values as the preceding blocks or the default values.

NURBS interpolation mode G132

The G132 is a modal G code of G code group No. 1 (referred as Group 1 hereafter). Therefore, the
NURBS mode will not be completed until G code commands of Group 1 other than a G132 (such as
G00, G01, G02, and G03) are executed.
Do not specify a G132 for the second NURBS block or later in the same NURBS section.
In the successive NURBS sections, the G132 can be omitted in the second and later sections.
When the G132 is not omitted, all addresses including ones between { } in the first block can be
omitted if they have the same command values as the preceding blocks or the default values. If that
is the case, the first block may have the G132 only.
When you specify the G132, always place it at the beginning of the block excepting a sequence
name and the block skip "/". Also, for G code commands (such as G00. G01, G02, G03) of the
Group 1 other than G132 specified for transition from the NURBS mode to the non-NURBS mode,
place them at the beginning of a block excepting a sequence name and the block skip command.
This constraint does not apply to G codes of the Group 1 other than G132 when they are not used to
quit the NURBS mode (as before).
When commanding the NURBS mode, expressions (= and variables) cannot be used for the G
code. Always specify as "G132". A mnemonic of "NURBS" may be used instead of "G132". When
using "NURBS", constraints such as the position for specification are the same as specifying as
"G132".
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-230
                                                                                      SECTION 25 NURBS Command

Rank P

The address P specifies the rank (order) of a NURBS curve. Always specify the rank in the first
block of a NURBS section. The effective range of P is 2 to 4. Regardless of ranks of NURBS
commands in preceding blocks, if this address word is omitted, it will be assumed as P4, i.e. the
start of a rank 4 NURBS curve.
The value for the rank should be an integer and an expression can be used as the conventional
integer specification.

Knot Q

Generally the required number of knot is (number of control points + rank). In this format, for control
blocks from the first control point to the last control point, a control point and a knot are specified in
the same block. The rank number of the remaining knots are specified in a block including only an
address Q.
If the knot of the first block of a NURBS section is omitted, this NURBS section will be interpreted as
starting with Q0.0 regardless of the knot values of NURBS commands in the preceding blocks.
However, if the knot value of the second or later blocks is omitted, the knot value will be assumed as
that of the preceding NURBS blocks.
When the number of control points is n, only the knot for the (n+1)th block cannot be omitted and
should be specified even if it is the same as that in the preceding block.
When the rank is P, command value Q[i] (i=1 to the control point number n + rank P) of each knot
should satisfy the following relationship:

Q [i]  Q [i+1] (i=1 to the control point number n + rank P-1)
Q [i] < Q [i+P] (i=1 to the control point number n)

Also, the range of NURBS curve to interpolate is specified as

Q [P]  t  Q [n+1],

where interpolation parameter is t.
And the rank number of Qs at the beginning and at the end should have the same values
respectively.
The first and the last control points of a NURBS section are the start and end points of a NURBS
curve in this section respectively. For command values for knots excepting the rank number of
knots at the beginning and at the end, the same value must not be assigned to more than P of knots
consecutively.
The knot command values Q [i], Q [i+1], . (i=1 to the control point number n + rank P-1) affect the
NURBS command in terms of their differences such as Q [i+1] - Q [i]. Therefore, you should specify
a value close to 0 to each knot command value Q [i] to maintain the data accuracy by preventing
degradations such as canceling.

Control point position XYZ

The position of a control point of the NURBS command is defined with maximum of three axes and
are specified by the addresses X, Y, and Z. The specified rank or more number of control points
more must be specified.
If the rank number or more of successive control points are the same point, there would be no
effective section for interpolation. Avoid such a command when possible.
The control point position value is modal and is handled in the same way as those specified with
addresses X, Y and Z of G code command (G00, G01, G02, G03, etc.) in the other group 1.
However, the equation ("=" and variable) cannot be used.
To specify the control point in the 1st block of the NURBS section, set the same command value as
the end point position specified before the NURBS section.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-231
                                                                                                   SECTION 25 NURBS Command

           Weight R

            A weight will be applied to a control point specified within the same block.
            If a weight is omitted, it will be interpreted as R 1.0 regardless of the weight value of the NURBS
            command specified in preceding blocks.
            For example, if the rank number or more of weight values are 0.0 in succession, the NURBS
            interpolation cannot be executed. Avoid a weight specification of this type.

           Feedrate F

            In the NURBS mode, you should specify the feed by minute (G94). Specify the feedrate by minute
            with address F as before. As the feedrate is a modal value, it can be omitted unless a change is
            made after the preceding block.
            The value of an F in the NURBS mode will affect NURBS segments starting from that NURBS block.
            An F can be specified to any NURBS blocks except for those in a general command invalidating
            block.

           Sequence name N

            A sequence name can be assigned to a NURBS block as before. Always place a sequence name at
            the beginning of a block (except for a block skip specification "/").
            A sequence name in the NURBS mode becomes the name of the NURBS segment starting with that
            NURBS block.
            A sequence name may be assigned to any NURBS block. A return search or a sequence stop will
            not detect a sequence name assigned in a general command invalidating block.

           Other address characters

            Don't specify any address characters other than the aforementioned NURBS commands to NURBS
            blocks. You can only specify the G codes described above.

25-3-1.Supplemental Note

            (1) Numerical Syntax
                   "Knot Q" and "Weight R" are addresses specified in real number. However more digits may be
                   specified than in the conventional real number specification of the OSP. The syntax of these
                   two address values are described below:

                 Real number: Mantissa

                   A character string in decimal notation that starts with a sign + or - followed by a numbers from 0
                   to 9 and a maximum of one decimal point without any other characters (space and alphabetical
                   characters).
                   If no decimal point is included, the position is assumed at the end.
                   If a numeric string between the sign + or - and the decimal point contains only 0's, these 0's
                   may be omitted. In other words, "0." can be expressed as ".". The positive sign + may be
                   omitted.
                   Characters that are actually analyzed and processed are up to 17 digits excluding the signs, the
                   decimal point and preceding 0's before the decimal point. 18th digit and after are rounded
                   assuming them as a positive number excluding preceding 0's.
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-232
                                                                                      SECTION 25 NURBS Command

     Index

      Index cannot be specified. Therefore a number must be expressed with a mantissa only even if
      the number of digits increases.
      The range of real number for actual analysis and process is:
      The range of real number for actual analysis and process is:

           Real number = 0.0
           and 1.0000000000000000x10-63 | real number | 9.9999999999999999X1063
           (|real number| means the absolute value of a real number.)

                                                                                                                                                                                         ME32128R0302700040001

(2) Unit System of Numbers
      The current setting of the program unit system determines the command unit for the control
      point position and the feedrate as for the coordinate commands or the feedrate commands of
      the ordinary control axes.
      Since addresses (P, Q, R) other than these addresses have no unit, a specified value will be
      interpreted as it is.

(3) Handling of Numbers in Incremental Mode
      As the coordinate commands of the ordinary control axes, specified values of the "control point
      position (X, Y, Z)" of NURBS commands can have two types of formats of the absolute
      command (G90) and the incremental command (G91).
      Note that specified values are applied as they are in other addresses (P, Q, R, or F) even in G90
      and G91 commands.
                                                     6124-E P-233
                         SECTION 25 NURBS Command

25-4. Programming

The format of NURBS commands is described in "NURBS Command Format". This section
describes reminders and constraints in terms of the relation between respective NC functions
specified by the program and NURBS commands.

Key points of the constraints on NURBS commands

   No addresses in formats other than prescribed one can be specified.

   Not applicable simultaneously with tool diameter compensation

   Not applicable simultaneously with three-dimensional tool compensation

   Not applicable simultaneously with feed per revolution

   Not applicable simultaneously with the axis name specification

25-4-1.G Code Functions

The state of a modal G code specified before entering the NURBS mode is inherited in the NURBS
mode and the state is also maintained after the NURBS mode is exited.
In the NURBS mode (NURBS block), any G codes cannot be specified except for those in G code
group No. 1 (such as G00, G01, G02, and G03) used for quitting the NURBS mode. If any of them
is specified, an alarm will be generated.

(1) G Code Functions Not Applicable In NURBS Mode
      The following functions cannot be used in the NURBS mode, otherwise alarmed.

        Tool diameter compensation (G41, G42)

        Three-dimensional tool compensation (G44)

        Feed per revolution (G95)

        Axis name specification (G14)

        Cylindrical side machining mode (G175)

(2) Chamfer at Arbitrary Angle (G246, G249)
      Since chamfering applies only to corners between two lines, a block following a chamfering
      command must be either in the G00 or G01 mode. Other mode including the NURBS mode will
      be alarmed.

(3) Fixed Cycle (G73 to G76, G81 to G89, G284 and G274)
      When a program enters into the NURBS mode, if the program is in the fixed cycle (including
      synchronized tapping) mode, the fixed cycle mode will be automatically terminated.

(4) Coordinates System Change, Graphic Operation and Command Correction
      The following functions operate as before when specified:

        Translation or rotation of coordinate

        Work coordinate system selection

        Enlarging and reducing of patterns
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-234
                                                                                                   SECTION 25 NURBS Command

                    Mirror images

                    Tool length compensation

                    Slope machining

                    Manual shift amount cancellation

                    Copy function

                    Attachment swivel compensation

                         Traces may be interrupted if the above function(s) are specified immediately before the
                         NURBS mode, and the interruption is alarmed.

            (5) Exact Stop (G61)
                   An "in-p check" will be conducted automatically at the end of each NURBS segment.

25-4-2. Feed Command

            Alarm will be generated if a NURBS command is attempted during the feed per revolution mode.
            Switch to the feed per minute mode beforehand. The F1 digit feed can be run simultaneously as it
            corresponds to the feed per minute.

25-4-3.STM and Others

            Addresses such as the STM other than those in the prescribed format cannot be specified,
            otherwise alarmed.
                                                              6124-E P-235
                                  SECTION 25 NURBS Command

25-5. Operation of NURBS Program

One special preparation is required before you run a program containing NURBS commands
(referred as NURBS program hereafter). That is to set the "Super-NURBS" EXECUTION MODE to
"CONTROL ON". "25-5-1. Super-NURBS" will describe the detail.
Except for this preparation, the program can be treated as usual as long as you run it in an ordinary
manner
When you press the start button, a program will be executed. Since a NURBS program allows using
the tool length compensation and the work coordinate, be sure to make the tool data and zero
setting ready in advance as needed. The interpolation using the NURBS command is conducted in
the cutting mode. The cutting feed override is available.
There are reminders and constraints specific to the NURBS command in a special start up using a
return search or in some operations during the execution. This section describes these reminders
during machining. Also refer to "25-4. Programming" for more information on the relationship
between the NURBS command and the existing program commands such as G code functions.

Key points of the constraints on the NURBS command

   Turn the EXECUTION MODE of "Super-NURBS" "ON" before running the NURBS command.

   The NURBS command is not available during the MDI operation mode.

   A sequence name in a general command invalidating block in the NURBS section cannot be

      specified as a sequence name for a return search or a sequence stop.

   In a return search to a NURBS block, the return function to the middle of a block cannot be

      used.

   A single block command issued to a NURBS block will result in a stop operation for a NURBS

      segment as a unit.

   An axis command cancellation is invalid.
                                                                   6124-E P-236
                                       SECTION 25 NURBS Command

25-5-1.Super-NURBS

With the NURBS command, the Shape Reconstruction Function conducts machining at a feedrate
appropriate to a shape. Before running the NURBS command, turn on the "Super-NURBS"
EXECUTION MODE in one of the following methods:

   Specify G131 (Super-NURBS execution ON) in a program.

   Select "CONTROL ON" to the "EXECUTION MODE" on the "Super-NURBS CONTROL

      PARAMETER" screen.

Specify appropriate values on the "Super-NURBS CONTROL PARAMETER" screen in advance.
The parameters on "Super-NURBS CONTROL PARAMETER" effective during executing NURBS
commands are represented with a circle in the table below.

FEEDRATE UPPER LIMIT             

MACHINING TOLERANCE              

PROGRAM TOLERANCE                ­

UTILIZE RECONST. SHAPE           ­

MAX BLOCK LENGTH                 ­

MIN BLOCK LENGTH                 ­

PROGRAM FILTER MODE              ­

FILTER VALUE: LENGTH             ­

FILTER VALUE: ANGLE              ­

A program containing NURBS commands often contains G01 commands in general. Therefore, set
appropriate values to the control parameters irrelevant to NURBS commands (marked with "-").
When most sections are machined using NURBS commands, we recommend selecting "HIGH" for
the "UTILIZE RECONST. SHAPE."

25-5-2.NURBS Curve Continuation Check

Like an arc in the G02, NURBS commands determine the shape and position of a curve. Even
when a machining trace is continuous in a program, it may be interrupted if the pattern is mirror-
imaged or enlarged immediately before the program enters the NURBS mode. The command
accuracy may also interrupt a trace.
This function turns the alarm ON if a start point of a NURBS segment and an end point of the
preceding block are separated by the following parameter value.

                        NC optional parameter long word

No  Range and unit of the value                          Function

27  0  99999999 [m]              NURBS curve continuation check value (default)
                                                              4 m
                                                                   6124-E P-237
                                       SECTION 25 NURBS Command

25-5-3.Notices on Operating Functions

This section describes special reminders and constraints for executing the NURBS command.

(1) Operation Mode
      NURBS commands can be specified only for the automatic operation mode.
      NURBS commands cannot be specified for the MDI operation mode, otherwise alarmed.

(2) Limit Check
      The OSP checks travel limits (including soft limits) when interpreting and executing a program.
      Since NURBS commands execute along a curve, only an end point of a NURBS segment is
      checked during interpretation as in G02/G03 commands.
      During program execution, command positions directed to a machine are checked sequentially
      as before.

(3) Feedrate Management
      The cutting feed override can be applied as before. The following operations are also available.
      Since the Super-NURBS is in operation, the feedrate will not increase beyond the "FEEDRATE
      UPPER LIMIT." The actual feedrate changes automatically according to the shape.

        Dry run

        F1 digit feed

        F1 digit feed by parameter

(4) Single Block
      A single block operation for NURBS commands will cause a unit wise operation determined by
      a NURBS segment (single segment).
      The single block switch may be turned ON during the execution of a program. Because the
      Super-NURBS is in operation, the program stops at an end of a NURBS segment closest to the
      execution point after the switch is turned ON.

(5) Mirror Images
      The program commands, the panel switches or both of them operate the NC as before. If you
      turn ON and OFF the switch for the mirror image during the execution of NURBS commands,
      you will be alarmed.

(6) Axis Command Cancellation
      If the axis command cancellation is enabled before or during executing a NURBS command, an
      alarm is generated and the NURBS command becomes non-executable.

(7) Manual Interruption
      You may interrupt a program manually during the execution of NURBS commands as before.
      The method of the interruption maintained.

(8) MDI Interruption
      An MDI interruption is valid in a program containing NURBS commands. If a NURBS block is
      interrupted, commands from the MDI are disabled (because it is in the middle of the NURBS
      execution). An alarm is generated when executing a MDI command.
                                                                           6124-E P-238
                                               SECTION 25 NURBS Command

(9) Return Search and Sequence Restart
      The return search and sequence restart can be used in programs including NURBS commands
      as before. Operating procedure is also maintained. However a NURBS segment is the unit of
      operation.

       Return Search

         The program returns to a NURBS segment where the first block carries a specified
         sequence name. The following shows a case where "NRST" is specified in return search:
         The following shows a case where "NRST" is specified in return search:

               :

      G132X0.141Y0.000Z0.159Q0.000R1.0P4F=VC3

      X1.567Y0.000Z0.567Q0.000

      X2.989Y0.000Z0.987Q0.000

      X5.982Y0.000Z1.933Q0.000

      X7.552Y0.000Z2.464Q4.448

      X10.896Y0.000Z3.782Q4.448

      X15.216Y0.000Z6.234Q9.417

      X16.094Y0.000Z6.889Q15.229

      X17.720Y0.000Z8.265Q15.229   Returns to this segment
      X18.474Y0.000Z8.978Q18.509

      The block counter and the repetition can be specified as before.

       Sequence restart

         A program makes a sequence restart based on the above mentioned return search even in
         the NURBS mode, and then is resumed to operate the rest as before.
         However you cannot use the restart function to the middle of a block. If the NC detects a
         block specified during the return search in the NURBS segment, the NC checks this option
         and generates an alarm.

                                                    6124-E P-240
                        SECTION 25 NURBS Command

25-6. Display Function

No special display screen is provided for the NURBS command. The existing screens keep the
same display in the NURBS mode. Because special displays are made on some screens in the
NURBS mode, this section descries the difference from the conventional display functions.

(1) Program Display
      A NURBS segment consists of multiple block command values. In the "EXECUTED MAIN
      PROGRAM", a block in execution is indicated with "upward arrow" marking. In the NURBS
      mode, the last NURBS block in a NURBS segment under execution is indicated with the arrow.
      If a large number of NURBS segments in sequence have very short transfer distances, the
      "upward arrow" marking is not indicated on rare occasions.
      The "READ MAIN PROGRAM" indicates that block is being read with ">>" mark as before.

(2) Block Data Display
      In the X, Y, Z columns on the BLOCK DATA screen, the end point of a NURBS segment is
      indicated instead of X, Y, and Z values specified as a control point position.
      Except for the last NURBS segment of a NURBS section, a control point position and an end
      point of the NURBS segment do not coincide usually.
      In the NURBS mode, the value of "Rank P" of a NURBS section is indicated on the column Pn
      of the block data screen. Otherwise, 0 is indicated.

(3) High Speed Plotting of Animation
      Traces in animation (line drawing) are normally plotted in a curve according to NURBS
      commands.
      In a high feedrate plotting mode, however, traces are plotted in straight lines to the end of
      NURBS segments. Straight lines are also used for plotting during the sequence search.

(4) Machining Time on Animation Display
      The "MACHINING TIME" on the animation display screen indicates a time calculated based on
      the travel distance when the machine is locked.
      In the NURBS mode, machining time is calculated based on the distance of travel not along a
      curve but along a straight line to the end of NURBS segment which is an assumed transfer
      path. In addition, the feedrate is calculated using the "FEEDRATE UPPER LIMIT". Therefore,
      considerably shorter time than actually required for machining will be indicated.
      In the case of a non-machine lock, actual machining time is measured.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-241
                                                      SECTION 26 AUTOMATIC ATTACHMENT INDEXING FUNCTION

SECTION 26 AUTOMATIC ATTACHMENT INDEXING
                   FUNCTION

26-1. Overview

            This function indexes an attachment in intervals of 5 degrees, 15 degrees, or 45 degrees.
            The index angle intervals (5, 15, 45 degrees) are determined by the machine specification. Refer to
            the specification for approval.

26-2. Index Commands

            The attachment index command includes the direction through which the attachment is rotated.
             M94 ........ Attachment indexing in the forward direction
             M95 ........ Attachment indexing in the reverse direction

26-3. Angle Commands

            Attachment index angle is defined as absolute angle position in the forward rotation direction with
            the front position taken as 0 degree.
            RH = 

                       
            Angle 0 - 359

26-4. Command Format

M  RH = 

  

  Angle

Rotating direction

[Supplement]

 1) The M code and an angle command must be designated in the same block.
 2) The angle command should be given after the spindle rotation has stopped.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-242
                                                                                SECTION 27 SLOPE MACHINING FUNCTION

SECTION 27 SLOPE MACHINING FUNCTION

27-1. Outline

            The slope machining function converts the commands specified in the basic coordinate system into
            the commands in a desired coordinate system so that the part program created in the X-Y-Z
            coordinate system can be executed in a desired coordinate system.

                                                                                                                                                                                                     ME32128R0303000010001

            The desired coordinate system, stated above, is formed by shifting parallel and rotating the basic
            coordinate system.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              ME32128R0303000010002

            (1) Specify the shift amounts from the basic coordinate system origin to the desired coordinate
                   system origin with X, Y, and Z commands, and move parallel the coordinate system.

            (2) Specify a rotation angle and rotate the shifted coordinate system with respect to any of the axes
                   X', Y', and Z'.

            (3) The slope coordinate system X"-Y"-Z" is thus formed by the movements in the above steps (1)
                   and (2). The above two movements are counting as twice of conversion, and this function can
                   perform conversion up to three times.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-243
                                                                                SECTION 27 SLOPE MACHINING FUNCTION

27-2. Coordinate Conversion in Automatic, MDI Operation
        Mode

The 3-D coordinate conversion command instructs the NC to set a slope coordinate system and
perform normal machining in the set coordinate system.

27-2-1.Setting Slope Coordinate System

There are two command formats:

a. [G69 [X_] [Y_] [Z_] P_Q_R_ ]

X, Y, Z      : Parallel shift amount of each axis
X, Y, P      : X-Y plane rotation angle (rotary shift with respect to Z-axis)
X, Y, Q      : Z-X plane rotation angle (rotary shift with respect to Y-axis)
X, Y, R      : Y-Z plane rotation angle (rotary shift with respect to X-axis)
P, Q, R      : -9999.9999 to 9999.9999 (including the rotary axis with rotation limits)

 For the P, Q, R rotary axes, their CCW direction is regarded as the positive direction when
    viewed from the positive direction of the linear axis not belonging to the plane formed with
    the other two linear axes.

b. [G69 H1]

[Supplement]

 1) If X, Y, or Z value is omitted, "0" is set by default.
 2) For P, Q, and R commands, be sure to specify only one of them.
 3) When the command in b) is specified, the NC makes conversion based on the coordinate

      conversion parameters set in the manual operation mode (refer to 27-3. Coordinate
      Conversion in Manual Operation Mode in this section).

As described earlier, coordinate conversion can be done up to three times.

When the command in a) is specified, occurrence of G69 command counts up the conversion
times. If a conversion command is specified 4 times, the NC regards that the 3rd setting is
changed and replaces the 3rd setting with the new setting (4th setting), and executes
conversion.

When the command in b) is specified, the NC deletes all the settings and regards the new
setting as the 1st setting even if the coordinate conversion is in progress. The number of
conversion times is the same with the number of parameters.

If the command in a) is specified after issuance of the command in b), the number of conversion
times is counted up or the 3rd setting is changed.

When the G68 command is specified, the NC cancels the last setting of the coordinate
conversion data. With this cancellation, the number of conversion times is counted down. This
conversion cancel command applies to the command in a) and the command in b) in the same
manner.
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-244
                                                                   SECTION 27 SLOPE MACHINING FUNCTION
[Supplement]
 1) The G69 command can be cancelled in a batch by the NC reset Button or M02 command. If
      the manual operation mode is selected with the command a), the command remains
      unchanged until the 3-D COORDINATE SET screen is displayed.
 2) On the graphic display screen, there may be a case where the displayed tool path deviates
      from the immediately displayed tool path right after the coordinate conversion is executed or
      canceled.

                                                                                                                                                                                                           ME32128R0303000030001

Notes on setting the slope coordinate system

   When the slope coordinate system is set with a G69 command, position all the X-, Y- and Z-axis

      by the absolute dimensioning mode (G90 command) before moving axes, or specify the
      coordinate recalculation function (G97 command). [(1) and (2) in the program example below.]
      However, this is not necessary if optional parameter bit No.60 bit 2is set to ON.

      Example:
      X10Y10Z10
      G69P30
      G69Q30
      G90X0Y0Z0 ........ (1)
      X100Y100
      G69R30
      G97 ..................... (2)
      X200Y200Z200

   Since the slope coordinate conversion OFF mode (G68 command) is paired to G69, G68 must

      be specified for the number of the times G69 command is specified (maximum three times). If
      axes are moved during two or more G68 commands, the first axis travel should be done by the
      absolute dimensioning mode (G90 command) for all the X-, Y- and Z-axis, or the coordinate
      recalculation function (G97 command) should be given in the block before the axis travel. [(1)
      and (2) in the program example below.]

      Example:
      X10Y10Z10
      G69P30
      G69Q30
      G69R30
      G97
      X100Y100
      G68
      G68
      G90X0Y0Z0 ........ (1)
      X50Y50
      G68
      G97 ..................... (2)
      X100Y100

27-2-4.Upper Limit Return (M52)

            During coordinate conversion, the upper limit return commands (M52) used in a fixed cycle or a
            milling cycle are all converted into R-point return commands (M54). The upper limit return command
            issued in the last cycle of the coordinate calculation function is also disabled. However, specified-
            point return command (M53) can be used.

27-2-5.Axis Command Cancel

            Axis command cancel is disabled in the converted coordinate system.

27-3. Coordinate Conversion in Manual Operation Mode

            To perform manual operation in the slope coordinate system, set the coordinate conversion
            parameters in the manual operation mode and turn ON the slope selection switch.
            The coordinate conversion parameters set in the manual mode can be also called and set in the
            automatic or MDI mode by specifying G69H1 in the automatic or MDI mode.

27-3-1.Setting Coordinate Conversion Parameters

            Display the 3-D COORDINATE SET screen.
            Procedure :

           1 Select the manual operation mode with the mode selection key.
           2 Press the function key [F1] (3-D COORD/SET).

            The 3-D COORDINATE SET screen consists of the 1st and the 2nd screens. The screen to be
            displayed can be selected from the two screens by the setting at the NC optional parameter (bit) No.
            11, bit 5 (refer to 27-7. Parameter Setting in this section).
            1st screen

                                                                                                                                                                                                                       ME32128R0303000100001

            In this screen, set the parallel SHIFT AMOUNT, ROT.CEN.AXIS (ROTARY AXIS) and its REV.
            ANGLE, which are the data required when you specify the G69 command.
                                                           6124-E P-253
            SECTION 27 SLOPE MACHINING FUNCTION

2nd screen

                                                                                                                                                                                             ME32128R0303000100002

In this screen, set the coordinate point position () in the slope coordinate system instead of
specifying the parallel shift amounts, and set the rotary axis name and its rotation angle in the same
way as setting data on the 1st screen.

 Coordinate point position in the slope coordinate system
     The coordinate point position means the spindle position located after execution of 3-D
     coordinate conversion, in other word, the actual position in the slope coordinate system.

[Supplement]

 1) The coordinate conversion parameters set on the 1st screen are the same with those set on
      the 2nd screen through their expressions are different. It is impossible to set different
      parameters on these two screens.

 2) As described earlier, the coordinate conversion parameters set in the manual mode can be
      transferred to the parameters used in the automatic or MDI operation mode by specifying
      G69H1 command.

        1st screen: The SHIFT AMOUNT, ROT. CEN. AXIS (ROTARY AXIS), and REV. ANGLE

          parameters are transferred for each conversion level as they are.

        2nd screen: The ROT. CEN. AXIS and REV. ANGLE are the same as those in the 1st

          screen. For the SHIFT AMOUNT, however, the total SHIFT AMOUNT is transferred as the
          1ST CONV., and the SHIFT AMOUNT for the 2ND CONV. and 3RD CONV. are transferred
          as 0.
          * When specifying G68 while the 2nd screen is selected, ensure that the shift amounts for
          the last conversion are properly cancelled.
 3) If "IGNORE" is specified for the ROT. CEN. AXIS, subsequent conversions become invalid. If
      for example, "IGNORE" is set for the ROT. CEN. AXIS for the 2ND CONV. (2ND REV), only
      the 1ST CONV. (1ST REV) will be valid, and the data set for the 2ND CONV. (2ND REV) and
      3RD CONV. (3RD REV) will be ignored.
 4) If G69H1 is specified when the coordinate conversion parameters are invalid ("IGNORE" is set
      for the ROT. CEN. AXIS for the 1ST CONV. (1ST REV)), an alarm will occur.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-254
                                                                                SECTION 27 SLOPE MACHINING FUNCTION

27-3-2.Transferring Coordinate Conversion Parameters on Selection of
         Manual Mode

            You may want to correct the program by selecting the manual operation mode during machining in
            automatic or MDI operation mode.
            When the manual mode is selected, the coordinate conversion data which has been used
            immediately before selection of the manual mode can be automatically transferred and displayed as
            the coordinate conversion parameters.
            Whether the data is automatically transferred or not can be selected by the setting at the NC
            optional parameter (bit) No. 11, bit 6.

                                                                                                                                                                              ME32128R0303000110001
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-255
                                                                   SECTION 27 SLOPE MACHINING FUNCTION

If "1" is set at NC optional parameter (bit) No.11, bit 6, and the manual mode is selected at the
timings indicated with (1) to (4) during execution of the above program, data is transferred as the
coordinate conversion parameters. The table below shows the relation between the mode change
timing and the transferred data.
Conversion data which has not been cancelled with G68 command is transferred by M02 or reset
commands.

Mode changed timing      1st conversion  Data to be transferred  3rd conversion
                                 (a)         2nd conversion               (c)
             (1)                 (a)                  (b)                  -
             (2)                 (a)                  (b)                  -
             (3)                   -                    -                  -
             (4)                                        -

The currently set coordinate conversion parameters are all cleared before the data is transferred.
Therefore, if some of the conversion data are not transferred, no data is set at the corresponding
conversion levels (indicated with "-" in the table).

27-3-3.Executing Coordinate Conversion

To enable coordinate conversion parameters during manual operation mode, turn ON the slope
selection switch. With this, coordinate conversion is executed.
The X, Y, and Z axis feed at rapid traverse, cutting feed, and pulse handle override commands
specified during coordinate conversion become the commands in the slope coordinate system.
Feed rate and feed amount of each axis are as follows:

- Rapid traverse         .......... The feedrate of the slowest axis becomes the feedrate of
                                     synthesized machine axis.

- Cutting feed           .......... The commanded feedrate becomes the feedrate of
                                     synthesized machine axis.

- Pulse handle override  .......... The commanded distance becomes the distance of the
                                     synthesized machine axis.

[Supplement]

 1) Do not change the travel axis or coordinate variable parameters during axis movement.
 2) If any of the machine axes hits the travel limit during coordinate conversion, all the axes stop.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-256
                                                                                SECTION 27 SLOPE MACHINING FUNCTION

27-4. Displaying the Actual Position

            The actual position in the slope coordinate system can be displayed. Switching between the slope
            coordinate system and the work coordinate system can be done by the setting at the NC optional
            parameter (SLOPE MACHINING) screen.
            However, when displaying the actual position in the slope coordinate system in the manual
            operation mode, turn ON the slope selection switch besides changing the bit data.

            [Supplement]

              1) The actual position on the slope is displayed only in the screens called from the function key.
                   The program display screen always displays the actual position in the work coordinate
                   system.

              2) As a result of accurate calculation by OSP, the displayed actual position may deviates from
                   the commanded coordinate values in the minimum unit.

              3) If the conversion into the local coordinate system (G11) is specified during the 3-D coordinate
                   conversion, the position obtained by converting the work coordinates into the local coordinates
                   is displayed as the actual position in the local coordinate system.
                   This means that the actual position in the local coordinate system is obtained by conversion
                   from the actual commands without including 3-D coordinate conversion.
