                                                                                          SECTION 28 TAS-S/TAS-C FUNCTION

SECTION 28 TAS-S/TAS-C FUNCTION

28-1. Outline

            The TAS-S/TAS-C function compensates the thermal deformation caused during spindle rotation or
            ambient temperature change. This function can be classified into the following three functions.
            (1) TAS-S

                   Compensates the thermal deformation due to the heat generated by the spindle bearings and
                   the spindle drive motor during spindle rotation.
                    Fig.28-1 shows the screen of THERMAL DEFORMATION COMP. MONITOR 1.
            (2) TAS-C
                   Compensates the thermal deformation of the machine (except the spindle) caused by ambient
                   temperature change.
                    Fig.28-1 shows the screen of THERMAL DEFORMATION COMP. MONITOR 1.
            (3) TAS-C with thermal expansion compensation function
                   In addition to the compensation by the above TAS-C, this function compensates even a
                   difference in thermal deformation between positive and negative travel ends of a component
                   such as the table. This function is suitable for the machines having a wide machining range
                   such as double-column machining centers.
                    Fig.28-2 shows the screen of THERMAL DEFORMATION COMP. MONITOR 1.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                     ME32128R0303100010001

                                  Fig.28-1 Thermal Deformation Compensation Monitor 1 (TAS-S, TAS-C)
                                                                                                         6124-E P-264
                                                                   SECTION 28 TAS-S/TAS-C FUNCTION

                                                                                                                                                                       ME32128R0303100010002

Fig.28-2 Thermal Deformation Compensation Monitor 1 (TAS-C with Thermal Expansion
                                           Compensation Function)
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-265
                                                                                          SECTION 28 TAS-S/TAS-C FUNCTION

28-2. TAS-S, TAS-C (including the combined use with TAS-S)

28-2-1.System Configuration and Features

            The temperature sensors embedded in the various locations of the machine send temperature
            information to the temperature measuring unit. Based on the received information and the machine
            operating conditions, the NC calculates the present thermal deformation amount.
            Based on this result, the NC automatically outputs the compensation amount to the axes of which
            coordinate needs to be compensated.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                ME32128R0303100020001

                                                  Fig.28-3 Example of System Configuration

           <The operator can forget these compensation amounts. >

            The compensation amounts are internally processed and do not affect the currently selected
            coordinate system or tool length offset amount. Therefore the operator does not need to be
            conscious of the compensation amount.

           <Function for adjusting output compensation amount>

            Compensation parameters suitable for the machine are factory-set. TAS permits the operator to
            adjust the compensation amount according to the machining conditions for more accurate
            machining. For details, refer to "Adjusting Output Compensation" in 28-2-3.

           <Compensation function is always effective. >

            This function becomes effective when the power is turned ON in all the operation modes (automatic,
            MDI, and manual). The compensation amount changes in real time.

           <Consideration to machining quality>

            TAS compensates deformation in units of 0.1 m even if the minimum data setting unit for NC is 1
            m so that the user can achieve high quality machining. Various compensating methods are
            available. Select desired methods according to your machining needs. For details, refer to "Setting
            Compensating Operations" in 28-2-3.

            This screen shows the temperatures measured by sensors and calculated compensation amounts.

           Explanation of screen items

            (1) SENSOR [TEMP]
                   Temperatures measured by the temperature sensors are displayed at the right of sensor
                   numbers.
                   Temperature can be measured at a maximum of 36 places.
                   "OVER(-)" is displayed at the unused sensor channels (where NO is set according to the
                   explanation "Common Parameters for Thermal Deformation Compensation" in 28-2-3).

            (2) ESTIMATE
                   The compensation amounts calculated from the temperature data is displayed in mm or inch.
                   "non-c" is displayed for the axis which is not compensated.

            (3) COMP.
                   The compensation amount that has been output to each axis is displayed in mm or inch.
                   "non-c" is displayed for the axis which is not compensated.

28-2-3.Parameter Setting Screen

The compensation parameters described on the following pages are factory-set. Do not change
their values except the following cases:

  Changing the TRAVERSE parameter to change the compensation method. (Refer to "Setting

    Compensating Operations" in 28-2-3.)

  Changing the COMP. OUTPUT [%] parameter to adjust the compensation amount to be

    output. (Refer to "Adjusting Output Compensation Amount" in 28-2-3.)

To change compensation parameter, "NO" must be previously set at 1 COMPENSATION SW.
The No. 1 COMPENSATION SW can be changed only in the manual operation mode. If changed
in the other operation mode, 5466 "Auto operation" will occur. (However there are cases where
no such error occurs depending on the software specifications.)
If the parameter is changed when "YES" is selected, "5380 Thermal deviation compensation
executing" will occur.
If any other TAS-related alarm occurs, the NC stops compensation and retains the compensation
amounts which have been set before alarm occurrence.
Press the RESET button to restart compensation operation.

n Setting Compensating Operations
The following compensating operation methods are available.

     Compensating                     Setting       Effective                     Explanation
         operation      TRAVERSE parameter: NO      operation
                                                                         Updates compensation
(1) Updates                                            mode              amount in units of 0.1 m
      compensation                                                       in real time and output to
      constantly.                                AUTO, MDI,              the axes.
      (Normal setting)                           MANUAL                  Updates compensation
                                                                         amount and outputs to the
(2) Updates             TRAVERSE parameter: YES AUTO, MDI,               axes only during axis
      compensation                                               MANUAL  movement.
      amount only
      during axis       M401: Stops updating     AUTO, MDI               M codes are used to stop
      movement.         M402: Restarts updating                          or restart compensating at
                                                                         desired timing.
(3) Stops and restarts
      updating at
      desired timing.

(1) Updating compensation amount constantly and outputting to the axes (Normal setting)
      Setting method

         1 Select the manual operation mode. (This step may not be required depending on the
              software specifications.)

         2 Set "NO" at 1.COMPENSATION SW.

         3 Set "NO" at TRAVERSE parameter.

         4 Set YES at 1.COMPENSATION SW.

      Description
      Compensation amount is updated in units of 0.1 m in real time and output to the axes in
      automatic, MDI or manual operation mode. This method is effective in all the machining
      methods.

(2) Updating compensation amount only during axis movement and outputting to the axes
      Setting method

         1 Select the manual operation mode. (This step may not be required depending on the
              software specifications.)

         2 Set "NO" at 1.COMPENSATION SW.

         3 Set "YES" at TRAVERSE parameter.

         4 Set "YES" at 1.COMPENSATION SW.

      Description
      Compensation amount is updated and output to the axes only during axis movements in
      automatic, MDI or manual operation mode.
      Compensation amount remains unchanged but it is updated when the axis movement is carried
      out.
      In face machining, for example, compensation is stopped in the direction vertical to the
      machined face.
                                                      6124-E P-273
                 SECTION 28 TAS-S/TAS-C FUNCTION

(3) Stopping and restarting compensation at desired timing
      Setting method
      Designate the following M codes for "Stopping update of compensation" and "Restarting
      compensation."

Stopping update  : M401 "Compensation update inhibit"

Restarting update : M402 "Cancel of compensation update inhibit"

        M402 is effective when the power is turned ON.

        If the NC RESET button is pressed after designation of M401, M402 becomes effective.
              If manual mode is selected after designation of M401, M402 becomes effective.
              In other cases, M402 remains effective from designation of M401 until designation of
              M402.

      Description
      Compensation amount can be stopped or restarted at desired timing using M codes.
      Specify M401 when you want to stop update of compensation, and specify M402 to restart
      update of compensation when the machining is completed.

[%]

 Normally select "100%" for COMP. OUTPUT [%]. If a value is changed, compensation
        amount will differ from factory-set amount.

 Use this parameter when adjusting compensation amount. For details, refer to the next page.

n Adjusting Output Compensation

Optimum values for the machine are set at the compensation parameters when the machine is
shipped from the factory. TAS enables the operator to adjust the compensation amount according to
the machining conditions to achieve more accurate machining,

 Adjust the output value carefully. If a value other than "100%" is entered, compensation

  amount differs from the factory-set compensation amount. Improper setting will deteriorate
  machining accuracy.
  When the machining is finished and adjustment is no more necessary, return the COMP.
  OUTPUT [%] parameter to "100%."

 Adjust the output rate by changing the setting at COMP. OUTPUT [%] in increments/

  decrements of10% and check the machining result. Though the setting range is "-500 to
  500%", remember that the effective adjustment range is "50 to 150% or so."
  If accuracy does not improve even when "50 to 150%" range is exceeded, the cause may be
  other than thermal deformation.

(1) Setting method

         1 Change the COMP. OUTPUT [%] value to a desired one for the required compensation
              object (1) (2).

1 There are nine compensation objects.

No. 1 to 3 SPINDLE X/Y/Z                : Compensation for thermal deformation of
                                           the spindle system caused by spindle
                                           rotation

No. 4 to 6 CONSTRUCTION X/Y/Z           : Compensation for thermal deformation of
                                           the machine body caused by ambient
                                           temperature change

No.7 to 9 WORK X/Y/Z                    : Compensation for thermal deformation of
                                           workpiece caused by ambient temperature
                                           change

Of these objects, only those for which USE is selected at COMP. OBJ can be adjusted.
                                                                  6124-E P-275
                             SECTION 28 TAS-S/TAS-C FUNCTION

2 Compensation amount can be adjusted by changing the value set at COMP. OUTPUT
      [%]. The factory-set value is "100%."
      If a larger (smaller) value is set at COMP. OUTPUT [%], compensation amount of the
      relevant object becomes large (small).
      When changing the value, increase or decrease the value at COMP. OUTPUT [%] in
      increments/decrements of 10 % and check the marching result. Refer to the "Example of
      Adjustment 1 and 2" described below.
      Note that compensation amount remains the same after change of COMP. OUTPUT [%]
      value if NO USE is selected at COMP. OBJ.

(2) Example of adjustment 1

Condition  : The machine is equipped with TAS-S function.
              The machine, which is not sufficiently warmed up, performs machining at high
              spindle speed (spindle temperature increases during machining.)

Result     : Machined dimension in Z direction is minus (the spindle advances toward the
              workpiece).

Cause      : The compensation function moves the Z-axis in positive direction to
              compensate for thermal deformation of the spindle in Z direction (deformation
              that makes the machined dimension minus). However, the compensation
              amount fell shorter than the actual spindle deformation in Z-direction,
              resulting in minus dimension.

Adjustment : Slightly increase the COMP. OUTPUT [%] value for SPINDLE Z to improve
                     dimensional accuracy.

(3) Example of adjustment 2

Condition  : The machine is equipped with TAS-C function.
              Machine operating condition is the same but the workpiece material or size is
              changed (workpiece temperature increases during machining).

Result     : Machined dimension in Z direction is plus (the spindle retracts from the
              workpiece).

Cause      : The compensation function moves Z-axis in positive direction to compensate
              for thermal deformation of the workpiece in Z direction (deformation that
              makes the machined dimension minus). However, workpiece deformation in Z
              direction became smaller than the compensation amount because the
              workpiece material or size is changed, resulting in plus dimension.

Adjustment : Slightly decrease the COMP. OUTPUT [%] value for WORK Z to improve
                     dimensional accuracy.

                     MONITOR from the list.

           Explanation of screen items

            (1) SENSOR [TEMP]
                   Temperatures measured by the temperature sensors are displayed at the right of sensor
                   numbers.
                   Temperature can be measured at a maximum of 36 places.
                   "OVER(-)" is displayed at the unused sensor channels (where NO is set according to the
                   explanation "Common Parameters for Thermal Deformation Compensation" in 28-3-3).

            (2) ESTIMATE
                   The compensation amounts calculated from the temperature data is displayed in mm or inch.
                   "non-c" is displayed for the axis which is not compensated.
                   If "non-c" is displayed at EXPANSION/SHRINKAGE, the cutter position is shifted by the
                   estimated common compensation amount.

            (3) COMP.
                   The compensation amount that has been output to each axis is displayed in mm or inch.
                   "non-c" is displayed for the axis which is not compensated.
                                                                                    6124-E P-278
                                               SECTION 28 TAS-S/TAS-C FUNCTION

(4) EXPANSION/SHRINKAGE (Displayed only for TAS-C.)
      Shows the compensation amount from the fixed work position per 1 m caused by expansion or
      shrinkage in units of mm/m, and shows "non-c" for the axis not compensated.

(5) COMP. OBJECT
      Displays WORK in the machining mode where thermal deformation of the work is compensated
      during machining, and displays MACHINE in the gauging mode where gauging system ()
      such as a touch sensor fixed to the machine is compensated.
      Also refer to "Setting Compensating Operation" in 28-3-3. If the gauging mode and the
      machining mode are not clearly distinguished, "-" appears.
       The gauging time by the touch probe is included in the machining mode.

The sensor channels and corresponding sensor positions for TAS-S function are as follows:

(1) Vertical/horizontal machining center

    4CH                Column

 3CH sensor may not be used depending on the machine model.
 The above settings are also used for MCV-AII (double-column machining center).

                       Spindle cooling oil piping (return)

                3CH    Not used
                4CH    Column
                5CH
                6CH

    3CH                Spindle cooling oil piping (discharge)

      spindle.

How to Calculate Compensation Amount

COMP. shows the compensation amount at the fixed work position. The compensation amount at
the actual position (cutting edge position) can be calculated by the following formula.
<Compensation amount in the current coordinate system>
= <Compensation amount> + <Expansion/shrinkage> × <PW - P> / 1000
PW : Machine coordinate at the fixed work position
P : Current machine coordinates
Example
The THERMAL DEFORMATION COMP. MONITOR 1 screen shows the following current
coordinates and fixed work coordinates, compensation amount in the current coordinates can be
calculated by the following formula.
<X-axis compensation amount in the current coordinate>
= <-0.0756> + <0.0112> × < (-1090) - (-90) >/1000 = -0.0868 mm
<Y-axis compensation amount in the current coordinate>
= <-0.0056> + <-0.0235> × < (1300) - (800) >/1000 = 0.0062 mm

28-3-3.Parameter Setting Screen

The compensation parameters described on the following pages are factory-set. Do not change
their values except the following cases:

  Changing the TRAVERSE parameter to change the compensation method. (Refer to "Setting

    Compensating Operations" in 28-3-3.)

  Changing the COMP. OUTPUT [%] parameter to adjust the compensation amount to be

    output. (Refer to "Adjusting Output Compensation Amount" in 28-3-3.)

  Changing user input parameter according to the machining feature

To change the common parameters for thermal deformation compensation, "NO" must be
previously set at 1 COMPENSATION SW.
The No. 1 COMPENSATION SW can be changed only in the manual operation mode. If changed
in the other operation mode, 5466 "Auto operation" will occur.
If the parameter is changed when "YES" is selected, "5380 Thermal deviation compensation
executing" will occur.
If any other TAS-related alarm occurs, the NC stops compensation and retains the compensation
amounts which have been set before alarm occurrence.
Press the RESET button to restart compensation operation.

(1) Updating compensation amount constantly and outputting to the axes (Normal setting)
      Setting method

         1 Select the manual operation mode.
         2 Set "NO" at 1.COMPENSATION SW.
         3 Set "NO" at TRAVERSE parameter.
         4 Set "YES" at 1.COMPENSATION SW.

      Description
      Compensation amount is updated in units of 0.1 m in real time and output to the axes in
      automatic, MDI or manual operation mode. This method is effective in all the machining
      methods.

(2) Updating compensation amount only during axis movement and outputting to the axes
      Setting method

         1 Select the manual operation mode.
         2 Set "NO" at 1.COMPENSATION SW.
         3 Set "YES" at TRAVERSE parameter.
         4 Set "YES" at 1.COMPENSATION SW.

      Description
      Compensation amount is updated and output to the axes only during axis movements in
      automatic, MDI or manual operation mode.
      Compensation amount remains unchanged but it is updated when the axis movement is carried
      out.
                                                                 6124-E P-285
                            SECTION 28 TAS-S/TAS-C FUNCTION

In face machining, for example, compensation is stopped in the direction vertical to the
machined face.

(3) Stopping and restarting compensation at desired timing
      Setting method
      Designate the following M codes for "Stopping update of compensation" and "Restarting
      compensation."

Stopping update    : M401 "Compensation update inhibit"
Restarting update  : M402 "Cancel of compensation update inhibit"

  M402 is effective when the power is turned ON.

  If the NC RESET button is pressed after designation of M401, M402 becomes effective.
        If manual mode is selected after designation of M401, M402 becomes effective.
        In other cases, M402 remains effective from designation of M401 until designation of
        M402.

Description
Compensation amount can be stopped or restarted at desired timing using M codes.
Specify M401 when you want to stop update of compensation, and specify M402 to restart
update of compensation when the machining is completed.

(4) Changing machining mode and gauging mode
      Setting method
      Specify the following M codes to designate auto judgment mode, machining mode, or gauging
      mode.

M914  : Auto judgment mode
M915  : Machining mode
M916  : Gauging mode

  M914 is selected with the power ON.

  If NC reset button is pressed after designation of M915 or M916, M914 becomes
        effective.
        If manual operation mode is selected after designation of M915 or M916, M914 becomes
        effective. In other cases, the first designated M code is effective.

Description
It is possible to change the compensation method by specifying the machine state to machining
mode or gauging mode with relevant M codes. Normally operate the machine with M914 mode,
and if the NC cannot automatically judge the state, specify M915 or M916 to forcibly change the
compensating condition.
In M 914 mode, if the gauging system fixed to the table such as a touch sensor or a bloom
sensor becomes effective, the gauging mode is established. In other cases, compensation is
done as in the case of machining mode.
Work gauging done with a probe is included in the machining mode.
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-286
                                                                             SECTION 28 TAS-S/TAS-C FUNCTION
(5) Method of enabling or disabling the thermal deformation compensation function to change the
      user input parameters
      Setting method
      Specify the following M code to turn on or off the thermal deformation function.
       M912 : Thermal deformation compensation OFF
       M913 : Thermal deformation compensation ON
        M913 is selected in the initial state established when the power is turned on.
        If the NC reset button is pressed, the M912 mode changes to the M913 mode.
              If the manual operation mode is selected, the M912 mode changed to the M913.
              In the other cases, once M912 is specified, it remains effective until M913 is specified.
      Description
      The thermal deformation compensation state can be changed as desired using the relevant M
      codes.
      In the common parameters for thermal deformation compensation, the thermal deformation
      compensation switch is set to YES and the compensation is turned off by a programmed M912
      command, the alarm D 4329 "Thermal displacement countermeasure is invalid 2" is displayed.
      When using the thermal deformation compensation function after changing the user input
      parameter with system variables in the NC program, specify M913. When M913 is specified, the
      thermal deformation compensation function is activated and the alarm D is released.

[%]

n Adjusting Output Compensation

Optimum values for the machine are set at the compensation parameters when the machine is
shipped from the factory. TAS enables the operator to adjust the compensation amount according to
the machining conditions to achieve more accurate machining.

 Adjust the output value carefully. If a value other than "100%" is entered, compensation

  amount differs from the factory-set compensation amount. Improper setting will deteriorate
  machining accuracy.
  When the machining is finished and adjustment is no more necessary, return the COMP.
  OUTPUT [%] parameter to "100%."

 Adjust the output rate by changing the setting at COMP. OUTPUT [%] in increments/

  decrements of10% and check the machining result. Though the setting range is "-500 to
  500%", remember that the effective adjustment range is "50 to 150% or so."
  If accuracy does not improve even when "50 to 150%" range is exceeded, the cause may be
  other than thermal deformation.
                                                                             6124-E P-288
                                        SECTION 28 TAS-S/TAS-C FUNCTION

(1) Setting method

         1 Change the COMP. OUTPUT [%] value to a desired one for the required compensation
              object (1) (2).

1 There are nine compensation objects.

No. 1 to 3 SPINDLE X/Y/Z                : Compensation for thermal deformation of the
                                           spindle system caused by spindle rotation

No. 4 to 6 CONSTRUCTION X/Y/Z           : Compensation for thermal deformation of the
                                           machine body caused by ambient
                                           temperature change

No.7 to 9 WORK X/Y/Z                    : Compensation for thermal deformation of
                                           workpiece caused by ambient temperature
                                           change

Of these objects, only those for which USE is selected at COMP. OBJ can be adjusted.

2 Compensation amount can be adjusted by changing the value set at COMP. OUTPUT
      [%]. The factory-set value is "100%."
      If a larger (smaller) value is set at COMP. OUTPUT [%], compensation amount of the
      relevant object becomes large (small).
      When changing the value, increase or decrease the value at COMP. OUTPUT [%] in
      increments/decrements of 10 % and check the machining result. Refer to the "Example of
      Adjustment 1 and 2" described below.
      Note that compensation amount remains the same after change of COMP. OUTPUT [%]
      value if NO USE is selected at COMP. OBJ.

(2) Example of adjustment 1

Condition  : The machine is equipped with TAS-S function.
              The machine, which is not sufficiently warmed up, performs machining at high
              spindle speed (spindle temperature increases during machining.)

Result     : Machined dimension in Z direction is minus (the spindle advances toward the
              workpiece).

Cause      : The compensation function moves the Z-axis in positive direction to
              compensate for thermal deformation of the spindle in Z direction (deformation
              that makes the machined dimension minus). However, the compensation
              amount fell shorter than the actual spindle deformation in Z-direction,
              resulting in minus dimension.

Adjustment : Slightly increase the COMP. OUTPUT [%] value for SPINDLE Z to improve
                     dimensional accuracy.

(3) Example of adjustment 2

Condition  : The machine is equipped with TAS-C function.
              Machine operating condition is the same but the workpiece material or size is
Result        changed (workpiece temperature increases during machining).
Cause
           : Machined dimension in Z direction is plus (the spindle retracts from the
              workpiece).

           : The compensation function moves Z-axis in positive direction to compensate
              for thermal deformation of the workpiece in Z direction (deformation that
              makes the machined dimension minus). However, workpiece deformation in Z
              direction became smaller than the compensation amount because the
              workpiece material or size is changed, resulting in plus dimension.
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-289
                                                                             SECTION 28 TAS-S/TAS-C FUNCTION

       Adjustment : Slightly decrease the COMP. OUTPUT [%] value for WORK Z to improve
                            dimensional accuracy.

User Input Parameters

For a large workpiece or a workpiece of which linear expansion coefficient differs from that of iron,
further accurate compensation is possible by setting the work fixed position and the linear expansion
coefficient at the user input parameters.

USER INPUT PARAMETER P1 (for setting work position information)

Set the information such as work position or material as parameters.
For the detail method of changing user input parameters, refer to USER INPUT PARAMETER P2.

                                                                                                                                                        ME32128R0303100120001

(1) Screen display method
      Parameter setting  [F8] (DISPLAY CHANGE)  Select THERMAL DEFORMATION COMP.
      PARAMETERS from the list  (extension key)  [F5] (USER INPUT PARAMETER). The
      USER INPUT PARAMETER pop-up window appears.
      When the thermal deformation compensation switch is set to YES on the THERMAL
      DEFORMATION COMP. PARAMETERS screen, the entire items on the USER INPUT
      PARAMETER screen are half-erased, disabling data change.
      If [F6] (PARAMETER CHANGE) is selected at this time, the following message appears
      "Thermal deformation is turned off. OK? Y/N."
      When you select [F6] (YES), the half-erased display returns to normal and the data change is
      enabled. (Some of unused items remain unchanged depending on the environment.)
      To terminate the parameter setting operation, close the pop-up window by pressing [F7] (QUIT).
      In the manual operation mode, if the thermal deformation compensation is turned off with
      parameter change, the compensation is automatically turned on.
      However, if parameter change is pressed in a mode other than the manual operation mode, the
      error 5466 "Auto operation" is displayed.

 Relative distance calculation command

     Relative distance calculation formula:
     Calculated value = Actual coordinate - Reference position setting coordinate - Input
     value (Fraction below decimal point is rounded.)

Example:                                         : -1600.05 mm (machine coordinate)
                                                 : -3600 mm (machine coordinate)
            X-axis positive travel limit         : Middle point
            X-axis negative travel limit         : -2000.325
            X-axis reference position
            X-axis actual position in
            machine coordinate system

When "180" is entered, the relative distance is:
(-2000.325) - (-2600) - (180) = 419.625 = 420

Since the calculation command is used in the manual mode, the current coordinates show
the spindle nose position and not the tool edge position. Therefore, use the attachment
T301.

Setting example from the part program:
Set the reference position of the fixed work position X at CENTER and set the X coordinate
in the work coordinate system at the relative position (the value "400" is based on the
command unit system.)
VPTX2 = 400 + VZOFX[VACOD] - (VPLSX + VNSLX)/2

                                           s expansion during temperature
PALLET TEMP Menu                YES/NO

                                                 Temperature at CH used for pallet

For the sensor channel set at NO in the thermal deformation compensation parameters, there is no
choice other than NO.

1 If "0" is set, deformation is compensated in the condition that the workpiece thermal
          expansion is set to "0."

2 The time constant is used to calculate the temperature change rate at which the work
          expansion follows the temperature change when the workpiece temperature has changed
          rapidly.
          The time constant is expressed with the time required until the thermal expansion reaches
          63.2% of the difference between initial work expansion amount and finally reached
          expansion amount when the work temperature has changed rapidly as shown in the graph
          below.
          Therefore, if a small time constant is set, calculation is made so that the work expansion is
          immediately followed by the work temperature change.
          If a large time constant is set, calculation is made so that the change of work expansion
          delays (slowly follows) the work temperature change.

                Work thermal expansion

63.2%

                Time constant           Time

                    Fig. Change of work thermal expansion following the work temperature change

                                                                                                                                                                                  ME32128R0303100120006

(1) Changing the user input parameters
      It is possible to make further accurate compensation according to the workpiece when the
      operator enters the information such as work position or material.

 After changing the user input parameters, initialize the calculated compensation amount.
 Do not change the input parameters during machining. Doing so may affect normal machining.

Setting procedure

  1 Move the tool and spindle to a position where they do not collide with the workpiece or
       machine components.
        When initializing the calculated compensation after changing the user input
       parameters, perform axis movement for compensation.

  2 Select the manual operation mode.
        Parameter cannot be changed on the NC operation without selecting the manual
       operation mode.

  3 Display the USER INPUT PARAMETER screen.
  4 [F8] (PARAMETER CHANGE)  Do you quit thermal deformation compensation? (Y/N)

        [F6] (YES)  Alarm D "Thermal deformation compensation invalid" appears, disabling
       data change.
        To change the parameter values, it is necessary to stop the TAS-C function.
  5 Set the parameter values. For details, refer to "THERMAL DEFORMATION COMP.
       PARAMETER P1" and later.
  6 [F7] (QUIT)  THERMAL DEFORMATION COMP. PARAMETERS screen appears.
       The alarm D "Thermal displacement countermeasure is invalid" disappears, and the
       calculated compensation is initialized.
        If the screen is changed without pressing [F7] (QUIT), redisplay the USER INPUT
       PARAMETER screen and follow the above step 6.

Method of changing the user input parameters from the program

The operator can set the information such as work position or material as parameters for more
accurate compensation according to the workpiece.

 After changing the user input parameters, initialize the calculated compensation amount.
 Do not change the input parameters during machining. Doing so may affect normal machining.

n Setting method

 1 Move the tool and spindle to a position where they do not interfere with the work or machine
      components.
       When the calculated compensation is initialized after changing the user input parameters,
      axis movement is done for compensation.

 2 Execute M912  Alarm D "Thermal deformation compensation invalid" is displayed, enabling
      data change.

 3 Set the parameter data in the pattern of variable name = set value.
      For detail of each parameter, refer to "THERMAL DEFORMATION COMP. PARAMETER P1"
      and later.
       If M912 is not specified or the set value or variable name is wrong, the alarm "System
      variable setting condition" occurs.

 4 Execute M913  THERMAL DEFORMATION COMP. PARAMETERS screen appears and the
      alarm D "Thermal dispalcement countermeasure is invalid" disappears, indicating that the
      calculated compensation has been initialized.

SECTION 29 TOOL WEAR COMPENSATING FUNCTION

29-1. Outline

            The tool length offset/cutter radius compensation function allows you to set the tool geometry
            compensation amount (standard compensation amount) and the wear compensation amount
            (additional compensation amount) separately.
            During machining, the sum of these compensation amounts is used as the tool length offset amount
            or cutter radius compensation amount.
            This manual describes only what has been changed by addition of the tool wear compensating
            function.
            Please read this section in conjunction with the separate volume Operation Manual.

29-2. Detailed Specifications

29-2-1.Tool Offset Amount

            Because the tool tip position varies depending on the tool kind, we adopted the concept of tool
            offset, which includes the tool length offset and the cutter radius compensation.
            The tool offset amounts consist of the geometry offset amount and the wear offset amount (see Fig.
            1), and the total offset amount is the sum of the above two amounts.
            Tool offset amount = Geometry offset amount + Wear offset amount

                                                                                                                                                                                   ME32128R0303200020001

            The tool offset amount is used when the following functions are activated:
            (1) Tool length offset function (G53-G59)

                   H command is used to specify the tool length offset number.
            (2) Cutter radius compensating function (G40-G42)

                   D command is used to specify the cutter radius compensation number.
            (3) 3-D cutter radius compensating function (G43,G44)

                   D command is used to specify the cutter radius compensation number. (1)
            (4) Area machining function (FMILR etc.)

                   D command is used to specify the cutter radius compensation number. (1)
            The compensation amounts being used by these functions can be confirmed at "H=" and "D=" on the
            actual position screen or the program display screen in the operation mode, or on the TOOL ON/
            OFF pop up window.
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-305
                                                    SECTION 29 TOOL WEAR COMPENSATING FUNCTION

The amounts displayed on these screen are the total tool offset amount (the sum of geometry offset
amount and wear offset amount).

Be sure to set "0" when you do not use the wear offset amount.

The number of tool length offset data pieces and that of cutter radius compensation data pieces is
999 (sets) each.

The system does not distinguish between geometry compensation and wear compensation when
adjusting the actual position according to the tool length offset and cutter radius compensation
amounts.

*1: This is an optional function.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-306
                                                                 SECTION 29 TOOL WEAR COMPENSATING FUNCTION

29-2-2.Setting the Tool Length Offset/Cutter Radius Compensation
         Amounts

            The tool length offset and cutter radius compensation amounts can be set on the TOOL OFFSET/
            COMPENSATION screen (Screen 1) displayed in the tool data setting mode.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                     ME32128R0303200030001

                        Fig.29-1 Screen 1 Tool Length Offset/Cutter Radius Compensation Setting Screen
            Procedure :

           1 Select the tool data setting mode.
           2 Press [F8] (DISPLAY CHANGE) and select TOOL OFFSET/COMPENSATION.

                   The TOOL OFFSET/COMPENSATION screen (Screen 1) appears. The screen can be also
                   displayed by pressing [F6] (ITEM ) or [F7] (ITEM ).

           3 One screen displays ten sets of tool length offset data (geometry and wear) and cutter radius

                   compensation data (geometry and wear).
                   If the desired offset number is not found in the displayed page, change the screen using the
                   page keys or [F4] (FIND).

           4 Move the cursor to the desired position using the cursor keys.
           5 Set the data in any of the following three methods:

                   a. If the tool data is known
                         Press [F1] (SET) and enter the tool data.

                   b. If the tool data has been set and the change amount is known. Press [F2] (ADD) and enter
                         the change amount.
                                                                                                                6124-E P-307
                                                 SECTION 29 TOOL WEAR COMPENSATING FUNCTION

    c. When using [F3] (CAL) function
          This function can be used only for setting the tool length offset data (geometry and wear).
          Before following the steps 1 to 4, set the zero offset data of each tool axis, and then mount
          the tool for which offset data is to be set. Bring the tool nose to the reference plane by
          manual operation.
          Locate the cursor on the tool length offset geometry No. of the mounted tool. Press [F3]
          (CAL) to enter the actual position measured from the zero position.
          The value obtained by subtracting the geometry compensation amount from this function's
          calculation result is set as the wear data.
          When the geometry data is set using the CAL function, the wear compensation
          amount is automatically set to "0."

6 Press the WRITE key.

7 Check if the data has been changed.

    Table 1 shows the range for setting the tool offset amount.

Table 1 Tool Offset Amount Setting Range

  Geometry offset amount                      Wear offset amount
-999.999 mm to +999.999 mm                -99.999 mm to +99.999 mm

Do not forget to change the wear offset amount when changing the geometry offset
amount.

A yellow belt appears as the background of the tool length offset No. and cutter radius
compensation No. being used.
                                                                                                     6124-E P-308
                                       SECTION 29 TOOL WEAR COMPENSATING FUNCTION

Detailed explanation

(1) Tool length geometry offset amount

 Offset numbers H1 to H999

   VTFGH [expression]

     Expression : Tool offset number (1 to 999)

     Explanation : Reads and writes the tool length geometry offset amount of the tool offset
                           number specified in "expression".

 Offset numbers HA/HB/HC

   VTOHG [expression 1, expression 2]

Expression 1 : Tool number (1 to 99999999)

Expression 2  : Tool offset number (HA, HB, HC)
Explanation     Set 10001, 10002, and 10003 for HA, HB, and HC, respectively.

              : Reads and writes the tool length geometry offset amount of the tool
                offset number specified in "expression 2" which is prepared for the tool
                number specified in "expression 1".

(2) Tool length wear offset amount

 Offset numbers H1 to H999

   VTWOH [expression]

     Expression : Tool offset number (1 to 999)

     Explanation : Reads and writes the tool length wear offset amount of the tool offset
                            number specified in "expression".

 Offset numbers HA/HB/HC

   VTOHW [expression 1, expression 2]

Expression 1 : Tool number (1 to 99999999)

Expression 2  : Tool offset number (HA, HB, HC)
Explanation     Set 10001, 10002, and 10003 for HA, HB, and HC, respectively.

              : Reads and writes the tool length wear offset amount of the tool offset
                number specified in "expression 2" which is prepared for the tool
                number specified in "expression 1".

(3) Cutter radius geometry compensation amount

 Compensation numbers D1 to D999

   VTFGD [expression]

     Expression : Tool offset number (1 to 999)

     Explanation : Reads and writes the cutter radius geometry compensation amount of
                            the tool offset number specified in "expression".
                                                                           6124-E P-310
             SECTION 29 TOOL WEAR COMPENSATING FUNCTION

 Compensation numbers HA/HB/HC

   VTODG [expression 1, expression 2]

Expression 1 : Tool number (1 to 99999999)

Expression 2 : Tool offset number (HA, HB, HC)
                      Set 10001, 10002, and 10003 for HA, HB, and HC, respectively.

Explanation  : Reads and writes the cutter radius geometry compensation amount of
               the tool offset number specified in "expression 2" which is prepared for
               the tool number specified in "expression 1".

(4) Cutter radius wear compensation amount

 Compensation numbers D1 to D999

   VTWOD [expression]

Expression   : Tool offset number (1 to 999)
Explanation
             : Reads and writes the cutter radius geometry compensation amount of
               the tool offset number specified in "expression".

 Compensation numbers HA/HB/HC

   VTODW [expression 1, expression 2]

Expression 1 : Tool number (1 to 99999999)

Expression 2 : Tool offset number (HA, HB, HC)
                      Set 10001, 10002, and 10003 for HA, HB, and HC, respectively.

Explanation  : Reads and writes the cutter radius wear compensation amount o the
               tool offset number specified in "expression 2" which is prepared for the
               tool number specified in "expression 1".
                                                                                             6124-E P-311
                               SECTION 29 TOOL WEAR COMPENSATING FUNCTION

(5) Tool length offset amount

 Offset numbers H1 to H999

   VTOFH [expression]

Expression : Tool offset number (1 to 999)

Explanation  : Reads the sum of the tool length geometry offset amount and the tool
for reading    length wear offset amount of the tool offset number specified in
               "expression".
               VTOFH [expression] = VTFGH [expression] + VTWOH [expression]

Explanation : Writing differs depending on the setting in NC optional parameter bit

for writing  No.68 bit 6.

             0:
             Writes "0" into the tool length wear offset amount of the tool offset
             number specified in "expression" at the same time as writing the data
             into the tool length geometry offset amount of the tool offset number
             specified in "expression".

                 VTFGH [expression] = (setting value)
                 VTWOH [expression] = 0
             The allowable range of setting values at this time is from -999.999 to
             999.999.

             1:
             Writes the following calculated result in the tool length wear offset
             amount of the tool offset number specified in "expression".

                 VTWOH [expression] = (setting value) - VTFGH [expression]
             The allowable range of setting value is the values that make the
             calculated result from the expression above from -99.999 to 99.999.
                                                                                        6124-E P-312
                          SECTION 29 TOOL WEAR COMPENSATING FUNCTION

 Offset numbers HA/HB/HC

   VTOHT [expression 1, expression 2]

Expression 1 : Tool number (1 to 99999999)
Expression 2 : Tool offset number (HA, HB, HC)

Explanation  : Reads the sum of the tool length geometry offset amount and the tool
for reading    length wear offset amount of the tool offset number specified in
               "expression 2" which is prepared for the tool number specified in
               "expression 1".
                   VTOHT [expression 1, expression 2] = VTOHG
               [expression 1, expression 2] + VTOHW [expression 1, expression 2]

Explanation : Writing differs depending on the setting in NC optional parameter bit

for writing  No.68bit 6.

             0:
             Writes "0" into the tool length wear offset amount of the tool offset
             number specified in "expression 2" at the same time as writing the data
             into the tool length geometry offset amount of the tool offset number
             specified in "expression 2" which is prepared for the tool number
             specified in "expression 1".

                 VTOHG [expression 1, expression 2] = (setting value)
                 VTOHW [expression 1, expression 2] = 0
             The allowable range of setting values at this time is from -999.999 to
             999.999.

             1:
             Writes the following calculated result in the tool length wear offset
             amount of the tool offset number specified in "expression 2" which is
             prepared for the tool number specified in "expression 1".

                 VTOHW [expression 1, expression 2] = (setting value) - VTOHG
             [expression 1, expression 2]
             The allowable range of setting value is the values that make the
             calculated result from -99.999 to 99.999.
                                                                                         6124-E P-313
                           SECTION 29 TOOL WEAR COMPENSATING FUNCTION

(6) Cutter radius compensation amount

 Compensation numbers D1 to D999

   VTOFD [expression]

Expression : Tool offset number (1 to 999)

Explanation  : Reads the sum of the cutter radius geometry compensation amount and
for reading    the cutter radius wear compensation amount of the tool offset number
               specified in "expression".
                   VTOFD [expression] = VTFGD [expression] + VTWOD [expression]

Explanation : Writing differs depending on the setting in NC optional parameter bit

for writing  No.68 bit 6.

             0:
             Writes "0" into the cutter radius wear compensation amount of the tool
             offset number specified in "expression" at the same time as writing the
             data into the cutter radius geometry compensation amount of the tool
             offset number specified in "expression".

                 VTFGD [expression] = (setting value)
                 VTWOD [expression] = 0
             The allowable range of setting values at this time is from -999.999 to
             999.999.

             1:
             Writes the following calculated result in the cutter radius wear
             compensation amount of the tool offset number specified in
             "expression".

                 VTWOD [expression] = (setting value) - VTFGD [expression]
             The allowable range of setting value is the values that make the
             calculated result from the expression above from -99.999 to 99.999.
                                                                                         6124-E P-314
                           SECTION 29 TOOL WEAR COMPENSATING FUNCTION

 Compensation numbers HA/HB/HC

   VTODT [expression 1, expression 2]

Expression 1 : Tool number (1 to 99999999)
Expression 2 : Tool offset number (HA, HB, HC)

Explanation  : Reads the sum of the cutter radius geometry compensation amount and
for reading    the cutter radius wear compensation amount of the tool offset number
               specified in "expression 2" which is prepared for the tool number
               specified in "expression 1".
                   VTODT [expression 1, expression 2] = VTODG [expression 1,
               expression 2] + VTODW [expression 1, expression 2]

Explanation : Writing differs depending on the setting in NC optional parameter bit

for writing  No.68 bit 6.

             0:
             Writes "0" into the cutter radius wear compensation amount of the tool
             offset number specified in "expression 2" at the same time as writing
             the data into the cutter radius geometry compensation amount of the
             tool offset number specified in "expression 2" which is prepared for the
             tool number specified in "expression 1".

                 VTODG [expression 1, expression 2] = (setting value)
                 VTODW [expression 1, expression 2] = 0
             The allowable range of setting values at this time is from -999.999 to
             999.999.

             1:
             Writes the following calculated result in the cutter radius wear
             compensation amount of the tool offset number specified in "expression
             2" which is prepared for the tool number specified in "expression 1".

                 VTODW [expression 1, expression 2] = (setting value) - VTODG
             [expression 1, expression 2]
             The allowable range of setting value is the values that make the
             calculated result from-99.999 to 99.999.

[Supplement]

    The unit of setting values accords to the operation unit system.

    With offset numbers (H/D) 1 to 999, change the 1st tool offset value (HA/DA) of the same tool

      number as the offset number to the same changed value when the offset amount is changed,
      and vice versa.
      e.g.)
      a) When the offset amount of the tool length offset number "H10" is changed to "100"

          Change the 1st tool length offset value "HA" of tool number "10" to "100".
      b) When the tool length offset value "HA" of tool number "10" is changed to "200"

          Change the offset amount of the tool length offset number "H10" to "200".

29-2-4.Parameter Input/Output Function

This function allows you to input or output the setting data of tool length geometry offset, tool length
wear offset, cutter radius geometry compensation and cutter radius wear compensation.
For more information, refer to "SECTION 4. PARAMETER I/O FUNCTION" in OPERATION
MANUAL - DATA OPERATION.

Data kind  Offset/Comp. Geometry/Wear  P No.    Q No. range     Setting range
Tool data                               200   1 to 999       -999.999 to 999.999
           Tool length  Geometry        201
           offset       Wear            210                   -99.999 to 99.999
                                        211                  -999.999 to 999.999
           Cutter radius Geometry
           compensation Wear                                  -99.999 to 99.999

The above compensation amounts are all parameters compared with measured values including the
gauging error.

29-2-5.Interactive Gauging Function (Optional)

When the tool length is measured by the interactive gauging function

a. The tool length offset value is represented by the sum of tool length geometry offset amount
      and the tool length wear offset amount set with the tool length offset HA, HB or HC and the tool
      length offset number.
      Tool length offset amount =
      Tool length geometry offset amount + Tool length wear offset amount

b. By parameter setting, you can select the offset amount in which the measured value is set from
      between the tool length geometry offset and the tool length wear offset. It is possible to change
      the setting by the offset amount setting parameter in the tool length measurement screen or by
      NC optional parameter bit No.68, bit 5.

       Offset amount setting parameter: geometry offset amount
       NC optional parameter bit No.68, bit 5: Checked

                The measured value is set in the tool length wear offset amount.
                The value obtained by "measured value minus tool length geometry offset amount" is
                set as the tool length wear offset amount.

Offset amount setting parameter: wear offset amount
NC optional parameter bit No.68, bit 5: Not checked

        The measured value is set as the tool length geometry offset amount, and the tool
        length wear amount is set to "0."
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-317
                                                                 SECTION 29 TOOL WEAR COMPENSATING FUNCTION

29-2-6.Manual Gauging Function (Optional)

            When the manual gauging function measures the tool length explained in (1)

            a. The tool length offset value is represented by the sum of tool length geometry offset amount
                   and the tool length wear offset amount set with the tool length offset HA, HB or HC and the tool
                   length offset number.
                   Tool length offset amount =
                   Tool length geometry offset amount + Tool length wear offset amount

            b. By parameter setting, you can select the offset amount in which the measured value is set from
                   between the tool length geometry offset and the tool length wear offset. It is possible to change
                   the setting by the offset amount setting parameter in the tool length measurement screen or by
                   NC optional parameter bit No.68, bit 5.

                    Offset amount setting parameter: geometry offset amount
                    NC optional parameter bit No.68, bit 5: Checked

                             The measured value is set in the tool length wear offset amount.
                             The value obtained by "measured value minus tool length geometry offset amount" is
                             set in the tool length wear amount.
                    Offset amount setting parameter: wear offset amount
                    NC optional parameter bit No.68, bit 5: Not checked
                             The measured value is set in the tool length geometry offset amount, and the tool
                             length wear offset amount is set to "0."

            When the manual gauging function measures the cutter radius explained in (3)

            a. The cutter radius compensation amount is represented by the sum of the cutter radius
                   geometry compensation amount and the cutter radius wear compensation amount set with the
                   cutter radius compensation DA, DB or DC and the cutter radius compensation No. Cutter radius
                   compensation amount = Cutter radius geometry compensation amount + Cutter radius wear
                   compensation amount

            b. It is possible to select the target to which the measurement value is set from between the cutter
                   radius geometry compensation and the cutter radius wear compensation by setting the
                   following parameter. It is possible to change the setting by the offset amount setting parameter
                   in the tool radius measurement screen or by NC optional parameter bit No.68, bit 5.

                    Offset amount setting parameter: geometry offset amount
                    NC optional parameter bit No.68, bit 5: Checked

                             The measured value is set in the cutter radius wear compensation amount.
                             The value obtained by "measured value minus cutter radius geometry offset amount" is
                             set as the cutter radius wear compensation amount.
                    Offset amount setting parameter: wear offset amount
                    NC optional parameter bit No.68, bit 5: Not checked
                             The measured value is set as the cutter radius geometry compensation amount, and
                             the cutter radius wear compensation amount is set to "0."
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-318
                                                                 SECTION 29 TOOL WEAR COMPENSATING FUNCTION

29-2-7.Automated Function (Optional)

By parameter setting, you can select the offset amount in which the tool length obtained by
automatic tool length offset function is set from between the tool length geometry offset and the tool
length wear offset.

NC optional parameter (bit) No.68, bit 6

   0: The offset amount is set in the tool length geometry offset amount, and the tool length
         wear offset amount is set to "0."

   1: The value obtained by [Tool length offset amount with measured value] - [Tool length
         geometry offset amount] is applied to [Tool length wear amount].

29-2-8.DNC-C Function (Optional)

The following DNC-C functions/commands use the tool offset data:

(1) Data input, output, and verification by DNC-C in program operation mode

(2) Tool data setting command "D,TOF" sent from host to CNC

(3) Tool data response command "C,TOF" sent from CNC to host in response to the tool data
      request command "D,TOF" sent from host to CNC

Each of the functions is as follows.

Data input         : The offset amount is set in the geometry offset amount, and the wear
                      offset amount is set to "0."

Data output        : The offset amount is the sum of the geometry offset amount and the
                      wear offset amount.

Data verification  : The offset amount is the sum of the geometry offset amount and the
                      wear offset amount.

"D,TOF" (host  CNC) : The offset amount is set in the geometry compensation amount, and
                                      the wear compensation amount is set to "0."

"D,TOF" (CNC  host) : The offset amount is the sum of the geometry offset amount and the
                                      wear offset amount.
                                                                                 6124-E P-319
                   SECTION 29 TOOL WEAR COMPENSATING FUNCTION

29-2-9.Parameters

a. NC optional parameter (bit) No.68, bit 5
      Selects the offset amount to which the tool length offset/cutter radius compensation data
      obtained by the manual/interactive gauging function is set from between the tool geometry
      offset and the wear offset.

         1: The measured value is set in the tool length wear amount.
               The value obtained by "measured value minus tool length geometry offset amount" is
               set as the tool length wear offset amount.

         0: The measured value is set as the tool length geometry offset amount, and the tool
               length wear amount is set to "0."

b. NC optional parameter (bit) No.68, bit 6
      Selects the offset amount to which the data is to be set from between the tool geometry offset
      and the wear offset when the system writes the data in the system variables VTOFH, VTOHT or
      VTOFD, VTODT.
      This parameter also selects the offset amount to which the data obtained by the automatic tool
      length offset function is set from between the tool geometry offset and the tool wear offset.

1: The data is set in the tool wear amount.
0: The data is set in the geometry offset amount, and the wear offset amount is set to "0."

SECTION 30 FS-9 TAPE CONVERSION FUNCTION

30-1. Outline

This manual explains the function to convert NC programs made for the FANUC SYSTEM 9
(referred to as "FS-9" hereafter) to NC programs readable by the OSP.

Using this function in the program operation mode, the following G and M codes for the FS-9 can be
converted to the program format for the OSP.

Cutter radius compensation                           G40, G41, G42
Three-dimensional tool offset                        G40, G41, G42
Tool length offset                                   G43, G44, G49
Local coordinate system set                          G52
Machine coordinate system select                     G53
Work coordinate system select                        G54 to G59
Mirror image                                         G62
Macro call                                           G65
Macro modal call                                     G66
Cancel of macro modal call                           G67
Fixed cycle                                          G73 to G87, G89
Work coordinate system change                        G92
Fixed cycle - return to initial level/R-point level  G98, G99
Reference point return                               G27 to G30
Subprogram call                                      M98
End of subprogram                                    M99
                                                                                  6124-E P-321
                           SECTION 30 FS-9 TAPE CONVERSION FUNCTION

30-2. Operating Procedure

30-2-1.Selecting Tape Conversion Function

            Whether or not the tape conversion function is made effective in program operation is set at the NC
            optional parameter (bit) No. 48, bit 7.

           Parameter Setting Procedure

            Procedure :

           1 Select the parameter setting mode and displays the NC optional parameter (long word, word,

                   bit) setting screen.

           2 Select the machine operation method with the NC optional parameter (bit) No. 48, bit 7.

                   NC optional parameter (bit) No. 48, bit 7

Check                      Operation Method

       Tape conversion function effective (operation in tape conversion mode)

       Tape convert function ineffective (normal operation by OSP)

30-2-2.Operation in the Tape Conversion Mode

            Start program operation after setting "1" at the NC optional parameter (bit) No. 48, bit 7.
            A series of operations from program selection to machine start is the same as that for the OSP.
            Refer to the Operation Manual.
                                                                                          6124-E P-322
                                   SECTION 30 FS-9 TAPE CONVERSION FUNCTION

30-3. Details of Conversion

Conversion is performed in program operation in units of one block. The G and M codes after
conversion are displayed on the block data display screen.

unchanged.

30-3-2.Details of G and M Code Conversion

          Cutter Radius Compensation (G40, G41, G42)

When any of the I, J, and K commands is missing in the G41 or G42 block, the system will recognize
it as the cutter radius compensation command. And G40 designated after G41 or G42 will be
recognized as the cutter radius compensation cancel command.

< Conversion >
G40, G41, G42
G40, G41, and G42 are converted to G40, G41, and G42 respectively.

Note

  Cutter radius compensation can not be canceled by D00.
  For start-up and cancel motion, type B of the FS-9 is used.

Three-dimensional Tool Offset (G40, G41, G42)

When all of the I, J, and K commands are present in the G41 or G42 block, the system will recognize
it as the three-dimensional tool offset command. And G40 designated after G41 or G42 will be
recognized as the three-dimensional tool offset cancel command.

< Conversion >

G40  G43 M232
G41  G44 M232

                       (The three-dimensional offset value is used as it is.)
G42  G44 M233

                       (The three-dimensional offset value is used with the sign, + or -, preceding it
                       inverted.)

Note

  Cutter radius compensation can not be canceled by D00.
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-324
                                                           SECTION 30 FS-9 TAPE CONVERSION FUNCTION

Tool Length Offset (G43, G44, G49)

Tool length offset is executed for the axis designated in the G43 or G44 block.

< Conversion >

 For the X-axis

             G43 Xx Hh  G54 Xx Hh M230
                                            (The tool offset value is used as it is.)

             G44 Xx Hh  G54 Xx Hh M231
                                            (The tool offset value is used with the sign, + or -e, preceding it
                                            inverted.)

 For the Y-axis

             G43 Yy Hh  G55 Yy Hh M230
             G44 Yy Hh  G55 Yy Hh M231

 For the Z-axis

           G43 Zz Hh   G56 Zz Hh M230
           G44 Zz Hh   G56 Zz Hh M231

The tool length offset command for the 4th-, 5th-, or 6th-axis can be converted in the same
manner, to G57, G58, or G59 in this order.

 Canceling tool offset compensation

           G49         G53 M230

Note

  When no axis command is present in the G43 or G44 block, tool length offset is executed for

    the Z-axis.
    G43 Hh  G56 Hh M230
    G44 Hh  G56 Hh M231

  Tool length offset can not be canceled by H00.

Local Coordinate System Setting (G52)

The local coordinate system canceling command is converted to G10, and the local coordinate
system set command is converted to G11. Conversion to G10 is executed only when "0" is set for all
of the X, Y, and Z axes in the G52 block.

< Conversion >

G52 X0 Y0 Z0  G10

G52 Xx Yy Zz  G11 Xx Yy Zz

G52 X0 Y0         G11 X0 Y0
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-325
                                                           SECTION 30 FS-9 TAPE CONVERSION FUNCTION

Machine Coordinate System Select (G53)

The machine coordinate system (H00) is selected.

< Conversion >

G53              G16 H00

                (machine coordinate system)

Work Coordinate System Select (G54 to G59)

Coordinate systems specific to the machine should be preset.
Then, a required coordinate system can be selected.

< Conversion >

G54              G15 H01

                (work coordinate system 1)

G55              G15 H02

                (work coordinate system 2)

G56              G15 H03

                (work coordinate system 3)

G57              G15 H04

                (work coordinate system 4)

G58              G15 H05

                (work coordinate system 5)

G59              G15 H06

                (work coordinate system 6)

                                                                             6124-E P-327
                      SECTION 30 FS-9 TAPE CONVERSION FUNCTION

Macro Call G65

< Conversion >         CALL Op Q1 <argument>
G65 Pp L1 <argument>

Note

  Designate a program name for address P. (The program name must be a numeral within four

    digits.)

  Any value within 1 and 9999 can be designated as the number of repetitions. When no value is

    set, the system automatically sets 1.

  When L or the command following L is omitted, the address characters following P are all

    recognized as an argument.

  To reference the argument on the macro side, the address character preceded by "P" is used.

    (Same as the variable setting in the G code macro function.)

  An alarm occurs unless G65 is designated following the sequence number.

  The following error results when the program is selected in other than the tape converting

    operation mode.
    -5228 Tape convert file load
    When G65 is designated in other than the tape converting operation mode, the following alarm
    will occur.
    -Alarm B 2210 Program bad direct: G-code 41

  An axis move command can not be designated in the G65 block.

  The nesting level up to 8 is allowed for sub routine (including M98, G65, CALL, etc.).

  Argument assigned to G65

    a) Selection of an argument, type I or type II, is made on-line according to the parameter
        setting.

    b) For the conversion of the argument to the local variable, refer to Tables 1 and 2.

    c) The number of address characters which designate an argument in one block must be within
        32. If this number is exceeded, the following alarm will occur.
        Alarm B 2216 Program factor over

    d) When more than one argument is designated by the same address character (excluding the
        duplicated designation of "I", "J", or "K" of the argument type II ), the argument designated by
        the last address character becomes effective.

    e) In the argument type I, address characters G, N, O, and P can not be used as an argument.
        G:Recognized not as an argument but as an general G code.
        The following alarm occurs when a G code other than those designating OSP operation or
        tape converting operation is designated.
        Alarm B 2225 Unusable:G-code
        N:The following alarm occurs during the execution of conversion.
        Alarm B 2230 Unusable:direct of left side 3601
        O:The following alarm occurs during the execution of conversion.
        Alarm B 2230 Unusable:direct of left side 3801
        P:The following alarm occurs during the execution of conversion.
        Alarm B 2230 Unusable:direct of left side 3801

Note

In the macro call by the FS-9 tape convert function, the local variables transferred from arguments
are added to the standard local variables. Depending on the argument type of the FS-9 tape
convert function, the following arguments transfer the values to the local variables PC01 to
PC33.The argument type III is the same with the argument type I.

Macro Modal Call G66

< Conversion >
G66 Pp L1 <argument>  MODIN Op Q1 <argument>

Note

  Any value within the range of 1 and 9999 can be designated as the number of repetitions. The

    default value is 1.

  Only the argument type I can be used. (A to Z)
  When L or the command following L is omitted, the address characters following P are all

    recognized as an argument.

  To reference the argument on the macro side, the address character preceded by "P" is used.

Cancel of macro modal call (G67)

< Conversion >         MODOUT
G67

Fixed Cycle G73 to G87, G89

< Conversion >

   The following G codes are converted to the same G codes.

      G73, G76, G80, G81, G82, G83, G85, G86, G87, G89

   A Q command having the same value as the P command is generated when a P command is

      present in the block.
      G74, G84

Example:
G74 X20 Y50 Z0 R10 P15 F20

Note

  G88 (boring command) can not be designated.

    The number of repetitions (address L) can not be designated.

  Only the fixed cycle II (back boring) is allowed for the G87 command.
  The shift amount for G76 (fine boring) and G87 (back boring) is set at the NC optional

    parameter (long word) No. 11.

Work Coordinate System Change G92

< Conversion >
G92 is converted to G92.

Note

  Only the work coordinate system which is selected when G92 is designated is changed. (Other

    work coordinate systems are not changed.)

  The work coordinate system is changed according to the selected dimensioning mode, absolute

    or incremental.

(Ex.) In the case of incremental command (G91)
        G90G0X-100
        G91
        G92X25
        The incremental value 25 is added to the actual position (-100) so that the work
        coordinate changes to a value calculated by -100+25=-75.

        G90G0X-100
        G91
        G92X0
        The actual position is changed to the incremental position measured from the actual
        position (-100) so that it becomes a value calculated by -100+0=-100.
        Since this value is the same with the actual position, no work coordinate is changed.

Fixed Cycle - Return to Initial Level/R-point Level G98, G99

< Conversion >
G98 is converted to M53. (Initial level return is executed.)
G99 is converted to M54. (R point level return is executed.)

Note

  The initial level refers to the absolute position of the infeed axis in the fixed cycle mode.

                                                           SECTION 30 FS-9 TAPE CONVERSION FUNCTION

Reference Point Return G27 to G30

The reference point refers to a point fixed on the machine and is the home position for the OSP
There is a total of four sets of reference points, and the 1st reference point is selected when no
designation is made.
The home position numbers corresponding to the reference points are set at the NC optional
parameters (word) No. 29 to 32 an listed below.

Parameter No.  Contents                                      Initial Setting    Setting
                                                                                Range
29             Home position number for 1st reference point          1
                                                                     2            1-8
30             Home position number for 2nd reference point          3
                                                                     4
31             Home position number for 3rd reference point

32             Home position number for 4th reference point

Note

  The axis movement sequence for positioning from the intermediate to the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, or 4th

    reference point is determined according to the system parameter setting for the home position
    No. 1 to No. 8.

  An alarm of level B will occur when G27, G28, G29 or G30 is designated in any of the following

    modes:
    In the G-code mode other than G00
    In the cutter radius compensation mode
    In the tool length offset mode

Return to Reference Point (G27)

< Conversion >
G27 is converted to G27.
Machine operation varies depending on setting at the following NC optional parameter (bit).

NC optional parameter (bit) No. 47, bit 7

Check                                          Description

       On receiving G27 command, the NC checks if all the axes are in their
       reference points. If not, the NC generates an alarm B before axis
       movement. When in the reference points, the NC moves the specified
       axes at the rapid traverse.

       On receiving G27 command, the NC checks if only the specified axis is
       in its reference point. If not, the NC generates an alarm B before axis
       movement. When in the reference point, the NC moves the specified
       axis at the rapid traverse.
