                                                           SECTION 30 FS-9 TAPE CONVERSION FUNCTION

Automatic Return to Reference Point (G28)

< Conversion >
G28 is converted to G28.
Machine operation varies depending on setting at the following NC optional parameter (bit).

NC optional parameter (bit) No. 47, bit 7

Check                                          Description

       All the specified axes are fed to the end point (which becomes an
       intermediate point) at the rapid traverse. Then, they move from the
       intermediate point to the reference point at the rapid traverse.
       The coordinate values of the specified axes are stored in the memory.
       For the other axes, the coordinate values specified in the previous G28
       block remain effective.

       The axis specified with G28 is fed to the end point (which becomes an
       intermediate point) at the rapid traverse. Then, it moves from the
       intermediate point to the reference point at the rapid traverse.
       The coordinate value of the specified axis is stored in the memory. For
       the other axes, the coordinate values specified in the previous G28 block
       remain effective.

NC optional parameter (bit) No. 47, bit 4

Check                                          Description

       When G91 is selected and the middle point is specified, the command
       value specified after G28 is an increment from the position before G28.
       When the middle point is specified, the command value is an increment
       from the middle point of G28 command.

       In G91 mode, the command value following G28 should be an increment
       from the reference point.

Automatic Return from Reference Point (G29)

< Conversion >
G29 is converted to G29.
Machine operation varies depending on setting at the following NC optional parameter (bit).

NC optional parameter (bit) No. 47, bit 7

Check                                          Description

       All the axes are positioned to the intermediate point stored by the
       previous G28 command at the rapid traverse and then positioned to the
       point specified by G29 at the rapid traverse.
       The axes whose intermediate points are not stored move from the
       reference point to the point specified by the command values.

       The axis specified with G29 is positioned to the intermediate point stored

       reference point to the point specified by the command values.

Check                                          Description

       When G91 is selected and the middle point is not specified, the
       command value specified with G29 is an increment from the last position
       of the program command.
       With the middle point is specified, the command value specified with G29
       is an increment from the middle point.

       In G91 mode, the command value specified with G29 is an increment
       from the middle point (reference point without a middle point command).

Note

1) The axis does not stop at the intermediate point even in single block mode.
2) If the work coordinate zero or tool length offset amount is changed by a command designated

    between G28/G30 and G29, the intermediate point deviates in the machine coordinate system.

G30 is converted to G30. Machine operation varies depending on setting at the following NC

NC optional parameter (bit) No. 47, bit 7

Check                                          Description

       All the specified axes are positioned to the end point (which becomes an
       intermediate point) at the rapid traverse and then positioned from the
       intermediate point to the 2nd, 3rd, or 4th reference point at the rapid
       traverse. The axes move in the same manner as in the case of G28
       except that they return not only to the reference point but also to the 2nd
       3rd or 4th reference point.

       The intermediate point specified by G30 is also stored. Therefore, if G29
       is specified following G30, the axes are positioned to the point specified
       by G29 via the intermediate point specified by G30.

       The effective P command values are 2, 3, and 4, and the other command
       values will cause an alarm. The default P command value is 2.

       The axis specified by G30 is positioned to the end point (which becomes
       an intermediate point) at the rapid traverse and then positioned from the
       intermediate point to the 2nd, 3rd, or 4th reference point at the rapid
       traverse. The axis moves in the same manner as in the case of G28
       except that it returns not only to the reference point but also to the 2nd
       3rd or 4th reference point.

       The intermediate point specified by G30 is also stored. Therefore, if G29
       is specified following G30, the axis is positioned to the point specified by
       G29 via the intermediate point specified by G30.

       The effective P command values are 2, 3, and 4, and the other command
       values will cause an alarm. The default P command value is 2.

       from the reference point.

Subprogram Call (M98)

Note

  Designate a program name for address P. (The program name must be a numeral within four

    digits)

  Any value within 1 and 9999 can be designated as the number of repetitions.

    When no value is set, the system automatically sets 1.

  An alarm occurs unless M98 is designated following the sequence number.

  The following error results when the program is selected in other than the tape converting

    operation mode.
        5288 Tape convert file load

  An axis move command can not be designated in the M98 block.

  The nesting level up to 8 is allowed for sub routine (including M98, M65, CALL, etc.).

  An argument can not be designated.

  Other commands such as an axis movement command can not be designated in the M98

    block.

Note

  In the M99 block, no other commands, for example an axis movement command or a P or L

    command of the FS-9, can be designated.

    occurs since the system recognizes M99 as a normal M code.
        5220 Program end code 2

Slope Coordinate Command (Optional G68/G69)

The slope coordinates command during FS-9 tape conversion are G68 (slope coordinate mode ON)
and G69 (slope coordinate mode OFF).

I/J Command for Cutter Radius Compensation (Optional)

The contents of I/J command for cutter radius compensation (G41/G42) differ between OSP and
FS-9.
While FS-9 tape convert expanded function is active, the compensation motion conforms to the
motion by FS-9.

30-5. Remarks for Tape Convert

            Using the tape conversion function, only the G and M codes of the FS-9 listed in "1. Outline" can be
            converted for the OSP. To convert other G and M codes which are not interchangeable between the
            FS-9 and the OSP, it is necessary to rewrite them in the OSP program format.
            For details of the OSP program format, refer to the Programming Manual.
            Although a maker's subprogram can be called during FS-9 operation, G52 (local coordinate system
            setting) will be canceled.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-339
                                                                       SECTION 31 TOOL GROOVE PROCESS FUNCTION

SECTION 31 TOOL GROOVE PROCESS FUNCTION

31-1. Outline

            In the tool groove process, the machine cuts the workpiece by changing the tool direction according
            to the moving direction of plane. For normal machining center, spindle orientation command (M19),
            CW/CCW, and stop commands (M03, 04, 05, and S) are used for controlling the spindle rotation.
            The conventional spindle control method by these commands, however, is no longer suitable for the
            tool groove process. This function provides a new spindle control method (C-axis mode) so that the
            machine can follow the change of X-Y plane moving direction at real time.

31-2. Program (Machining on X-Y Plane)

      <Flow of tool groove process>         (Stop the spindle.)
(1) Select the tool groove process mode.    (Establish the C-axis mode.)
                                            (At the time of cutting feed, issue the M code for
(2) Positioning with G00                    turning off the spindle rotating condition.)
(3) Cutting with G01, 02, 03.               (Positioning in initial direction)
(4) Turn off the tool groove process mode.
                                            (Adjust the spindle direction in positioning if the
                                            spindle direction is specified for the following
                                            cutting blocks.)

                                            (Control the spindle direction according to the X-Y
                                            plane moving direction.)

                                            (Reactivate the M code for setting the spindle
                                            rotating condition during cutting feed)
                                            (Select the spindle mode.)

<Spindle direction change followed by cutting direction change during the tool groove process>
Under the control for changing the spindle direction in response to the X-Y plane direction at real
time, the NC may fail to follow a radical moving direction change. With this function, the spindle can
follow the cutting direction change at the max rotating speed of C-axis. The NC rotates the spindle
by calculating the nearest path.

<G code>               Tool groove process ON
G142 [E???.???] [H??]  Tool groove process OFF
G141

 Set the initial spindle direction at E.

 - At H, set the spindle angle offset number in the tool groove process.

       - The offset numbers can be set at H0 to H10.

       - The H command number is saved in the NC and reused when G142 is specified next.
            This allows you to omit designation of the H command thereafter. To reset the H
            command value, reset the NC or restart the machine.

       - For the method of setting the offset data, refer to "31-5. Setting Spindle Angle Offset
            Data."

<Initial positioning command format during movement with G00>
G00X___Y___Z___E(Specify the direction with E.)
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-340
                                                                       SECTION 31 TOOL GROOVE PROCESS FUNCTION

31-3. Machining on Arbitrary Plane

            In tool groove process (G142), the cutter moving direction and the spindle rotating direction are
            determined from the X-, Y-axis travel distances on the X-Y plane of the machine coordinate system.
            The positive X-axis direction is regarded as the spindle 0° position.
            Even if the machining plane is changed with a plane change command (G17, G18, G19), the spindle
            direction is determined by the above method regardless of the designated plane.

            In the tool groove process (G288, G289) on the arbitrary plane, the workpiece can be cut by
            changing the tool direction according to the moving direction on Y-Z, Z-X, or even on an arbitrary
            plane as well as the X-Y plane. (The slope machining function is optional.)
            Unlike the tool groove process of which plane is limited to X-Y plane, when G18 (Z-X plane) or G19
            (Y-Z plane) is specified for the tool groove process on the arbitrary plane, the spindle moving
            direction can be negative (as in the case of G17) or positive.
            The figures below show the case of G19 (Y-Z plane).

                                                                                                             The spindle rotates in
                                                                                                             reverse direction.

                                                                   CW (M3) direction

                                                                                                                                G02 command

                                                When the spindle is moving in positive X-axis direction
                                                                                                               The spindle rotates in
                                                                                                               forward direction.

                                                                   CW (M3) direction

                                                                                                                               G02 command

                                                When the spindle direction is negative X-axis direction

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        ME32128R0303400030001

            When CW one circular movement is specified with the command G19G2YZJK, the spindle
            rotating direction changes according to the spindle moving direction.
            In the tool groove process function, the cutter direction is specified with the program command (G
            code).
                                                                                         6124-E P-341
                                SECTION 31 TOOL GROOVE PROCESS FUNCTION

The following are the directions in which the spindle angle becomes 0° (E0) on a plane other than a
slope.

    Positive X-axis direction on G17 plane
    Positive Z-axis direction on G18 plane
    Positive Y-axis direction on G19 plane

The angle 0° direction on a slope:
    Positive X-axis direction in the work coordinate system for a slope on G17 plane
    Positive Z-axis direction in the work coordinate system for a slope on G18 plane
    Positive Y-axis direction in the work coordinate system for a slope on G19 plane

The operator should adjust the cutter direction as shown above using the angle offset of the tool
groove spindle or the tool groove spindle angle origin.

On the G17 plane, E0 shows the           On a slope, E0 shows the
positive X-axis direction.               positive X'-axis direction (Z+).

                                G69Q-90

On the G19 plane, E0 shows the           On a slope, E0 shows the
positive Y-axis direction.               positive Y'-axis direction (X+).

31-3-1.Program

<Flow of tool groove process>

(1) Turn on the tool groove process mode. (Stop the spindle.)
                                                            (Select the C-axis mode)
                                                            (Issue the M code for deactivating the spindle
                                                            rotating conditions during cutting feed.)
                                                            (Locate the spindle in initial positions.)

(2) Perform positioning with G00.         (If the spindle direction for the subsequent cutting
                                          block is specified in positioning, match the actual
                                          spindle direction with the specified direction.)

(3) Perform cutting with G01, 02, or 03.  (Control the spindle direction according to the
                                          traveling direction on the arbitrary plane.)

(4) Turn off the tool groove process mode. (Issue the M code for activating the spindle rotating
                                                            conditions during cutting feed.)
                                                            (Select the spindle mode.)

<Changing spindle direction in response to the change of cutting direction during tool groove
process>
Under the control for changing the spindle direction at real time according to the axis movements on
an arbitrary plane, there may be a case where the NC fails to change the spindle direction in
response to the sharp direction change. This function follows the direction change with the max C-
axis speed. For a rotary movement, the NC calculates the minimum travel distance.

31-5. Setting Spindle Angle Offset Data

Set the spindle angle offset by regarding the angle formed when the tool is moved in the positive X-
axis direction for the tool groove process to be "0".
There are 11 data pieces. However, the offset value at No. 0 is added to No. 1 to 10 value displayed
on the spindle offset screen in the tool data setting mode. Refer to the following table.

Command   Offset data used

A G142H0  Spindle angle offset data at No. 0

B G142H1 to H10 Data at the spindle angle offset number that corresponds to the
                             sum of No. 0 data and command value

C G142    Offset data of A:
          if G142H? has not been specified after power supply or NC
          reset.
          Offset data of B:
          if G142H? has been specified after NC reset. (Use the offset
          data A for H0)

 The same offset number and data as shown above are used also when G288 or G289 is
specified.

Set the data in the procedure below.

Setting the spindle offset data in the tool data setting mode

Procedure :

  1 Select the tool data setting mode and display the SPINDLE OFFSET screen. Locate the cursor

      at the required data.

  2 Setting

      a. If offset data is known
            Press [F1] (SET) and enter the offset data through the keyboard.

      b. If the offset data has been set and the change amount is known
            Press [F2] (ADD) and enter the change amount through the keyboard.

  3 Press the WRITE key.

      Data is set at the cursor position on the displayed screen.
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-346
                                                          SECTION 31 TOOL GROOVE PROCESS FUNCTION

Setting data with the optional parameter long word No. 29

The set value corresponds to the No. 0 value displayed on the spindle offset screen in tool data
setting mode.

Procedure :

  1 Select the parameter setting mode, and display the NC optional parameter screen. Then,

      locate the cursor at the long word No. 29.

  2 Setting

      a. If the offset data is known
            Press [F1] (SET) and enter offset data through the keyboard.

      b. If the offset data has been set and the change amount is known
            Press [F2] (ADD) and enter the change amount through the keyboard.

  3 Press the WRITE key. Set this value in units of 1/1000°.

      When programming this offset value, specify "H0" with G142/G288/G289.
      Data setting range: 0 to 359999

[Supplement]

    The plane change command (G17, G18, G19) can be specified in the tool groove process

      mode.

    The slope command (G69, G68) can be specified in the tool groove process mode.

    If the tool groove process mode (G142, G288, G289) is turned ON or OFF during High-speed

      contouring mode, the High-speed contouring execution signal temporarily goes off.

    It is impossible to restart from a sequence in the tool groove process mode.

      If attempted, 2267 Alarm B "Sequence restart 4" occurs, stopping the program.

    When a built-in attachment is mounted to the spindle, the tool groove process ON command

      (G142, G288, G289) cannot be executed.
      If attempted, 1320 Alarm A "Cutting Tool Groove Process 2" occurs, stopping the program.

    Be aware that offset data can be set or changed (immediately valid) even when the program is

      running and the machine is operating.

    If the tool groove process ON command is issued in the tool groove process mode, 2556

      alarm B "Cutting Tool Groove Process 2" occurs, stopping the program.

    For gear-driven spindle, the tool groove process command can be executed only when the

      low-speed gear (servo data set number 0) is selected.
      If the command is executed without selecting the low-speed gear, 1320 Alarm A "CUTTING
      TOOL GROOVE PROCESS 1" occurs, stopping the program.

    In the tool grooving process, it is impossible to use the attachment that changes the spindle

      speed to a different tool rotation speed (such as a speed increasing attachment).
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-348
                                                          SECTION 31 TOOL GROOVE PROCESS FUNCTION

[Error]

5448 Spindle rotation within one turn

        The spindle has not turned exceeding one turn.
        [Character-string]
        None
        [Code]
        None

[Alarm]
Alarm A

1320 Cutting Tool Groove Process

        The tool groove process ON command (G142/G288/G289) was issued though the tool
        groove process conditions are not met.

                                    SECTION 32 FUNCTION FOR ENABLING/DISABLING NC RESET WITH M30

SECTION 32 FUNCTION FOR ENABLING/DISABLING NC
                   RESET WITH M30

32-1. Outline

            This function allows you to select whether the NC is reset or not by execution of M30 during
            schedule operation.
            By disabling NC reset, you can reuse the previously specified parameters (feedrate, tool length
            offset No., cutter radius compensation No., etc.), in each main program as common parameters.
            These parameters are usually cleared by NC reset executed when the main program is terminated
            during schedule operation.

32-2. Parameter Enabling or Disabling NC Reset by M30

32-2-1.Enable/Disable Switching Parameter

            NC optional parameter bit

                                                                   Setting      Setting

No. Bit  Description

66 4 Determines whether NC reset is effective or not               NC reset     NC reset
               when M30 is executed during schedule operation.     ineffective  effective

   To make this parameter setting effective, restart the machine.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-351
                                    SECTION 32 FUNCTION FOR ENABLING/DISABLING NC RESET WITH M30

32-2-2.Function Explanation

            When "1" is set at the NC optional parameter (bit) No.66, bit 4, the NC is not reset by M30 during
            schedule operation and the following information is used in the main program selected next.

               Spindle rotation command (M03, M04, M05)

               Spindle speed (S command)

               Feedrate (F command)

               Plane selection (G17, G18, G19)

               Programmable mirror image (G62)

               Tool length offset (G53 to G59) and tool length offset number (H command)

               Cutter radius compensation number (D) 1

               Local coordinate system (G11, G10)

               Fixed cycle mode 2

                        1 Since the cutter radius compensation commands (G40 to G42) are not transferred, it
                              is necessary to turn ON/OFF the compensation function in each main program.

                        2 In the fixed cycle mode, fixed cycle is performed in response to each axis movement.
                              So if the next program is selected and axis movement command is specified in the
                              fixed cycle mode before designation of the fixed cycle canceling G code (G80, G00 to
                              G03), the machine executes the fixed cycle. To prevent such unintended operation,
                              be sure to cancel the fixed cycle in the main program that has issued the fixed cycle.

                        3 Be sure to issue M02 to terminate the main program file finally selected with
                              PSELECT by the schedule program. Otherwise, the NC is not reset by M30, and
                              keeps executing the main program. So if you attempt to select the next program,
                              "5200 Main program executing" error will occur.

                                                                   SECTION 33 PERIODICAL MAINTENANCE FUNCTION

SECTION 33 PERIODICAL MAINTENANCE FUNCTION

33-1. Overview

            When a consumable part or lube oil used in the machine needs to be changed or supplied due to life
            expiration, the periodical maintenance function displays an alarm, notifying the operator that
            maintenance service is required for the part or oil.
            For this purpose, the periodical maintenance screen is added to the parameter setting screens. This
            maintenance screen is used to set the lifetimes of maintenance items such as consumable parts,
            and display the total elapsed time.
            If the elapsed time exceeds the life of the set item, this maintenance function generates an alarm.
            The lifetime is classified into 2 levels; notification time and warning time. If the total elapsed time
            exceeds the notification time, an alarm D occurs. If it exceeds the warning time, alarm C occurs.

33-2. Detail of Specifications

33-2-1.Maintenance Items

A maximum of 8 items can be set as maintenance items for controlling their periodical maintenance
time.

No.  Maintenance item         Count type                  Description
                          ALL TIME
1 AIR FILTER (ABSO)       AXIS MOV        Change the air filter when tits life has
                                          expired.
2 AXIS GREASE             ALL TIME
                                          Supply axis grease when its life has
3 ROTARY AXIS             LIFE COUNT      expired.

4 TURNING SPINDLE         ALL TIME        Supply grease by following the C-axis
                          ATC COUNT       grease supply guide screen in the
5 THRU-PIN (TIME)         COOLING         operation screen mode when the oil
6 THRU-PIN (COUNT)                        supply interval for the rotary axis
7 EXHAUST CLEANER                         exceeds the life hours.
8 (Spare)
                                          Supply grease by following the C-axis
                                          grease supply guide screen in the
                                          operation screen mode when the oil
                                          supply interval for the turning spindle
                                          exceeds the life hours.

                                          Change the spindle thru-pin when its
                                          life (hours) has expired.

                                          Change the spindle thru-pin when its
                                          life (count) has expired.

                                          Change the exhaust cleaner of the
                                          spindle cooling unit when its life has
                                          expired.

The method of adding elapsed time (count type) is determined by the maintenance item. This
means the count type cannot be solely changed.

      day of maintenance.
      When a count type other than all time is selected, the value displayed here is reset to "0" by
      input of the date of maintenance, and the maintenance function starts adding the elapsed time
      according to the selected count type. If the elapsed time exceeds the max value, the
      maintenance function stops counting.

suppressed.
If the date of maintenance is not set (initial value = 0) or falls on a date after tomorrow, the
maintenance function does not count the power-on elapsed time of the item of which count type
is ALL TIME and does not count the elapsed time of each count type.

Proper input example:
2004/01/01, 2004/1/1, 2004/1/01, 2004/01/1, etc.

Improper input example:
04/01/01, 2004/2/30, 2005/2/29 (2/29 in the year other than the leap year), etc.
                                                                                    6124-E P-356
                        SECTION 33 PERIODICAL MAINTENANCE FUNCTION

33-2-3.Life Management

The periodical maintenance function monitors the elapsed time, life notification time, and life
warning time for each maintenance item set on the periodical maintenance screen.
If the elapsed time is equal to or greater than the life notification time, an alarm D occurs ..... (1)
If the elapsed time is equal to or greater than the life warning time, an alarm C occurs. ..... (2)

However, if the date of maintenance is not set (initial setting = 0), the maintenance function does not
generate either of the above alarms.
If the life notification time is not set (initial setting = 0), the maintenance function does not generate
the life notification alarm.
If the life warning time is not set (initial value = 0), the function does not generate the life warning
alarm.

This alarm D (life notification) can be reset. However, there are cases where the machine is
continuously operated for 24 hours; therefore, the maintenance function is so designed to restart
alarm check in 4 hours after occurrence of alarm D.
Even while the alarm of one maintenance item is being reset, the alarm of another maintenance
item is displayed each time the life notification time is reached.

Life warning (alarm C) cannot be released by resetting. After completing maintenance of the
required item, set the parameters.

                                                                                                                                6124-E P-359
                                                                      SECTION 34 SERVO ERROR INDICATING ALARM D

SECTION 34 SERVO ERROR INDICATING ALARM D

34-1. Overview

            When a servo ready condition is not met, the NC ignores the command from the cycle start button or
            the sequence restart button, or an input signal for manual axis feed.
            The ignored input signal and the cause are displayed with an alarm.

34-2. Description

34-2-1.Start Buttons

            The NC ignores the command from the NC start button or the sequence restart button in any of the
            following cases:

            (1) The machine is in the state of emergency stop.

            (2) The NC is being reset.

            (3) The spindle is not ready to start.

            (4) At least one NC axis is not ready to operate.

            An alarm D occurs (refer to "34-2-3. Alarm") to indicate for which cause, among the above four
            cases, the NC ignored the start button or sequence restart button command.
            When the start button is released, the alarm D disappears.

34-2-2.Manual Operation Buttons

            The alarm D also occurs in the following cases:
            On the condition (1) or (2) described in the above item 34-2-1, the spindle rotation button was
            pressed in the manual operation mode or manual interruption mode although the spindle was not
            read to operate. Or, the axis feed button was pressed when the axis selected for manual feed was
            not ready to operate. However, no alarm D occurs during axis feed by the pulse handle.
            When the manual operation button is released, the previously displayed alarm D disappears.
                                                                       6124-E P-360
              SECTION 34 SERVO ERROR INDICATING ALARM D

34-2-3.Alarm

Alarm D

4340 Motor not ready to start

        An axis movement command or spindle rotation command was specified although the motor
        was not ready to start (it takes several seconds until the motor becomes ready to start after
        the power is supplied or an alarm A is reset).
        [Index]
        Axis name (The spindle is excluded, but the attachment spindle is indicated as SB.)
        [Character-string]
        None
        [Code]
        XY
        X: Action

          1 ... Depression of NC start button
          2 ... Depression of sequence restart button
          3 ... Manual operation
          4 ... Spindle rotation
        Y: Cause
          1 ... The machine is in the state of emergency stop.
          2 ... The NC is being reset.
          3 ... The spindle is not ready to start.
          4 ... An NC-controlled axis is not ready to operate.
        [Probable Faulty Locations]
        Motor operation was attempted before the motor became ready to start.
        [Measures to Take]
        Wait until the motor is ready to operate. If the alarm state still continues, press the reset
        button.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-361
                                                     SECTION 35 WEIGHT PARAMETER AUTO SETTING FUNCTION

SECTION 35 WEIGHT PARAMETER AUTO SETTING
                   FUNCTION

35-1. OVERVIEW

            The workpiece weight auto setting function is used to identify the workpiece weight from the torque
            applied during operation with the workpiece loaded, select the optimum combination (referred to as
            servo combination) from the prepared servo data to match the identified workpiece weight, and then
            determine the servo constant for the corresponding axis.
            The following explanation uses the X-axis as an example.

35-2. Command Format

            The command described below is used to identify the workpiece weight and change the servo
            constant for the axis for which the workpiece weight auto setting function is applied.
            In order to identify the workpiece weight, the X-axis makes two return journeys of the distance
            specified by X from its current position (identification operation).
            Execute a command to move the Y- and Z-axes beforehand to prevent any interference between the
            workpiece and machine when the X-axis moves.

            Command format: [G448 (X) (F#) (P%) ]
             X : Specifies the name of the axis being identified and the amount that the axis is to be moved

                      when performing identification.
                      In this example, the command is used to move the "X-axis" by the specified movement
                      amount.
                      The X-axis makes two return journeys of X from the position at which the G448 command
                      is executed.
                      The axis moves in the negative direction if X < 0.
                      Set to approximately 500 mm (19.69 in.) as a guideline.
                       It may not be possible to accurately identify the workpiece weight due to identification

                           restrictions, and in such cases, the movement amount should be set to more than 200
                           mm (7.87 in.).
             F : Specifies the feed rate when performing identification.
                      This setting is required for the X-axis.
                      Set to approximately half the rapid traverse rate as a guideline.
                       It may not be possible to accurately identify the workpiece weight due to identification
                           restrictions, and in such cases, the feed rate should be set to more than 5,000 mm/min
                           (196.85 ipm).
             P : No setting ... Performs identification.
                       1 ... Sets the last identification result again. (There is no machine movement.)

              The cutting feed override switch is disabled while executing this function.
              The cutting feed override switch is fixed at 100%, and the machine will still move if set to 0%.
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-362
                                        SECTION 35 WEIGHT PARAMETER AUTO SETTING FUNCTION

(1) The P command is given priority if both the X and P commands are executed simultaneously.

(2) The command is terminated without any movement if there is neither an X command nor P
      command.

(3) The command is terminated without any movement if executed while the machine is locked.

(4) All commands other than X, P and F are ignored.

(5) The Super-NURBS and Hi-CUT Pro functions are automatically set to "Disable" while this
      command is being executed.

X-axis position during when G448                               +X
                                        2 return journeys

                                        ME32128R0303800020001

Auto block generation

G448 X F# G94                           (No axis movement, auto G94 generation)
G01 X G09 M130 M136                     (Axis movement)
X G09                                   (Axis movement)
X G09                                   (Axis movement)
X G09                                   (Axis movement)
G/M codes prior to G448 are restarted.  (No axis movement, G94/G00/M130/M136 restart)

In addition, speed clamping at the upper limit feed rate set at the PARAMETERS screen is not
performed.
(Clamping is performed at the upper limit feed rate in the servo data.)
                                                                                           6124-E P-363
                 SECTION 35 WEIGHT PARAMETER AUTO SETTING FUNCTION

35-3. Operation

The flow for the process used to identify the workpiece weight at the X-axis and change the servo
constant is as follows.

(1) Move the Y- and Z-axes to a position at which it is okay to perform identification for the X-axis.

(2) Move the X-axis to the identification start position.

(3) Specify G448 X F# and execute the command.

(4) The X-axis moves automatically from its current position by X in the positive direction at F#.

(5) The X-axis moves automatically by X in the negative direction at F#.

(6) The X-axis moves automatically from its current position by X in the positive direction at F#.

(7) The X-axis moves automatically by X in the negative direction at F#.

(8) The servo combination is selected (servo constant is changed) based on the identified
      workpiece weight.

Subsequent axis movements are made based on the parameter (workpiece weight) determined by
identification.

With steps (4) to (7), the cutting feed override is ignored and operates at 100%.

The identified workpiece weight can be checked at "WORK WEIGHT SET" on the PARAMETER
screen.
While using the identified data, the identified workpiece weight (values are rounded off if less than
the setting unit) displays in the "WORK WEIGHT SET" item, "Identified data using" displays to the
right, and the maximum workpiece weight with the selected servo combination displays below in
"MAX WORK WEIGHT".
"Identified data using" does not display if the identified data is not used.
The setting units can be displayed in either [ton] or [100 kg] units.

                 ME32128R0303800030001
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-364
                                                     SECTION 35 WEIGHT PARAMETER AUTO SETTING FUNCTION

35-3-1.Sequence Restart Operation

            With the G448 identification command, multiple blocks are automatically generated based on the
            "2Command Format".
            Sequence restart is possible by specifying a block counter for each block, however, identification is
            not performed.

            In addition, a mid-block restart with the upgraded sequence restart function (option) cannot be
            applied to the G448 block.
            "2267 Alarm B "Sequence restart 7" occurs.

            Also ensure that the mid-block restart point is not applied to the G448 block.
            The operation will stop at the restart point during identification, and it will not be possible to
            accurately measure the torque.

35-3-2.Reset Operation

            The selected servo combination is set to the default value if a reset is performed during
            identification.
            If a G448P1 command is then executed, the previous identified data is selected.
            The default value will be the servo combination selected when "0" is set for the workpiece weight.

35-3-3.Operation Following Alarm

            Identification is aborted if an alarm occurs during identification.
            The selected servo combination and servo parameters at this point will be the values during
            identification.
            The default servo combination value is selected after resetting.
            The default value will be the servo combination selected when "0" is set for the workpiece weight.

35-3-4.Operation when Turning Power ON

            If the servo combination selected when the power was last turned OFF has been selected with the
            workpiece weight corresponding axis identification function, the servo combination and servo
            parameters at that time are selected when the power is next turned ON.

            If a reset was being performed during identification when the power was last turned OFF, the default
            servo combination is selected.
            The default value will be the servo combination selected when "0" is set for the workpiece weight.

            In addition, if the machine is restarted when an alarm has occurred (reset not performed) during
            identification, the servo combination prior to the alarm is selected and the machine is started up.
            (However, the servo constant will not be the parameter obtained by identification, but the default
            value for the corresponding servo combination.)

            If the servo combination is selected with the workpiece weight selection function, this servo
            combination is selected and the machine is started up.
                                                                                                                                6124-E P-365
                                                     SECTION 35 WEIGHT PARAMETER AUTO SETTING FUNCTION

35-4. Relationship with Workpiece Weight Setting Function

            With the workpiece weight setting function, after entering the workpiece weight at the PARAMETER
            screen "WORK WEIGHT SET" item, a suitable servo combination for the entered workpiece weight
            is selected by performing a reset.

            If the workpiece weight setting function workpiece weight is changed, the servo combination and
            servo constant selected with the workpiece weight corresponding axis identification function is
            ignored after performing a reset, and the servo combination and servo constant selected with the
            workpiece weight setting function are selected.

            The previous identified servo combination and servo constant are also selected by executing the
            G448P1 command after selecting the servo combination with the workpiece weight setting function.

35-5. G Code Conversion

            Command G429 is converted to G448 with bit 5 of the NC optional parameter (bit) No.90.

            However, if equipped with the rotation axis parameter auto setting function (option), the following
            parameter is set to , and Alarm B appears when G429 is specified.

NO Bit

90 5 Converts command G429 to G448.  Doesn't convert command G429 to G448.

 The G429 command is the infinity-turn axis parameter auto setting command.
                                                                                        6124-E P-366
              SECTION 35 WEIGHT PARAMETER AUTO SETTING FUNCTION

35-6. Alarms

35-6-1.Alarm A

           1335 Servo data change error

                     The servo data corresponding to the set weight at the workpiece weight selection function
                     (servo file selection function) is incorrect.

                     [Index]
                     Axis

                     [Character-string]
                     None

                     [Code]
                     XXYYZZZZZ
                     XX: Old servo combination no. (1~)
                     YY: New servo combination no. (1~)
                     ZZZZ: Cause
                     1000: There was no response from MCS even after 0.5 sec. elapsed since requesting servo
                     combination switching.

                              (The selected combination no. did not match the new servo combination.)
                     2000: There was no new servo combination in the slave axis servo data for tandem control.
                     2001: There was no new servo combination in the slave axis servo data for the X slave 3-axis
                     specifications.
                     2002: There was no new servo combination in the slave axis servo data for synchronous axis
                     control.
                     3001: The G448 process to return the servo parameter change value to the servo file value
                     was not completed even after 1 second.
                     3002: Servo combination switching was not completed even after 1 second following a reset
                     performed while executing G448.

                     [Probable Faulty Locations]
                     Servo data file
                     NC software

                     [Measures to Take]
                     Set the weight prior to the change at the parameter setting mode workpiece weight setting
                     screen, and then reset.

                     [Related specifications]
                     Weight parameter auto setting function

                SECTION 35 WEIGHT PARAMETER AUTO SETTING FUNCTION

35-6-2.Alarm B

2244 Data word: 'F'

        The F command is incorrect.

        [Index]
        None

        [Character-string]
        None

        [Code]
        3: There was no F command in the G448 command block. Alternatively, it was an F0
        command.

        [Measures to Take]
        Specify an F command in the G448 command block.

2247 Data word: 'P'

        The P command is incorrect.

        [Index]
        None

        [Character-string]
        None

        [Code]
        5: The weight parameter auto setting function P command is other than 1.
        8: The weight parameter auto setting function (G448) P command is P1 when identification is
        incomplete.

        [Probable Faulty Locations]
        G448 command block P command

        [Measures to Take]
        Execute a G448P1 command after performing identification with the weight parameter auto
        setting function.

2253 Data word: axis command

        The axis command value lies outside the range or is incorrect.
        8) An axis other than the workpiece weight corresponding axis was specified when executing
        the weight parameter auto setting command (G448).
        Alternatively, multiple axes were specified.

        [Index]
        None

        [Character-string]
        None

        [Code]
        - For weight parameter auto setting command (G448)
        A1: An axis other than the workpiece weight corresponding axis was specified.
        A2: Multiple axes were specified.

        [Probable Faulty Locations]
        Axis command value in NC program

        [Measures to Take]
        Specify one axis only for the workpiece weight corresponding axis when executing the weight
        parameter auto setting command.
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-368
                                        SECTION 35 WEIGHT PARAMETER AUTO SETTING FUNCTION

2263 Data word: G code

        G code command defect

        [Index]
        None

        [Character-string]
        None

        [Code]
        D0: G429 or G448 was specified in other than normal mode.
        D1: G429 or G448 was specified with the parameter change function specification bit
        disabled.
        D5: G429 or G448 was specified during tool nose point control.
        D6: G448 was specified with no workpiece weight corresponding axis.

        [Probable Faulty Locations]
        G code in NC program

        [Measures to Take]
        Correct the part of the NC program that displays the G code.

2267 Sequence restart

        The sequence restart operation is incorrect.

        [Index]
        None

        [Character-string]
        None

        [Code]
        7: The restart designation block inserted mid-block with the upgraded sequence restart
        function is a C-axis identification G429 command block or weight parameter auto setting
        G448 command block.

        [Probable Faulty Locations]
        Incorrect sequence restart operation
        - The RETURN and START buttons were mistaken.
        - The sequence to be restarted was mistaken.
        - The machine status does not permit a restart.
        Operation example:
        =RS N10 
        [START] [WRITE/EXECUTE]  The SEQUENCE RESTART button should be pressed.
        (Alarm code 2)

        [Measures to Take]
        Perform operation correctly after resetting.
        Perform after rotating the spindle at least once.

2641 mcs b-data transfer

        Unable to acquire data correctly with the designated data communication interface.

        [Index]
        None

        [Character-string]
        None

        [Code]
        XXYY
                                                                                                                   6124-E P-369
                                        SECTION 35 WEIGHT PARAMETER AUTO SETTING FUNCTION

        XX: Machine no.
        YY: Command code
        FFFFFFFF: If the "02: Data measurement status check" awaiting response code receipt
        status at the designated data communication interface continues for 3 seconds or longer.

2642 work inertia limit over

        The inertia calculation produced an abnormal calculation value.
        [Index]
        None
        [Character-string]
        None
        [Code]
        1: A limit over error occurred during ATFF calculation.
        2: A limit over error occurred during T1max calculation.
        3: A 0 division occurred during workpiece inertia calculation.
        4: A 0 division occurred during workpiece ATFF calculation.
        5: A limit over error occurred during Tpmax calculation.

35-6-3.Alarm D

4312 SPM Control cannot execute

        An attempt was made to perform high-speed contour control (SPM) when unable to.
        (High-speed contour control (SPM) = Hi-CUT Pro or Super-NURBS.)
        High-speed contour control is turned OFF internally, operation is performed, and this alarm is
        displayed during this time.
        [Index]
        None
        [Character-string]
        None
        [Code]
        500: The workpiece weight auto setting function is being executed.

4351 Work weight param. auto setting func. disenable

        Unable to use workpiece weight auto setting function.
        [Index]
        None
        [Character-string]
        None
        [Code]
        1: The MCS drive unit firmware is not supported.
        [Measures to Take]
        1: Replace the MCS drive unit or upgrade the firmware.
        [Related specifications]
        Weight parameter auto setting function
